For `gui.c`, the serial log confirms `gui_demo` was actually running (`Launching GUI demo...`) and two four-second press-and-holds **without moving the pointer** — the exact `steps == 0` condition — produced **0 PANIC, 0 Exception**. For `fb.c`, a real logout showed a clean login screen with no trace of the session behind it, and the second desktop came up **completely clean** — no Calculator, no Minesweeper, no second Terminal from the session that had just ended, which is the stale back buffer the fix clears. Harness kept at `scratchpad/guiverify.py`. **No new syscalls** (still 52). Regression: 4-CPU smpstress **0 integrity failures**, `threads` **8000/8000 OK**, `pstorm 30` **0 faulted / 0 MAPCORRUPT / 0 PANIC**, and no faults across every GUI run. |
| v5.9.0-LTS | **P0.1 CLOSED — the intermittent ring-3 `[fault]` was a preemptible CR3-switch→iretq window in the syscall-exit path. LTS release.** The one release-blocking bug in the default configuration, open across the whole SMP arc, is root-caused and fixed with a single `cli`. **The mechanism, found by REASONING after in-guest instrumentation was proven to perturb the race away:** the syscall-exit path (`isr_stubs.asm`) does `mov cr3,[user]` and then runs ~20 instructions (CPL 0, already on the USER CR3, committed to ring 3) before the `iretq`. If a timer preempts the task *in that window*, `irq_common` saves a frame whose CS reads 0x08 — genuinely ring 0 — so `irq_scheduler_tick` resumes it on the KERNEL CR3 under its rule "a ring-0 frame switches to user itself before its iretq". But this task has ALREADY switched, so its `push 0x1B; iretq` returns to ring 3 **on the kernel CR3**; the first user instruction fetch faults with CR2==RIP. **Why it matched every observed property:** the window is only preemptible with IF=1, and syscall ENTRY masks IF via SF_MASK — so a *simple* syscall runs cli'd end-to-end and can never be hit. Only BLOCKING syscalls (`futex_wait`, `do_waitpid`, poll, pipe read) `sti` to yield and *return with IF=1* (verified: both break paths of `futex_wait` leave IF set), which is why the fault was **always in `threads`** (the only futex-heavy workload), **load-dependent** (the timer must land in ~20 instructions), and **present in the default single-core config** (it is a preemption-window bug, NOT an SMP race). It also explains why every earlier scheduler-*decision* probe showed a "correct" `saw_cs=0x8`: the decision *is* correct for a ring-0 frame; the frame cannot reveal that the CR3 switch already happened. **The fix:** one `cli` immediately after `signal_dispatch`, before the user-CR3 reload — making the CR3-switch→iretq window non-preemptible; the `iretq` restores the user RFLAGS (IF=1) atomically as it crosses to ring 3. Covers every syscall regardless of what it left IF as; the entry side needs no equivalent (IF=0 from SF_MASK) and the irq/isr return paths run under interrupt gates (IF=0), so no other such window exists. **Verified in the linked binary** (`fa` cli at 0x100343, between `call signal_dispatch` and `mov %gs:0x10,%rax`) and by an **interleaved A/B under build load** alternating the two ISOs every boot: baseline-without-cli reproduced P0.1 (`pid 7 (threads) … cs=0x1b <ON-KERNEL-CR3>`) while the cli build ran **23 valid rounds, 0 faults** under identical contention. The proof is primarily *mechanistic* — the cli closes the only (CPL0 + user-CR3 + IF=1) window in the kernel — with the A/B as the corroborating control. Also this arc: the fault diagnostic was corrected (`isr_common` now passes the faulting CR3 to `isr_handler`; a prior `read_cr3()` there reported the kernel CR3 on *every* ring-3 fault, an artefact that had driven five dead hypotheses), and `sched_forget()` closes a `sched_cur` use-after-free at both `kfree(process)` sites. **No new syscalls** (still 52). Regression on the fixed build: 4-CPU smpstress **0 integrity failures**, `threads` **8000/8000 OK**, `pstorm 30` **0 faulted / 0 MAPCORRUPT / 0 PANIC**; 23+ clean loaded rounds. **Known limits (opt-in, OFF by default, documented):** `smpbalance on` process-spreading and thread-group un-pinning still wedge under fork/exec churn (P2.x) — the default configuration does not touch them, and the VFS/terminal remain unlocked against a user process deliberately placed on an AP. | 
| v5.9.1 | **"Nightfall" desktop — a central GUI palette, and the brand purple everywhere the placeholder green used to be.** First increment of the visual track that follows the LTS. **The refactor that made it possible: `kernel/theme.h`.** Colours were `fb_rgb(r,g,b)` literals scattered across `compositor.c` and every `*_win.c`, so changing the accent meant hunting call sites and the desktop could not be themed at all. Call sites now name a ROLE — `THEME_ACCENT`, `THEME_WINDOW_BG`, `THEME_BORDER`, `THEME_ROW_DIV`, `THEME_TASKBAR_*`, `THEME_TEXT`, `THEME_ON_ACCENT` — and the values live in one header. They are macros rather than a const struct because `fb_rgb()` is a runtime function, which also leaves the door open for a future Settings→accent by swapping them for a table lookup **without touching a single call site**. **The brand fix:** the accent is the brand purple `{130,90,210}` — the wallpaper's own default "Morado" — replacing the leftover placeholder green `fb_rgb(60,120,60)` on the Start-menu header band and its title text, and driving `taskbar_hl` so the **active Menu button and the focused window** now carry the accent. The window controls keep their traffic-light scheme (close red / minimise grey / maximise green): that green is a control affordance, not branding, and recolouring it would have broken a universal convention. **One accent swap was WRONG and the screenshot caught it.** The workspace indicator's active marker was moved to the accent like everything else — but it is drawn on the DESKTOP (`fh - TASKBAR_H - 10`), not the taskbar, and the wallpaper's base colour is user-selectable. Brand purple on the default purple wallpaper rendered the ACTIVE marker nearly invisible while the inactive greys stood out — exactly backwards, and strictly worse than the green it replaced. Zooming that 14x6 px region 8x is what exposed it; at full-screen scale it looked fine. Fixed with a dedicated `THEME_INDICATOR_ON`/`_OFF` pair (near-white vs dark grey) whose comment states the rule: anything painted over the wallpaper must be light-vs-dark, never accent-coloured, because it has to survive all eleven wallpaper choices. The About window's "NyxOS Desktop" heading also moved green → accent (it sits on a dark panel, so the accent reads fine there). **The desktop itself:** `draw_background()` rewritten as a Nightfall sky — a purple vertical gradient, a glowing moon built from concentric halos plus craters, and **110 deterministic LCG-seeded stars** that avoid the moon's area so the composition stays readable at any resolution. Two small helpers back it: `bg_isqrt()` (integer Newton square root — no FPU in the kernel) and `bg_fill_circle()` (horizontal-span fill). Determinism matters here: the star field is seeded, so every boot paints the identical sky rather than flickering to a new one on each repaint. **Branding swept beyond the GUI:** the README badges were still shields-green `00ff9d` while the brand is purple — all seven are now `825AD2`, so the repo, the login screen and the desktop finally agree on one colour. **No new syscalls** (still 52). No kernel-internals change: this increment is compositor + docs only, so the v5.9.0-LTS guarantees (P0.1 closed, 23 clean loaded rounds) carry over untouched. |
| v6.4.77 | **Userland (Arch/Void-quality north star): an `unexpand` command — the inverse of v6.4.72's `expand` — turns runs of spaces back into tabs.** New `cmd_unexpand` + an `unexpand_flush` helper in `core/kernel.c`, `unexpand [-a] [-t N] <file>`. It reproduces the GNU semantics: by DEFAULT only the LEADING blanks of each line are converted (the rest of the line is left exactly as-is), `-a` converts blank runs ANYWHERE on the line, and `-t N` sets the tab width (8 by default) and — like GNU — implies `-a`. A blank run is collapsed to the FEWEST tabs+spaces at N-column tab stops (`unexpand_flush` steps stop to stop, emitting a tab only when it saves >= 2 columns), and a single space is NEVER turned into a tab. Existing tabs in the input advance the column to the next stop; a newline resets the column and re-arms leading-conversion. Arg-based (no stdin into a kernel builtin) like expand/fold/tr/rev; wired as a builtin + a `man unexpand` page + placed in the help "Text" category next to `expand`. **Serial-verified** in QEMU (temp `[UNEXPTEST]` hook that writes space fixtures to /mnt and runs three modes, reverted — `grep TEMP-TEST`/`UNEXPTEST` empty in source AND the rebuilt binary; 0 real kernel faults, matched real `#PF`/`#GP`/`EXCEPTION`/`KERNEL PANIC` markers): with tabs revealed, `unexpand "        hello    world"` (8 spaces) → `\thello    world` (leading 8 → one tab, the mid-line spaces preserved in default mode); `unexpand -a` on the same → `\thello\t world` (the mid-line run also collapsed); `unexpand -t 4 "a    b"` → `a\t b` (a 4-space run to a 4-col stop = tab + one space, and `-t` implied `-a`). Build 0 warnings, `nyx-kernel.bin` 3225.1 KB, no new syscalls (57). A non-visual USERLAND step for variety (the prior two increments were the file-manager sort headers and the http-parser security self-test; the last plain userland was `expand` v6.4.72, whose inverse this is). Files: `kernel/core/kernel.c` (cmd_unexpand + unexpand_flush + command-table entry + man page + help "Text" category + forward decl), `kernel/core/kernel.h` (version). **NEXT: v6.4.78 — more userland (paste/comm/yes/nl), a personalization touch, docs (man coverage), or another north star; keep VARIETY (not userland/security/file-manager/P4 again).** |
| v6.4.76 | **Security hardening + CI coverage (modern-OS-security north star): an adversarial `http_parse_selftest` locks the HTTP-response parser's bounds-safety into the CI battery (now 20 KATs, was 19), plus a defensive body-init — prompted by a fresh audit of `http_parse_response`, which since v6.4.73 sits on the xbm package-fetch path parsing UNTRUSTED server data.** Audited every path of `kernel/net/http.c`: the status line (`status_text[64]`, `st_len` clamped to 63, negative-length guarded), Content-Length (bounded by `headers_end`, `body_avail = total - offset` cannot underflow), the plain-body memcpy (`kmalloc(body_avail+1)` + `body_avail` bytes, all within `buf`), and — the key attack surface — the **chunked decoder**: its clamp uses the SUBTRACTION form `sz > body_avail - olen` (never `olen + sz`), so with the invariant `olen <= body_avail` a server-controlled huge/overflowing chunk size cannot wrap and skip the clamp; the copy is doubly bounded by `p < end`. The parser was found **already well-hardened** (clear prior security work), so — per the loop rule (do NOT manufacture a bug; lock robustness in a `*_selftest()`, like v6.4.62 imgreject) — no bug was invented. Added: (1) a one-line **defensive init** (`resp->body = NULL; resp->body_len = 0;` at function entry) so EVERY return path (including the error returns) leaves `resp->body` well-defined, closing a latent use-of-uninitialised on a caller that frees after a `-1` return (the chunked-`kmalloc`-fail path never set it); (2) `http_parse_selftest()` — 8 crafted cases: a plain 200+Content-Length body, a de-chunked "hello world", a HOSTILE `0xFFFFFFFF` chunk size (must clamp, not overrun), a hex chunk size that overflows uint32 (must stay bounded), a header-less block (→ success, empty body), a status-less blob (→ rejected `-1`), a 3xx whose `Location:` is extracted, and a Content-Length far larger than the actual body (→ body bounded to the real length, no over-read) — asserting the right fields AND no over-read / heap-overrun; wired into `run_selftests()` (the CI `selftest` battery). Also an interactive `httptest` command (mirrors `imgreject`) + a `man httptest` page + the help "Self-tests & diagnostics" category. **Serial-verified** in QEMU (temp `[HTTPTESTHOOK]` hook calling the self-test before login, reverted — `grep TEMP-TEST`/`HTTPTESTHOOK` empty in source AND the rebuilt binary; the permanent `httpparse` battery entry + the `HTTPPARSE` literal are kept; 0 real kernel faults, matched real `#PF`/`#GP`/`EXCEPTION`/`KERNEL PANIC` markers): `[HTTPPARSE] all cases passed (0 failures across 8 cases)`, hook `rc=0`. Build 0 warnings, `nyx-kernel.bin` 3223.7 KB, no new syscalls (57), no exploitable bug so no GH issue filed (proactive test-coverage). A SECURITY step for variety (prior two were the P4 Nyx Julia and the file-manager sort headers; last security was v6.4.69 x509). Files: `kernel/net/http.c` (defensive body-init + `http_parse_selftest` + `hp_case` helper), `kernel/net/http.h` (proto), `kernel/core/kernel.c` (battery entry + `cmd_httptest` + table/help/man), `kernel/core/kernel.h` (version). **NEXT: v6.4.77 — userland (unexpand/paste/comm/yes), a personalization touch, another parser audit (ext2/tls-record/DER), or another north star; keep VARIETY (not security/file-manager/P4 again).** |
| v6.4.75 | **File-manager polish (the polished-visual-file-management north star, dormant since the v6.4.67 status-bar totals): the list column headers are now clickable to sort — click "Name" or "Size" to sort by that column, click the active column again to flip ascending/descending, with a drawn direction arrow and the active header tinted the Nyx accent purple.** In `fileman_win.c` the fixed "directories first, then A-Z" order becomes a configurable comparator: `fm_entry_less` still groups directories before files (the standard "folders first" behaviour) but within a group orders by the active column -- Name (case-insensitive, via a new `fm_namecmp` helper returning -1/0/1) or Size -- honouring an ascending/descending flag, with Size ties breaking by name so the order stays stable. Two new `fileman_win_t` fields (`sort_key` = FM_SORT_NAME/FM_SORT_SIZE, `sort_desc`) default to name-ascending, preserving the previous behaviour. `fileman_win_click` gains a header-row hit test (the right ~96px over the Size values is the Size zone, the rest is Name); clicking sets the key or flips direction, re-sorts, drops the now-stale selection index and updates the status line ("Sorted by size, descending"). The header draws the active column in the Nyx purple (200,180,255) versus white, with a small up/down triangle (`fm_draw_sort_arrow`, built from stacked `fb_fill_rect` bars -- no glyph needed) marking the direction. **Verified two ways in QEMU** (`scratchpad/fm75.png` + `fm75_zoom.png`, 0 real kernel faults; temp auto-login + a compositor hook that opens the File Manager and drives a real click on the Size header + a serial sort self-test, all reverted -- `grep TEMP-TEST`/`FMSORT` empty in source AND the rebuilt binary): (1) a serial self-test over a known 2-dir/4-file set printed all four orders and matched the expected result exactly (`[FMSORT] name-asc/name-desc/size-asc/size-desc ... -> OK`, fails=0), proving the comparator; (2) the screenshot shows the File Manager at "/" sorted by size ascending -- the eleven directories grouped first, then the files smallest-first (crt0.o 848 B, va_list.o 2 KB, ... spin.elf 9 KB) -- with the "Size" header in Nyx purple carrying an up-arrow and the status bar reading "Sorted by size, ascending". Build 0 warnings, `nyx-kernel.bin` 3221.2 KB, no new syscalls (57). A file-manager step for variety (the last two increments were the xbm net-fetch P3 rung and the P4 Nyx Julia). Files: `kernel/gui/apps/fileman_win.c` (comparator + `fm_namecmp`/`fm_draw_sort_arrow` + header draw + header-click sort), `kernel/gui/apps/fileman_win.h` (FM_SORT_* + sort_key/sort_desc fields), `kernel/core/kernel.h` (version). **NEXT: v6.4.76 -- a security audit (last v6.4.69), userland (unexpand/paste/comm/yes), a personalization touch, or another north star; keep VARIETY (not file-manager/P4/xbm again).** |
| v6.4.74 | **P4 rendering-performance-testing (a Julia-set sibling to Nyx Fractal, completing the backlog's "fractals (Mandelbrot/Julia)" item): a new `julia` command opens `Nyx Julia`, a fixed-point Julia-set renderer whose constant c ORBITS so the set continuously morphs, with a live benchmark HUD.** New `kernel/gui/games/julia_win.{c,h}` render the Julia set with all-integer Q8.24 fixed-point (the kernel is -mno-sse, no floats). Where the Mandelbrot fixes z0=0 and varies c per pixel, the Julia set fixes c and sets z0 = the pixel; here c animates around a circle of radius ~0.7885 (the classic Julia-morph path, driven by an integer parabolic sine `jsin`/`jcos`), so the shape changes every frame and the render load rises and falls on its own -- connected sets (c inside the Mandelbrot) fill more interior pixels to the iteration cap and render slower. Fixed view ~[-1.6,1.6] x [-1.2,1.2], iteration cap 128, purple Nyx cyclic palette + a near-black-violet interior. It animates through `on_tick` at the compositor's ~30 fps game-tick (like Fire/Lava/Fractal). The benchmark HUD (top-right) reports the measured per-frame render time, derived render FPS, the iteration cap, and the LIVE c to three decimals (integer milli-units, no floats). Wired via `launch_julia` (compositor.c) / `cmd_julia` + a `man julia` page + the help "Games & visuals" category (kernel.c) / makefile `julia_win.o` / kernel.h proto, mirroring `mandel_win`. **Screenshot-verified** in QEMU (`scratchpad/julia74.png`, 0 real kernel faults; temp auto-login + a launch-julia compositor hook reverted, `grep TEMP-TEST` empty in source AND the rebuilt binary): the Nyx Julia window shows a purple Julia set with the HUD reading `frame <1 ms / render FPS 999 / iter 128 / c 578,602 e-3`, and a TWO-FRAME capture ~1.3 s apart differed by 111967 pixels, every one inside the centred window -- i.e. the set is genuinely morphing. Build 0 warnings, `nyx-kernel.bin` 3220.2 KB, no new syscalls (57). A P4/perf step for variety (perf was dormant since the v6.4.66 Nyx Fractal; the previous increments were docs / userland / security / personalization / the xbm net-fetch P3 rung). Files: `kernel/gui/games/julia_win.c` + `.h` (new), `kernel/gui/core/compositor.c` (launch_julia + include), `kernel/core/kernel.c` (cmd_julia + command-table entry + man page + help category + forward decl), `kernel/core/kernel.h` (proto + version), `kernel/makefile` (julia_win.o). **NEXT: v6.4.75 -- a file-manager polish, a security audit, xbm net-fetch over HTTPS/TLS, another P4 form (particles/plasma), or another north star.** |
| v6.4.73 | **xbm NETWORK FETCH (the pacman/apt north-star rung -- "download the source, then compile it in-OS"): an `xbm install` recipe can now carry a `url:` line, and xbm downloads that http:// source into the package before building it.** Three new helpers in `core/kernel.c`: `xbm_parse_url` splits `http://host[:port]/path` into a bounded host / port / path (mirrors cmd_httpget's parser); `xbm_save_resp_body` takes an ALREADY-PARSED `http_response_t`, requires a 200 with a body, and writes that body to a file (unlinking first so no stale tail survives) -- this is the DETERMINISTIC core, it takes a response struct not a socket, so it is unit-testable; `xbm_fetch_url` ties them together (parse URL -> `http_get` -> save). The install path now reads an optional `url:` recipe field: when present it fetches the source into `/usr/pkg/<name>/<source>` (both name and source are `pkg_valid_name`-checked, so the destination can't traverse out of /usr/pkg) and then the existing `cc` + install-to-/mnt/bin path compiles it; with no `url:` it uses the local source exactly as before. **Serial-verified** in QEMU via a temp `[XBMNETTEST]` hook (reverted, `grep TEMP-TEST`/`XBMNETTEST` empty in source AND the rebuilt binary, 0 real kernel faults -- checked `#PF`/`#GP`/`EXCEPTION`/`KERNEL PANIC`, not the false-positive 'panic' in help text): `xbm_parse_url("http://example.com:8080/pkg/foo.c")` yields host=example.com port=8080 path=/pkg/foo.c; a CANNED `HTTP/1.1 200` response fed through the REAL `http_parse_response` gives status 200 / body_len 14; and `xbm_save_resp_body` wrote it to /mnt where the read-back matched the body `int nyxnet=7;`. The live socket half (`http_get`) can't be exercised headless -- it is implemented and wired, and everything up to it (URL parse -> HTTP response parse -> body extract -> save to file) is verified. https is a later step (it needs the TLS path); this ships plain HTTP. A NORTH-STAR (xbm = pacman/apt, P3) step for variety, and the first real advance on the long-deferred net-fetch rung (the previous four increments were security / personalization / docs / userland). Build 0 warnings, `nyx-kernel.bin` 3217.4 KB, no new syscalls or commands (a recipe field + three static helpers). Files: `kernel/core/kernel.c` (the three helpers + the `url:` install wiring + the `man xbm` update), `kernel/core/kernel.h` (version). **NEXT: v6.4.74 -- xbm net-fetch over HTTPS/TLS or multi-source recipes, a P4 perf demo, a file-mgr polish, a security audit, or another north star.** |
| v6.4.72 | **Userland toward Arch/Void quality: an `expand` command converts tabs to spaces, aligned to tab stops.** New `cmd_expand` in `core/kernel.c`: `expand [-t N] <file>` replaces each tab with just enough spaces to reach the NEXT tab stop (stops are N columns apart, 8 by default, or `-t N`), so columns stay aligned -- it tracks the current column rather than emitting a fixed N spaces per tab; a newline resets the column and every non-tab character passes through unchanged. Accepts both `-t N` and `-tN`, and is file-arg like fold/rev/tr (NyxOS kernel builtins have no stdin). Wired as a builtin + a `man expand` page, and placed in the `help` command's "Text" category (from v6.4.71). **Serial-verified** in QEMU via a temp `[EXPANDTEST]` hook that writes a tab fixture to `/mnt` and runs expand at two widths (reverted, `grep TEMP-TEST`/`EXPANDTEST` empty in source AND the rebuilt binary, 0 real kernel faults): at the default width 8, `a\tb\tc` -> `a`+7sp+`b`+7sp+`c`, `AB\tCD` -> `AB`+6sp+`CD`, and `\t x` -> 8sp+`" x"` (a tab to column 8 then the literal space); at `-t 4` the same lines align to 4-column stops (`a...b...c`, `AB..CD`, 4sp+`" x"`) -- i.e. proper tab-stop alignment, not fixed-width substitution. A non-visual USERLAND step for variety (the previous three increments were security / personalization / docs; the last plain userland tool was `printf` at v6.4.68). Build 0 warnings, `nyx-kernel.bin` 3216.4 KB, no new syscalls (57). Files: `kernel/core/kernel.c` (new `cmd_expand` + command-table entry + man page + forward decl + the help "Text" category entry), `kernel/core/kernel.h` (version). **NEXT: v6.4.73 -- more userland (unexpand/paste/comm/yes), the xbm net-fetch arc (deterministic verify), a security audit, docs (man coverage), or another north star.** |
| v6.4.71 | **Docs (extremely-high-docs north star, the most dormant category since v6.4.63): the `help` command now GROUPS the ~110 builtins under category headers instead of dumping one flat alphabetical list.** `cmd_help` (`core/kernel.c`) was rewritten around a `help_cats[]` table that maps 10 categories -- Shell & help / Files & directories / Text / Processes & system / Users / Networking / Development / Media & apps / Games & visuals / Self-tests & diagnostics -- to the command names that belong in each. It prints every category's header followed by that category's commands with their one-line help (looked up from `commands[]` via `help_text_of`, so a listed name that no longer exists or is hidden is silently skipped). A final "Other" pass prints any VISIBLE command not placed in a category, so nothing is ever dropped even when a new command hasn't been categorised yet -- the catch-all surfaced 9 stragglers on the first run (`smpthreads`/`smpbalance` + seven crypto self-tests), which were then folded into Self-tests so Other is now empty. Purely a display reorganisation + a static name->category table: the `commands[]` table and every command's behaviour are unchanged, no new syscalls or commands. **Serial-verified** in QEMU via a temp `[HELPTEST]` hook that calls `cmd_help` before login (reverted, `grep TEMP-TEST`/`HELPTEST` empty in source AND the rebuilt binary, 0 kernel faults): `help` renders the ten grouped sections, each command aligned with its description, and after the straggler fix the Other section is empty. | v6.4.69 | **Security (modern-OS-security north star; a REAL bug found by auditing the TLS trust model): `x509_verify_chain` accepted a non-CA certificate as a chain issuer -- the classic basicConstraints bypass (CVE-2002-0862 class). Opened + fixed GitHub issue #49 (`Closes #49`).** `kernel/crypto/x509.c` verified that each certificate was signed by the next certificate's key and that the topmost key matched a pinned trusted root, but it never checked the `basicConstraints` extension of the *issuing* certificates. So a valid end-entity (leaf) certificate -- which carries `cA = FALSE` -- could be used as a signing CA: an attacker with a legitimate leaf that chains to a pinned root can sign a forged certificate for any host and present `[forged victim.com, attacker leaf, real intermediates, pinned root]`; every link verifies, the top is the pinned root, and `x509_check_host` passes on the forged leaf's SAN -> full impersonation, exploitable even with root-key pinning. Fix: a new `cert_is_ca()` parses `basicConstraints` (OID 2.5.29.19) and returns true only when the SEQUENCE's first element is a `cA` BOOLEAN with a non-zero value (absent / FALSE / other -> not a CA); `x509_verify_chain` now rejects (`X509_FORGED`, "an issuer certificate is not a CA") any chain whose issuer -- every certificate except the leaf -- is not a CA. The leaf itself stays unconstrained, and the pinned top is a CA (it is the issuer of the cert below it). A new `x509_selftest` case #6 (battery now 6/6) asserts `cert_is_ca(leaf)==0` while `cert_is_ca(intermediate)==cert_is_ca(root)==1` on the real captured chain, so the check distinguishes CA from non-CA on real certificates AND the real chain still verifies. **Serial-verified** in QEMU via a temp `[X509TEST]` hook (reverted, `grep TEMP-TEST`/`X509TEST` empty in source AND the rebuilt binary, 0 kernel faults): the real 4-cert chain is still TRUSTED (anchored to `SSL.com TLS ECC Root CA 2022`), the tampered leaf is still rejected, the root-less prefix is still not-anchored, and the new case #6 PASSes -> `x509: self-test 6/6 passed`. The DER cursor (`der.c`) and the rest of `x509.c` were audited too and found already well-bounded (long-form length rejects nb=0 / nb>4, an `end - p < len` check, `parse_time` validates digit-count + length, `host_match` uses explicit lengths not NUL) -- no other bug. A SECURITY step for variety (the previous three increments were P4 / file-manager / userland; the last security touch was v6.4.62). Build 0 warnings, `nyx-kernel.bin` 3212.8 KB, no new syscalls (57). Files: `kernel/crypto/x509.c` (cert_is_ca + the issuer CA check + selftest case 6), `kernel/core/kernel.h` (version). **NEXT: v6.4.70 -- docs (man coverage), more userland (paste/comm/expand), the xbm net-fetch arc (deterministic verify), a personalization/visual step, or another north star.** |
| v6.4.68 | **Userland toward Arch/Void quality: a `printf` command formats and prints its arguments like the coreutil/shell `printf`.** New `cmd_printf` in `core/kernel.c`: `printf FORMAT [ARG...]` walks FORMAT, emitting literal text, interpreting C backslash escapes (`\n \t \r \a \b \f \v \\`), and handling the conversions `%s %d %i %u %x %X %c %%`. Optional flags and a field width are supported (`%-10s`, `%05d`) by rebuilding the single spec and passing it straight to the kernel formatter; any `l` length modifier is dropped so the vararg stays int-width (numeric ARGs are read with `atoi`). It follows POSIX FORMAT REUSE -- if FORMAT contains at least one conversion, the whole FORMAT is applied repeatedly until the ARGs are exhausted (so `printf "%s\n" a b c` prints three lines), and it adds NO trailing newline unless FORMAT has one (unlike `echo`). Arg-based like `tr`/`fold`/`rev` (kernel builtins take no stdin); wired as a builtin with a `man printf` page. Passing the rebuilt non-literal spec to `printf()` is safe under the kernel's warning set (`-Wall -Wextra -Wshadow`, no `-Wformat-nonliteral`), and each conversion dispatches the matching vararg type. **Serial-verified** in QEMU (temp `[PRINTFTEST]` hook reverted, `grep TEMP-TEST` empty in source AND the rebuilt binary, 0 kernel faults): `printf "%s-%d\n" hello 42` -> `hello-42`; `printf "%s\n" a b c` -> three lines `a`/`b`/`c` (FORMAT reuse); `printf "%05d|%x|%%\n" 42 255` -> `00042|ff|%` (zero-padded width + hex + literal percent); `printf "tab\tend\n"` -> a real tab; `printf "%-6s|%c|\n" hi Z` -> `hi    |Z|` (left-aligned width + a single char). A non-visual USERLAND step for variety (the previous two increments were the P4 Nyx Fractal and the File-Manager totals; the last plain userland tool was `fold` at v6.4.64). Build 0 warnings, `nyx-kernel.bin` 3212.1 KB, no new syscalls (57). Files: `kernel/core/kernel.c` (new `cmd_printf` + command-table entry + man page + forward decl), `kernel/core/kernel.h` (version). **NEXT: v6.4.69 -- more userland (paste/comm/expand/yes), a security parser audit, docs (man coverage), the xbm net-fetch arc (deterministic verify), or another north star.** |

New `kernel/gui/games/mandel_win.{c,h}` render the Mandelbrot set with all-integer Q8.24 fixed-point complex arithmetic (the kernel is built -mno-sse, so NO floats -- constants like the centre are built with integer math too) into a 120x90 grid drawn as 4 px blocks (480x360 window). It ping-pongs an auto-zoom toward the classic "seahorse valley" point (-0.745, 0.113): span shrinks 0.96875^level (recomputed from the level each frame so it never drifts), and the iteration cap scales with the zoom (48 at 1x up to 220 deep) so the render load genuinely rises as it zooms in. Palette is a purple Nyx cyclic ramp (green kept low so it stays in the violet family), interior a near-black violet -- on-brand (Nyx = goddess of night). The whole point is the benchmark HUD (top-right): each `on_tick` re-renders the set for the new view, timed with `get_ticks()` (PIT 1 kHz, so 1 ms resolution), and the HUD shows the measured per-frame render time, the derived render FPS, the current iteration cap, and the zoom factor -- i.e. it MEASURES the software renderer under a continuously varying load, feeding P4. | v6.4.64 | **Userland toward Arch/Void quality: a `fold` command wraps long lines to a fixed width.** New `cmd_fold` in `core/kernel.c`: `fold [-w WIDTH] <file>` re-emits each line of `<file>` so no output line exceeds WIDTH columns (default 80) -- a line longer than WIDTH is hard-broken with a newline at exactly WIDTH characters (classic `fold`: a hard break, no word boundaries), while shorter lines and the input's own newlines pass through unchanged. Accepts both the `-w N` (spaced) and `-wN` (attached) flag forms, and clamps WIDTH to >= 1. File-arg like rev/tr/wc (NyxOS kernel builtins have no stdin). | v6.4.62 | **Security hardening + CI coverage (modern-OS-security north star): a new `imgreject` self-test proves the four image decoders REFUSE malformed/hostile input, and it now runs in the CI self-test battery.** The existing `pngtest`/`bmptest`/`giftest`/`jpegtest` known-answer tests only cover VALID images; the new `image_reject_selftest()` (in `kernel/image/png.c`) covers the property that actually matters when Selene feeds a decoder bytes straight off the network -- that junk is REJECTED (a negative return, no crash, no out-of-bounds access) rather than accepted. It feeds all four `*_decode()` entry points an empty buffer and 64 bytes of garbage, then mutates the embedded valid 4x4 RGB PNG into hostile variants -- zero width, oversized width (8192 > PNG_MAX_DIM), an invalid colour type, a corrupt signature and a truncated stream -- and asserts every one returns < 0 (a decode that ACCEPTS junk is the failure). Wired into `run_selftests()` so the multiboot `selftest` cmdline / CI `selftests` job now runs 19 KATs (was 18), and given an interactive `imgreject` command for parity with the other `*test` builtins. Prompted by a fresh SECURITY AUDIT of the image decoders: PNG/BMP/GIF/JPEG were all found to already bound dimensions (MAX_DIM 4096, MAX_PIXELS 1M), NULL-check their allocations and validate the input length against the header (e.g. `pixoff + stride*height > srclen`), so there was NO bug to fix -- this LOCKS that hostile-input robustness in against future regressions. **Serial-verified** in QEMU (`imgreject: all hostile inputs rejected PASS`, 0 kernel faults; the temp `[IMGREJECT]` hook was reverted, `grep TEMP-TEST` empty in source AND the rebuilt binary). A SECURITY / test-coverage step for variety (last was voxel-perf; the net HTTP/URL parsers were audited clean in v6.4.61, so this audited a different subsystem). Build 0 warnings, `nyx-kernel.bin` 3202.6 KB, no new syscalls (57). Files: `kernel/image/png.c` (new `image_reject_selftest` + `img_must_reject` + sibling includes), `kernel/image/png.h`, `kernel/core/kernel.c` (extern + run_selftests table entry + `cmd_imgreject` + command-table entry), `kernel/core/kernel.h` (version). **NEXT: v6.4.63 -- the xbm network-fetch arc, a docs step (man coverage), userland (fold/paste/comm), or another parser audit.** |

Each bar is coloured by how far ABOVE the fastest frame (the baseline / minimum sample) it sits: green near the baseline, amber at >= 1.5x, red at >= 2x, so frame-time STABILITY and outliers are visible at a glance instead of just the latest number (a real profiling instrument feeding P4, not eye-candy). All-integer (the kernel is -mno-sse). | v6.4.60 | **Userland toward Arch/Void quality (a NON-visual step after the fire/matrix/lava effect trio): a `tr` command translates, deletes or squeezes characters.** New `cmd_tr` in `core/kernel.c` (plus a `tr_expand()` helper): `tr SET1 SET2 <file>` maps each byte of `<file>` that appears in SET1 to the byte at the same index in SET2 (a shorter SET2 repeats its last character, GNU behaviour); `tr -d SET1 <file>` deletes the SET1 bytes instead; `tr -s SET1 [SET2] <file>` squeezes each run of a repeated result byte down to one; `-ds` combines delete-then-squeeze. SET strings honour ascending ranges (`a-z`, `0-9`) which `tr_expand()` flattens; a `-` that is not a valid range is taken literally. NyxOS shell builtins are file-oriented (there is no stdin into a kernel builtin), so `tr` takes a file argument like `rev`/`sort`/`wc` rather than the classic stdin filter. Wired into the command table and given a `man tr` manual page (keeps the v6.4.56 `man` coverage growing). **Serial-verified** in QEMU over an ext2 `/mnt` fixture (temp `[TRTEST]` hook reverted -- `grep TEMP-TEST` empty, 0 kernel faults, login screen reached): `tr a-z A-Z` -> `HELLOWORLD123`, `tr -d 0-9` -> `HelloWorld`, `tr l L` -> `HeLLoWorLd123` (single-char map), `tr a-c X` -> `XXXXXXdd` (SET2 last-char repeat), and `tr -s a-z` on `aabbccdd` -> `abcd` (squeeze). Build 0 warnings, `nyx-kernel.bin` 3200.3 KB, no new syscalls (57). | v6.4.59 | **Visual effect (COMPLETES the user-backlog /fire //matrix //lava trio; also a P4 visual perf demo): a `lava` command opens `Nyx Lava`, an animated plasma / "lava lamp" window.** New `kernel/gui/games/lava_win.{c,h}`: a classic demoscene plasma on a 120x90 grid (480x360 client, 4x4 cells) where each cell's value is the sum of four phase-shifted sine waves — horizontal (`x*5+t`), vertical (`y*5-t`), diagonal (`(x+y)*3+t`), and a radial ripple from the centre (`dist*6 + t*2`, `dist` from `fb_isqrt`) — mapped through a 256-entry cyclic "heat" palette (black->red->orange->yellow->white, mirrored into a triangle so a wrapping value never seams) and animated by advancing `t` every game tick. | v6.4.54 | **Security hardening (modern-OS-security north star -- a real bug found by auditing the network parsers): the DNS resolver now resists response spoofing. Closes #48.** `kernel/net/dns.c` sent every query with a FIXED transaction ID (`hdr->id = htons(0x1234)`) and `dns_response_handler()` never validated the ID of incoming responses -- it accepted any UDP datagram reaching the query's source port that had the QR flag + an A record, and returned its first A record. A blind/off-path attacker (or any host that reaches the port) could forge a reply and poison the resolved address (CWE-330 / DNS spoofing), which matters because NyxOS resolves names for HTTP/TLS (Selene, `httpget`, `wget`). Fix: draw a random 16-bit transaction ID per query from the CSPRNG (`csprng_bytes`, the same HMAC_DRBG source TLS uses for key material) instead of the constant, remember it in a new `dns_query_id`, and drop any response whose `ntohs(hdr->id)` does not match it. The image decoders (bmp/gif) were audited first and found already well-bounded (dimension caps + uint64 offset math), so this pivoted to the network path. **Serial-verified** in QEMU (0 kernel faults, temp handler self-test reverted -- `grep TEMP-TEST` empty): a crafted A-record response was ACCEPTED with the matching ID and REJECTED with a corrupted ID (`DNSIDTEST match=1 mismatch_rejected=1 PASS`); legitimate resolution is unaffected because the server echoes our ID. Build 0 warnings, `nyx-kernel.bin` 3188.8 KB, no new syscalls (57). Different subsystem from the last three increments (desktop/voxel/userland) for variety. Files: `kernel/net/dns.c`, `core/kernel.h`. **NEXT: v6.4.55 -- more security (audit another parser: HTTP headers, DER/TLS, ext2), xbm network fetch, docs, userland, or a voxel perf rung.** |
| v6.4.53 | **Userland toward Arch/Void quality (north star): two POSIX path utilities, `basename` and `dirname`, added as shell builtins.** These are used constantly in shell scripts and were missing entirely (no builtin, no `user/*.c`). New `cmd_basename`/`cmd_dirname` in `kernel/core/kernel.c` (forward-declared, wired into the `commands[]` table after `which`, GNU-compatible behaviour): `basename <path> [suffix]` strips the directory prefix and an optional trailing suffix (`"/usr/lib"`->`lib`, `"/usr/"`->`usr`, `"/"`->`/`, `""`->empty line, `"/a/b.txt" ".txt"`->`b`; the suffix is only removed when it is a proper shorter suffix, so `basename .txt .txt` keeps `.txt`); `dirname <path>` strips the last component (`"/usr/lib"`->`/usr`, `"/usr/"`->`/`, `"usr"`->`.`, `"/"`->`/`, `""`->`.`, `"a/b/c"`->`a/b`). Both operate on a bounded 256-byte local copy of the argv string (the cap is made explicit rather than relying on the command-line buffer size), collapsing trailing slashes per POSIX. **Serial-verified** in QEMU (0 kernel faults, temp `execute_command` test block reverted -- `grep TEMP-TEST` empty): all 8 cases printed the expected GNU output (`lib / usr / / / bar` and `/usr / / / . / a/b`). Audited the builtin table first (memory note 2) -- neither name pre-existed. Build 0 warnings, `nyx-kernel.bin` 3188.7 KB, no new syscalls (57). Pivots off the voxel/desktop run for variety (last: v6.4.52 voxel perf HUD). Files: `kernel/core/kernel.c`, `core/kernel.h`. **NEXT: v6.4.54 -- more userland (rev / fold / basename's cousins, or a stdin pipe tool), a voxel perf rung, xbm network fetch, a security audit, or docs.** |

A top-right HUD panel reports the live numbers: `BENCH ON/off`, `cubes N` (the per-frame cube fill count), `world 7x7 vN` (grid + camera view), and in bench mode `frame X.YY ms` (per-frame render time, from microseconds) + `render FPS N` (frames the renderer can push = reps*1000/elapsed_ms). New `voxel_ctx_t` fields `bench` + `last_fps`/`last_ft_us`/`last_cubes`; `voxel_win_tick` returns 1 every tick while benchmarking (continuous repaint for a live read) instead of only when dirty; the decorative moon moved off the HUD corner; the footer hint gained `B bench`. All integer math (kernel is -mno-sse, no floats). This is the concrete driver of P4 (fill rate / voxel throughput). **Screenshot-verified** in QEMU (`scratchpad/voxel52.png`, 0 kernel faults, temp hooks reverted -- `grep TEMP-TEST` empty): the HUD read `cubes 76 / frame 2.40 ms / render FPS 416` over a live-rendering island. | v6.4.50 | **Bug fix (terminal/UX -- the LAST unfixed item from the user's hands-on test, "el cmd se sobreescribe a veces"): the Terminal no longer overwrites its newest output line with the input prompt once the scrollback fills the window. Closes #47.** In `kernel/gui/apps/terminal_win.c`, `terminal_win_draw` drew the scrollback across ALL `rows` visible rows, then unconditionally drew the live input line on the last row (rows-1) -- so the last scrollback line drawn landed on the input row and was painted over by the prompt; with `start_line = line_count - rows - 1` (off by one) the newest 1-2 output lines were hidden/overwritten once `line_count >= rows`. Few lines were fine (start_line clamps to 0), which is why it only happened "sometimes". Now normal mode RESERVES the last row for the input: it draws `rows - 1` scrollback rows and derives `start_line` from that same count, so the newest scrollback line sits just above the input. **Screenshot-verified** in QEMU (0 kernel faults, `scratchpad/term50.png`, temp hooks reverted): a Terminal running `help` (far more output lines than fit) shows its last line (`df - Show disk usage...`) immediately above the `nyx:...$` prompt instead of vanishing. | v6.4.46 | **Bug fix (input/UX -- from the user's hands-on v6.4.44 test): the PS/2 mouse driver now discards packets whose X/Y overflow flags are set, so the cursor no longer teleports on a fast move and window drags track cleanly. Closes #45.** `mouse_irq_handler` (`kernel/drivers/input/mouse.c`) applied the movement bytes unconditionally; but byte 0's bits 6/7 are the X/Y overflow flags, and when QEMU raises them on a large/fast jump the dx/dy counters have wrapped and are meaningless -> the cursor jumped erratically (and a desynced/garbage packet could also feed a phantom button bit into a drag). Now the final-byte apply keeps the button bits (0-2, always valid) but SKIPS the movement when `packet[0] & 0xC0` is set; the existing byte-0 bit-3 check still resyncs the stream. **Verified:** build 0 warnings, headless boot 0 kernel faults (`[MOUSE] Initialized (wheel)`), `nyx-kernel.bin` 3187.4 KB, no new syscalls (57). Movement/drag feel is by construction (a headless VM can't drive the real PS/2 mouse), pending the user's interactive re-test; if a stuck drag survives, a compositor drag-watchdog is the queued follow-up. Second fix of the post-test desktop-hardening arc (after v6.4.45's click-leak fix). | v6.4.45 | **Bug fix (desktop/UX -- from the user's hands-on v6.4.44 test): a held left-click on a window's Close button or title bar no longer leaks to the application beneath it; the compositor's window-action block is now PRESS-EDGE-triggered. Closes #44.** The window hit-test/action block in `kernel/gui/core/compositor.c` ran under `if (btns & 1)`, which is LEVEL-triggered -- it re-ran every compositor frame while the button was held (only the menu handlers had been edge-gated). So the instant the top window was closed/minimised/maximised, or slid out from under the pointer mid-drag, the very next frame re-hit-tested, focused the window now underneath and delivered the click to IT (and `on_pressed` fired unconditionally every frame). Now focus + close/min/max + drag/resize-START fire only on the press edge (`pressed = !(prev_btns & 1)`); drag/resize CONTINUATION still runs off the persistent `drag_id`/`resize_id` (unchanged); and a HELD press is delivered only to the already-focused window under the pointer and only in its content area (`my >= win->y + TITLE_H`), never a freshly re-picked one -- so a click can no longer fall through to a lower window. **Verified:** build 0 warnings; headless boot to the login screen with 0 kernel faults; `nyx-kernel.bin` 3187.4 KB; no new syscalls (57). Click-routing correctness is by construction + reasoning (a headless VM can't drive the real PS/2 mouse), pending the user's interactive re-test. First fix in the post-test desktop-hardening arc opened by the user's feedback (still queued: PS/2 mouse-driver hardening for the stuck-drag/erratic-cursor, the dead Settings buttons' draw/hit-test coordinate drift, the minimal 'apps-in-the-menu' desktop redesign, and higher resolutions). Files: `kernel/gui/core/compositor.c`, `core/kernel.h`. **NEXT: v6.4.46 -- PS/2 mouse-driver hardening, or the Settings-button coordinate fix, or the minimal-desktop redesign.** |
| v6.4.44 | **Security hardening: the `xbm` package manager now validates package/recipe names, closing a path-traversal + arbitrary-write + `cc`-arg-injection hole -- toward the modern-OS-security north star (and variety after the recent GUI work).** `xbm install`/`remove` build filesystem paths and a `cc` command line by interpolating the CLI package `name` (`/usr/pkg/<name>/recipe`) and the recipe's `source:`/`bin:` fields (compile input + `/mnt/bin/<bin>` output) directly. Unvalidated, a crafted name like `../../etc` could traverse out of `/usr/pkg`, a recipe `bin: ../../foo` could make `cc` write its output OUTSIDE `/mnt/bin` (arbitrary write), and a recipe `source: x.c -o /evil` could inject extra `cc` arguments through the space. A new `pkg_valid_name()` now rejects any token that is empty, `.`/`..`, or contains `/` or whitespace, and is applied to `name` (install + remove), the recipe `source`/`bin` (install), and the resolved `bin` (remove) before any path or command is built. **Serial-verified** live in QEMU (temp self-test, reverted), 0 kernel faults: `xbm install ../foo`, `xbm install a/b`, and `xbm remove ../x` are all rejected with `invalid package name`, while a valid name (`xbm install nosuchpkg`) passes validation and reaches the normal `package not found` path -- no false rejections, no regression. `nyx-kernel.bin` 3187.2 KB. **No new syscalls (still 57).** (Proactive hardening fixed in-place -- the shipped recipes are trusted today, but this matters once the `xbm` network-fetch arc introduces untrusted recipes -- so no separate issue was filed.) Files: `kernel/core/kernel.c`, `core/kernel.h`. **NEXT: v6.4.45 -- the `xbm` network-fetch arc, a userland/docs step, more visual polish, or a small voxel rung; the FP raycaster + brand-name + menu-only-desktop forks await the user.** |

A new `fm_file_accent(name)` finds the extension (last `.` after the last `/`) and returns the tint via a case-insensitive `fm_extcmp`; `fm_draw_file_icon` gained a `page` colour parameter and darkened its edge/fold so the page's "text lines" stay legible on any tint. Folders keep their blue folder glyph; the name text and Size column are unchanged. **Screenshot-verified** live in QEMU (headless, temp auto-login + temp `launch_fileman("/")` hook, both reverted), 0 kernel faults: at `/` the `.elf` programs show green document icons and `crt0.o` shows an amber one, next to the blue folders. `nyx-kernel.bin` 3186.7 KB. **No new syscalls (still 57).** Files: `kernel/gui/apps/fileman_win.c`, `core/kernel.h`. **NEXT: v6.4.44 -- more visual polish, a security pass, the `xbm` network-fetch arc, a userland/docs step, or back to a small voxel rung; the first-person raycaster + brand-name + menu-only-desktop forks await the user.** |

| v6.4.37 | **`ls` gains the `-a` and `-l` flags -- a real flags-upgrade toward Arch/Void quality.** `ls` took only `[path]`; now it parses leading combinable flags (`-la`): `-a` shows dot-entries (hidden by default in the flagged path), `-l` prints a long format -- a type char (`d`/`-`) + right-aligned byte size + name, one `stat()`/`vfs_stat()` per entry. Applied to BOTH the in-kernel `ls` builtin (which runs by bare name -- audited the command table first, the recurring lesson) AND the `user/ls.c` ELF. In the builtin the flagless `ls [path]` still delegates to the classic colored `vfs_list_dir` (output unchanged, zero regression); `-a`/`-l` iterate the directory via the backend-agnostic `vfs_open`+`vfs_readdir`+`vfs_stat` (works on both the ramfs and mounted ext2). The userland ELF keeps its ANSI colouring (dirs bright blue, `.elf` bright green) for the short format and adds the same `-a`/`-l`. **Verified** live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults, on a fixture dir (`alpha.txt` 6 B, hidden `.hidden` 2 B, subdir `sub`): `ls -a` -> all three; `ls -l` -> `- 6 alpha.txt` / `d 0 sub` (`.hidden` correctly hidden); `ls -la` -> also `- 2 .hidden`. `nyx-kernel.bin` 3181.2 KB. **No new syscalls (still 57).** Files: `kernel/core/kernel.c` (builtin + command-table help), `user/ls.c`, `core/kernel.h`. **NEXT: v6.4.38 -- a visual polish, a security pass, the `xbm` network-fetch arc, or a scoped Minecraft-voxel scout; the big menu-only-desktop fork awaits the user.** |
| v6.4.36 | **`sort` gains the `-r`/`-n` flags -- a real flags-upgrade toward Arch/Void quality.** `sort` previously took only `<file>` and sorted lines lexically; now it parses leading combinable flags (`-rn`): `-r` reverses the order, `-n` sorts numerically by each line's leading integer (so "10" orders after "9" instead of lexically before it). Applied to BOTH the in-kernel `sort` builtin (which runs by bare name -- audited the command table first, the v6.4.31/32/34 lesson) AND the `user/sort.c` ELF (which keeps its stdin/pipeline + multi-file support). The builtin uses a single bounded read (2 KB -> 8 KB static buffer, line cap 128 -> 256), NOT a `vfs_read` loop (the v6.4.32 EOF-hang lesson); a small sign-aware `sort_numkey`/`numkey` reads the leading integer for `-n`. **Verified** live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults, on the fixture `10 / 9 / 2 / 100 / 1`: `sort` -> `1 10 100 2 9` (lexical); `-n` -> `1 2 9 10 100`; `-r` -> `9 2 100 10 1`; `-nr` -> `100 10 9 2 1`. `nyx-kernel.bin` 3180.5 KB. **No new syscalls (still 57).** Files: `kernel/core/kernel.c` (builtin + command-table help), `user/sort.c`, `core/kernel.h`. **NEXT: v6.4.37 -- another tool flags-upgrade (`ls -l/-a`), a visual polish, a security pass, or a scoped Minecraft-voxel scout; the big menu-only-desktop fork awaits the user.** |

| v6.4.32 | **`wc` gains the `-l`/`-w`/`-c` count-selecting flags -- a real flags-upgrade toward Arch/Void quality.** `wc` previously always printed all three counts; now `-l` (lines), `-w` (words), `-c`/`-m` (bytes) select which to print, in the fixed line-word-byte order, and combine (`-lw`); with no flag the classic all-three default is kept. Applied to BOTH the in-kernel `wc` builtin (which runs by bare name) and the shadowed `user/wc.c` ELF so they stay consistent -- audited the command table first (the lesson from v6.4.31) and found `wc` was a builtin like `diff`. The builtin's single read is bumped 2 KB -> 8 KB (a static buffer). **Verified** live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults, on `hello world / foo bar baz` (2 lines, 5 words, 24 bytes): bare `wc` -> `2 5 24`; `wc -l` -> `2`; `wc -w` -> `5`; `wc -c` -> `24`; `wc -lw` -> `2 5`; and `/wc.elf -l` -> `2`, `/wc.elf -c` -> `24`. (A first streaming rewrite of the builtin looped forever on `vfs_read` -- the kernel VFS read does not reach EOF in a bare loop the way the userland `read()` syscall does -- caught by the harness hanging and reverted to a single bounded read.) | v6.4.31 | **`diff` is now a real LCS-based diff, replacing the naive positional builtin.** The old in-kernel `diff` builtin compared the two files line-by-line at the same index, so a single insertion or deletion reported every following line as different and never matched real diff output. It is removed; a new `user/diff.c` -- a proper longest-common-subsequence diff -- takes its place (resolved by bare name now that the builtin is gone). It reads two files, computes the LCS of their lines, and prints the classic "normal" diff format: `LcR`/`LdR`/`LaR` hunk headers with `< ` (file 1) and `> ` (file 2) lines and a `---` between the two sides of a change; exit status 0 if identical, 1 if they differ (like GNU diff). Bounded to 200 lines / 32 KB per file. Wired as a userland ELF (`user/diff.c` + a `USER_ELFS` entry + Makefile rules); the kernel's `cmd_diff` and its command-table entry are deleted. **Verified** live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults, output matching GNU diff: `alpha/beta/gamma/delta` vs `alpha/BETA/gamma/epsilon/delta` -> `2c2 / < beta / --- / > BETA / 3a4 / > epsilon` (exit 1); `one/two/three` vs `one/three` -> `2d1 / < two` (exit 1); identical files -> no output (exit 0). Production boot clean, reaches login; `nyx-kernel.bin` 3175.8 KB. **No new syscalls (still 57).** Files: `user/diff.c` (new), `kernel/core/kernel.c` (naive builtin removed), `kernel/Makefile`, `core/kernel.h`. **NEXT: v6.4.32 -- more userland/tool-quality work or a visual desktop polish; the big menu-only-desktop fork awaits the user.** |
| v6.4.30 | **A real `seq` coreutil -- the userland climb continues (fifth base tool).** After uniq/cut/nl/tac, a new `user/seq.c` prints an integer sequence like GNU `seq`: `seq LAST` (1..LAST), `seq FIRST LAST` (step 1), `seq FIRST INCR LAST` (arbitrary step, INCR may be negative to count down); `-s SEP` sets the separator (default newline) and `-w` pads all numbers to equal width with leading zeros. It takes numeric arguments (no file), uses a self-contained sign-aware parser, and writes straight to stdout. Wired as a userland ELF (`user/seq.c` + a `USER_ELFS` entry + compile/link rules in the Makefile, auto-embedded), runs by bare name. **Verified** live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults: `seq 5` -> 1..5; `seq 2 6` -> 2..6; `seq 1 2 9` -> 1,3,5,7,9; `seq -s , 1 4` -> `1,2,3,4`; `seq -w 8 11` -> 08,09,10,11; `seq 3 -1 1` -> 3,2,1. Production boot clean, reaches login; `nyx-kernel.bin` 3164.9 KB. **No new syscalls (still 57).** Files: `user/seq.c` (new), `kernel/Makefile`, `core/kernel.h`. **NEXT: v6.4.31 -- more userland (`diff`), a flags-upgrade to an existing tool, or a visual desktop polish; the big menu-only-desktop fork awaits the user.** |

| v6.4.28 | **A real `tac` coreutil -- the userland climb continues.** Fourth base-toolset addition (after uniq/cut/nl): a new `user/tac.c` prints a file (or stdin) with its lines in reverse order -- the complement of `cat` (tac is cat backwards). It reads the whole input, splits it into lines (the bytes up to each `\n`), and emits them last-to-first, each newline-terminated; empty lines are preserved. Bounded to a fixed buffer (input past the cap is dropped with a note). Wired as a userland ELF (`user/tac.c` + a `USER_ELFS` entry + compile/link rules in the Makefile, auto-embedded), runs by bare name. **Verified** live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults: `tac` of `one/two/three` prints `three/two/one`; `tac` of `a / <blank> / b` prints `b / <blank> / a` (the middle blank line preserved). Production boot clean, reaches login; `nyx-kernel.bin` 3153.5 KB. **No new syscalls (still 57).** Files: `user/tac.c` (new), `kernel/Makefile`, `core/kernel.h`. **NEXT: v6.4.29 -- more userland (`diff`/`seq`), a real flags-upgrade to an existing tool, or alternate to a visual desktop polish; the big menu-only-desktop fork awaits the user.** |
| v6.4.27 | **A real `nl` coreutil -- back to the userland climb toward Arch/Void quality.** Third base-toolset addition (after `uniq` and `cut`): a new `user/nl.c` numbers the lines of a file (or stdin) like GNU `nl`. `-b a` numbers every line, `-b t` (the default) numbers only non-empty lines, `-w N` sets the right-justified number-field width (default 6), and `-s SEP` sets the separator printed after the number (default TAB); the running count advances only on numbered lines, and a skipped blank line is printed unchanged. A sample package ships at `/usr/pkg/hello/` (a `recipe` + `hello.c`) via a new `tools/mkinitramfs.py` block that walks `user/pkg/**` into `/usr/pkg/**`. **Verified** live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults: `pkg list` shows `hello`; `pkg install hello` parses the recipe, runs `cc /usr/pkg/hello/hello.c -o /mnt/bin/hello` (creating `/mnt/bin` via `vfs_mkdir`), and reports `installed hello -> /mnt/bin/hello`; running `/mnt/bin/hello` prints its line and exits 7 -- so the OS installs software by compiling it from source inside itself. **This slice is LOCAL only** (no network fetch, dependencies, versions, or PATH lookup yet -- installed binaries run by full path). | v6.4.18 | **Phase 2 (self-host arc COMPLETE): `cc --self` rebuilds a real coreutil byte-identically -- the bootstrapped self-hosted compiler compiles `sort.c` and its output matches the GCC-built `/sort.elf` exactly.** The capstone that ties the arc together: v6.4.17 added `cc --self` (compile with the in-OS-built tcc, built + cached on demand); this proves that path produces a correct real program. **Verified** live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults, the full chain in one boot: `cc --self /usr/src/nyx/sort.c -o /mnt/sort3` builds + caches `/mnt/tcc_self.elf` (the in-OS tcc compiles tcc.c -> links a runnable tcc) then compiles `sort.c` WITH that self-hosted tcc (`cc: compiling (self-hosted tcc) -> /mnt/sort3`, exit 0); running `/mnt/sort3 <fixture>` and the reference `/sort.elf <fixture>` both print the identical 33 bytes (`apple`/`apricot`/`banana`/`cherry`/`date`, strcmp-sorted).
