| v6.4.17 | **Phase 2 (self-host bootstrap): the `cc` builtin can now compile with the SELF-BUILT tcc -- a new `cc --self` mode routes the compile through `/mnt/tcc_self.elf` (a tcc built by the in-OS tcc) instead of the GCC-cross-built `/tcc.elf`, building and caching that self-hosted compiler on demand.** `cc --self <src> [-o out]` (kernel/core/kernel.c, cmd_cc): if `/mnt/tcc_self.elf` is absent, a new `tcc_self_ready()` builds it from the shipped tcc source tree -- `cc -c /usr/src/nyx/tcc/tcc.c ... -o /mnt/tcc_self.o` then `cc /mnt/tcc_self.o /nyxshim.o /libtcc1.o -o /mnt/tcc_self.elf` -- and caches it on the persistent mount; later `--self` calls reuse it. The compiler path (`ccpath`) is resolved once and used for every tcc invocation in cmd_cc; `--self` is consumed (not forwarded to tcc). This is now practical because v6.4.16 lifted the 10-token command cap. **Verified** live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults, the full chain in one boot: a baseline `cc hello_cc.c` (via `/tcc.elf`) runs (exit 42, no regression); the FIRST `cc --self hello_cc.c -o /mnt/h2` prints `building the self-hosted tcc (first use...)`, compiles tcc.c -> links `tcc_self.elf` -> compiles hello_cc.c WITH the self-hosted tcc -> `/mnt/h2` prints `...6*7=42` and exits 42; the SECOND `cc --self ... -o /mnt/h3` prints NO build message (cache reused) and its program also runs, exit 42. The `building` message appears exactly once; all three programs print the identical line and exit 42. So the OS's own `cc` can build a program end-to-end using a compiler that the OS's own compiler built. Production boot clean, reaches login + compositor, 0 faults; `nyx-kernel.bin` 3102.5 KB. **No new syscalls (still 57).** Files: `kernel/core/kernel.c`, `core/kernel.h`. **NEXT: v6.4.18 -- widen (rebuild a real coreutil with `cc --self`, or consider making `--self` the default once fully proven), or keep hardening. Of the three issues opened 2026-08-01, only #40 (FPU/SSE ctx save -- LATENT) remains open.** |
| v6.4.16 | **Hardening (closes GitHub issue #42): the in-kernel command parser no longer silently truncates at 10 arguments -- the cap is raised to 32 (matching the exec/spawn path's own 32-arg limit) and an over-limit command line now reports "too many arguments" instead of dropping tokens.** The parser had `char* argv[10]` + `while (... && argc < 10)` in TWO places in `kernel/core/kernel.c` -- `execute_command` and the interactive-shell path (which also has a parallel `expanded[10][256]` for `$var` expansion) -- so any command past 10 whitespace tokens was silently truncated with no diagnostic, and it bit real toolchain invocations (a `cc`/`tcc` line with several `-I`/`-nostdlib`/`-static` flags plus object paths already exceeds 10). FIX: a shared `#define MAX_CMD_ARGS 32` (`core/kernel.h`, aligned with `process.c`'s `uargv[33]` and `syscall.c`'s `kargv[32]`) drives both sites; each loop now stops at 32 and, if tokens remain, prints an explicit `too many arguments (max 32)` and refuses the command rather than running a truncated one. The interactive path's `expanded[MAX_CMD_ARGS][256]` (8 KB) is made `static` -- the shell parse is serialised -- to keep it off the stack (the same pattern as `syscall.c`'s `kargv_store`). The two sites share the constant and guard shape but are not factored into one tokeniser (noted for a later cleanup). **Verified** live in QEMU (compositor harness), 0 kernel faults: an `echo` with 15 args now prints all 15 (`a1 ... o15`; the old cap of 10 would have dropped everything past the 9th), and a 35-arg command prints `error: too many arguments (max 32)` instead of silently truncating. Production boot clean, reaches login + compositor, 0 faults; `nyx-kernel.bin` 3102 KB. **No new syscalls (still 57).** Files: `kernel/core/kernel.c`, `core/kernel.h`. **NEXT: v6.4.17 -- widen the self-host (rebuild sort.c or a bigger program with `tcc_self.elf`, or bootstrap the `cc` builtin onto `tcc_self.elf`), or keep hardening.** |
| v6.4.15 | **Phase 2 (self-host widening): the SELF-BUILT tcc compiles a REAL coreutil byte-identically. In one boot the in-OS tcc compiles + links `tcc_self.elf` (a tcc built by the in-OS tcc), and THAT compiler compiles the real `grep.c` coreutil into `/mnt/grep3`, whose output is byte-identical to the GCC-built `/grep.elf`.** v6.4.12 proved the self-built compiler compiles a trivial `hello`; this widens the proof to a real program -- grep exercises `strstr` substring matching, file I/O via `open`/`read`, argv handling, and multi-file prefix logic. **Verified** live in QEMU, the full chain from shipped sources in a single boot, 0 kernel faults: (1) `cc -c /usr/src/nyx/tcc/tcc.c -I/usr/src/nyx/tcc -I/usr/src/nyx/tcc/nyxshim -o /mnt/tcc_self.o` (exit 0) -- the in-OS tcc compiles tcc's own ONE_SOURCE; (2) `cc /mnt/tcc_self.o /nyxshim.o /libtcc1.o -o /mnt/tcc_self.elf` (exit 0) -- links a runnable tcc; (3) `/mnt/tcc_self.elf -I/usr/src/nyx -nostdlib -static /crt0.o /libc.o /va_list.o /usr/src/nyx/grep.c -o /mnt/grep3` (exit 0) -- the self-built compiler compiles the coreutil; (4) `/mnt/grep3 foo <fixture>` and the reference `/grep.elf foo <fixture>` both print the identical 21 bytes (`apple foo` / `cherry foo`). The chain moves hundreds of KB through the v6.4.14 page-granular `copy_to_user`/`copy_from_user`. Nothing needed shipping -- `grep.c` has been at `/usr/src/nyx/grep.c` since v6.4.7 -- so this increment is the verified milestone plus the version bump. Production boot clean, reaches login + compositor, 0 faults; `nyx-kernel.bin` 3101.8 KB. **No new syscalls (still 57).** Files: `core/kernel.h`. **NEXT: v6.4.16 -- bootstrap the `cc` builtin onto `tcc_self.elf` (a `--self` path that compiles with the self-built tcc), or widen further (sort.c, or a larger in-OS program).** |
| v6.4.14 | **Hardening: `copy_to_user` / `copy_from_user` are now PAGE-GRANULAR -- they translate the user address once per page and bulk-`memcpy` the run up to the page boundary, instead of walking the page tables for every byte. Closes #41.** The two bulk user-copy paths (`kernel/core/syscall.c`) called `user_v2p_faultin` per byte, doing a full 4-level page-table walk for each byte copied: a 4096-byte `SYS_READ`/`SYS_WRITE` chunk cost 4096 walks, and a ~500 KB object read (the in-OS tcc loading a link input) cost on the order of 500,000. The rewrite translates once per 4 KB page, then `memcpy`s the contiguous in-page run (a user page is one physically-contiguous, identity-mapped frame, so a copy that stops at the page boundary is safe) and only re-translates when crossing into the next page -- O(len) walks becomes O(len / 4096). It PRESERVES the v6.4.11 demand-paging fix: `user_v2p_faultin` still runs once per page, so lazy/demand pages (fresh sbrk / mmap / stack) are materialised via `vm_handle_fault` exactly as before, and a genuinely-unmapped page still returns -1. **Verified** live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults, on a large cross-page workload: the 496,260-byte `tcc_self.o` was staged to the mount, the in-OS `cc` LINK read it all back through `copy_to_user` (many `read()`s, now one translation per page) and linked cleanly (exit 0), and the resulting `/mnt/tcc_self.elf` RAN and printed `tcc version 0.9.27` (exit 0) -- a single mistranslated page would have corrupted the linked binary, so a correct run proves every page copied byte-perfectly; the link also wrote the 306 KB executable out through `copy_from_user`. Production boot clean, reaches login + compositor, 0 faults; `nyx-kernel.bin` 3101.8 KB. **No new syscalls (still 57).** Files: `kernel/core/syscall.c`, `core/kernel.h`. **NEXT: v6.4.15 -- widen the self-host (have the in-OS-built `tcc_self.elf` rebuild a real coreutil byte-identically, or bootstrap the `cc` builtin onto `tcc_self.elf`).** |
| v6.4.13 | **A program persisted on the ext2 `/mnt` disk now runs across reboots -- the in-OS ext2 reader supports SPARSE files (holes).** An ELF written to `/mnt` and then loaded on a later boot failed to exec with `err -5`, while one freshly written during the same boot ran fine. Root-caused with a three-ELF discriminating repro spanning the three block-mapping layouts -- `hs.elf` (8976 B, direct blocks), `tc.elf` (183608 B, single-indirect), `dm.elf` (306020 B, double-indirect): the failing files were **sparse**. `debugfs write` (and ext2 in general) stores a binary's zero-filled regions as HOLES -- a block pointer of 0 -- rather than allocating zeroed blocks (`debugfs stat` on `hs.elf` showed just three blocks, at file-offsets 0/4/8, with 1/2/3/5/6/7 as holes). `ext2_read_inode_block` returned -1 for a 0 pointer, and `ext2_read_file`'s loop breaks on a negative return, so the read TRUNCATED at the file's first hole; the short byte count became the node's size, and `elf_load_image` then rejected the first program segment lying past that truncated size (`offset + filesz > size`) -- returning the `-5`. Files WRITTEN in-OS never triggered this because the ext2 writer allocates every block (dense, no holes), which is exactly why freshly-linked `/mnt` executables ran but externally-staged or reboot-persisted ones did not. **Fix:** `ext2_read_inode_block` now treats a 0 block pointer at every level -- a direct entry, an absent single/double-indirect block (`block[12]`/`block[13]`), an absent L2 table, or a 0 indirect-table entry -- as a sparse hole: it zero-fills the data block and returns success (0) instead of -1. -1 is now reserved for a genuine `ext2_read_block` failure (an out-of-range or corrupt pointer). That is the correct ext2 semantics (a hole reads back as zeros). **Verified** live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults: the three staged ELFs now read their full length (`readret` == `getsize`: 8976 / 183608 / 306020, previously 1024 / 1024 / 275456) and exec correctly -- `hs.elf` exits 42, and both `tc.elf` and `dm.elf` print `tcc version 0.9.27`. No dense-file regression is possible (dense files have no 0 pointers, so the new branches never fire), and all five callers of `ext2_read_inode_block` bound their loops by the inode size and break on a negative return, so hole-as-zero-and-continue is correct for each (directories are never sparse). Production boot clean, reaches login, 0 faults; `e2fsck -fn` clean; `nyx-kernel.bin` 3101.7 KB. **No new syscalls (still 57).** Files: `kernel/fs/ext2.c`, `core/kernel.h`. **NEXT: v6.4.14 -- widen the self-host (rebuild a real coreutil with `tcc_self.elf`, or bootstrap the `cc` builtin onto it), or continue hardening (the per-byte `user_v2p` in `copy_to`/`copy_from_user` is O(n) page-walks -- a page-granular fast path is a clean win).** |
| v6.4.12 | **Phase 2 (tcc-compiles-tcc arc, step 5): ★★★★★ THE SELF-BUILT tcc COMPILES -- the `tcc_self.elf` that the in-OS tcc builds is itself a working compiler: it compiles a NEW C program in-OS into a runnable ELF that runs correctly. A compiler produced by NyxOS's own compiler, in turn, compiles and produces working new code.** **Verified** live in QEMU end to end in a single boot, 0 kernel faults: (1) `cc -c /usr/src/nyx/tcc/tcc.c -I/usr/src/nyx/tcc -I/usr/src/nyx/tcc/nyxshim -o /mnt/tcc_self.o` (exit 0) -- the in-OS tcc compiles tcc's own source; (2) `cc /mnt/tcc_self.o /nyxshim.o /libtcc1.o -o /mnt/tcc_self.elf` (exit 0) -- it links into a runnable tcc; (3) `/mnt/tcc_self.elf -I/usr/src/nyx -nostdlib -static /crt0.o /libc.o /va_list.o /usr/src/nyx/hello_cc.c -o /mnt/hello2` (exit 0) -- **that** compiler compiles a fresh program; (4) `/mnt/hello2` prints `hello from a program built in-OS by the self-built tcc: 6*7=42` and exits `42`. The fixture is a new `user/hello_cc.c` (a `#include "libc.h"` program with a `printf` and `return 6*7`), shipped to `/usr/src/nyx/hello_cc.c` by `tools/mkinitramfs.py` (added to the same list that ships grep.c/sort.c). **No core kernel change was needed:** `tcc_self.elf` locates `<stdarg.h>` (which `libc.h` pulls in) through its built-in `CONFIG_TCCDIR=/usr/lib/tcc` system-include search, so the invocation needs no explicit `-I/usr/lib/tcc/include` and fits within the shell's argument budget. **Follow-up noted (not blocking):** an ELF written to the `/mnt` disk by a *previous* boot and loaded *cold* fails to exec (`err -5` from the ELF loader), while an ELF freshly written during the current boot execs fine -- every earlier `/mnt` exec in this project was also fresh-write-then-run, so cold-loaded mount ELF exec (the `ext2_read_file`-into-`ino->data` path vs the freshly-populated in-memory node) is worth root-causing; this increment sidesteps it by always linking the executable fresh. Shipped kernel boots clean and reaches the login screen, 0 faults; `nyx-kernel.bin` 3101.6 KB. **No new syscalls (still 57).** Files: `user/hello_cc.c` (new), `tools/mkinitramfs.py`, `core/kernel.h`. **NEXT: v6.4.13 -- root-cause the cold `/mnt` ELF exec `-5`, or widen the self-host further (rebuild a real coreutil with `tcc_self.elf`, or bootstrap the `cc` builtin onto it).** |
| v6.4.11 | **Phase 2 (tcc-compiles-tcc arc, step 4): ★★★★ THE SELF-HOST LOOP IS CLOSED -- in a single boot the in-OS tcc COMPILES tcc's own source, LINKS the result, and RUNS it. A tcc built entirely by the in-OS tcc executes inside NyxOS and prints its version, 0 kernel faults.** v6.4.10 got the in-OS tcc to compile `tcc.c` into a 496 KB object; this increment makes that object LINK into a runnable executable and RUN. **Verified** live in QEMU, 0 faults: `cc -c /usr/src/nyx/tcc/tcc.c -I/usr/src/nyx/tcc -I/usr/src/nyx/tcc/nyxshim -o /mnt/tcc_self.o` (exit 0) then `cc /mnt/tcc_self.o /nyxshim.o /libtcc1.o -o /mnt/tcc_self.elf` (exit 0, a clean link with zero undefined symbols) then `/mnt/tcc_self.elf -v` prints `tcc version 0.9.27` (exit 0). The on-disk `tcc_self.elf` is a valid 306 KB `ELF x86-64` executable (entry `0x4000b0`). Two pieces made it work. **(1) Link inputs.** `tcc.c` is ONE_SOURCE, so `tcc_self.o` is the whole compiler (including `main`); beyond the standard `cc` runtime (`/crt0.o` + `/libc.o` + `/va_list.o`) it needs tcc's OS adapter `nyxshim.o` (the POSIX glue: `read`/`write`/`open`/`exit`/`strtod`/`sscanf`/`__assert_fail`/...) and `libtcc1.o` (the long-double float<->integer helpers tcc's own codegen emits, `__floatundixf`/`__fixunsxfdi`). Both are now built as NyxOS-target objects and shipped in the initramfs at `/nyxshim.o` and `/libtcc1.o` (a new `libtcc1.o` Makefile rule, both added to the `initramfs_data.h` prerequisites, and `tools/mkinitramfs.py` ships them explicitly since they live in the `user/tcc/` subtree the loader scan does not walk). The static link's only symbol collision was `abort`, defined by BOTH `nyxshim.o` and `libc.o`; it was dropped from `nyxshim.c` (libc provides it, and the host `tcc.elf` build resolves `abort` from `libc.so` via `--just-symbols`, so both link models still build). **(2) A real kernel bug, root-caused and fixed.** The in-OS link first failed with `undefined symbol 'main'` followed by a flood of undefined symbols with EMPTY names -- even though the same object links cleanly on the host. It was NOT a read truncation: instrumenting the mount-file load proved the 496,260-byte object arrives in the kernel BYTE-PERFECT (a checksum matched the host object exactly). The cause was `copy_to_user` (and `copy_from_user`) in `core/syscall.c`: they translated each user byte through `user_v2p` and returned failure if the page was not mapped, without materialising a lazy/demand page. tcc `read()`s each object section into a freshly `malloc`'d (lazy-sbrk) buffer whose pages are reserved but untouched, so the copy hit the first unmapped page, returned a short read, and left the tail sections -- including the symbol string table -- zeroed, which is exactly why every symbol name came back empty. Small objects (wc/grep/sort) linked because their buffers fit in already-touched heap; the 496 KB object ran off into fresh lazy territory. The fix is a `user_v2p_faultin()` helper: when `user_v2p` returns 0 it calls `vm_handle_fault(va, 0x6)` -- the same demand-paging path the CPU's page-fault handler uses (fresh sbrk / mmap / stack) -- then retries, and it is used by both `copy_to_user` and `copy_from_user`. This is the DOOM-era "copy_to_user can't fault lazy sbrk" limitation (whose old workaround was to memset a buffer before reading into it), now fixed at the source -- a general correctness win for any large `read()` into fresh memory. Shipped kernel boots clean and reaches the login screen, 0 faults; `nyx-kernel.bin` 3100.7 KB. **No new syscalls (still 57).** Files: `kernel/core/syscall.c`, `kernel/Makefile`, `tools/mkinitramfs.py`, `user/tcc/nyxshim/nyxshim.c`, `core/kernel.h`. **NEXT: v6.4.12 -- run the in-OS-built `tcc_self.elf` to actually COMPILE a small program in-OS, proving the self-built compiler compiles (not just `-v`).** |
| v6.4.10 | **Phase 2 (tcc-compiles-tcc arc, step 3): ★★★ the in-OS tcc self-host object now PERSISTS IN FULL -- `cc -c /usr/src/nyx/tcc/tcc.c` writes a complete, valid, `e2fsck`-clean 496,412-byte `ELF x86-64 REL` (548 FUNC symbols) to the ext2 mount, 0 kernel faults.** v6.4.9 got the compile to `exit 0`, but the object truncated at 274,432 bytes on write. **The v6.4.9 root-cause ("heap fragmentation in `vfs_pwrite`") was WRONG** -- a second misdiagnosis in this arc. The real cap: the NyxOS ext2 WRITER only mapped 12 direct + single-indirect blocks, and the shipped image uses 1024-byte blocks, so it capped every file at `12 + 1024/4 = 268` blocks = exactly 274,432 bytes; `ext2_write_file` rejected anything larger and `flush_mount_node` ignored the failure. Fixed with THREE coordinated changes across the filesystem and allocator, all verified by `e2fsck`: **(1) ext2 double-indirect write** (`kernel/fs/ext2.c`) -- the write loop grew a `block[13]` path mirroring the read path (using scratch buffers 2 and 3 for the L1 table and L2 indirect block), the up-front size check was raised to `12 + ppb + ppb*ppb` blocks, and `i_blocks` now charges the double-indirect metadata; **(2) write-back / flush-on-close** (`kernel/fs/vfs.c`) -- the old code re-flushed the WHOLE file to ext2 on every 4 KB `write()`, an O(n^2) I/O cost that made the 496 KB write take over nine minutes; `vfs_pwrite` now sets a `dirty` flag and `vfs_close` flushes once at the last close (orphaned/unlinked files are skipped; the editor's whole-file `vfs_write` path still flushes immediately); **(3) geometric buffer growth** (`kernel/fs/vfs.c` + a new `ksize()` in `kernel/mm/memory.c`) -- `vfs_pwrite` used to `kmalloc`-realloc the entire in-memory buffer on every extending write (O(n) reallocs); it now reallocs only when the write passes the buffer's real capacity (read from the `kmalloc` header via `ksize()`), doubling each time (O(log n)). **Verified** live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults: after `cc -c /usr/src/nyx/tcc/tcc.c ...` the on-disk `/mnt/tcc_self.o` is **496,412 bytes**, a valid `ELF x86-64 REL` with 548 FUNC symbols; a small-file regression (`cc /mnt/rt.c` then run) still prints `rt: ok 42`; and `e2fsck -fn` reports the image CLEAN both before AND after deleting the file (the double-indirect blocks are freed correctly). Shipped kernel boots clean, 117 initramfs files, 0 faults; `nyx-kernel.bin` 3091.3 KB. **BEHAVIOUR CHANGE (documented):** files on the ext2 mount are now WRITE-BACK -- they persist to disk on close rather than on each write, so a crash between a write and its close loses the unflushed tail (POSIX-ish, acceptable for this OS). **No new syscalls (still 57).** Files: `kernel/fs/ext2.c`, `kernel/fs/vfs.c`, `kernel/mm/memory.c`, `core/kernel.h`. **NEXT (v6.4.11): link `tcc_self.o` (ONE_SOURCE, so it is the whole compiler) with `crt0.o` + `libc.o` + `va_list.o` + `nyxshim.o` into a runnable `tcc_self.elf`, then run `tcc_self.elf -v` in-OS -- a tcc built by the in-OS tcc, closing the self-host loop.** |
| v6.4.9 | **Phase 2 (tcc-compiles-tcc arc, step 2): ★★ TCC COMPILES TCC INSIDE NyxOS -- the in-OS tcc compiles tcc's OWN whole source (`tcc.c`, ONE_SOURCE, ~40,000 lines) to a relocatable object, exit 0, 0 kernel faults.** `tools/mkinitramfs.py` ships tcc's own source tree onto the NyxOS filesystem at `/usr/src/nyx/tcc/` -- the 18 top-level `.c`/`.h` files tcc.c pulls in the x86_64/ELF ONE_SOURCE build (`libtcc.c`, `tccpp.c`, `tccgen.c`, `tccelf.c`, `tccasm.c`, `tccrun.c`, `x86_64-gen.c`, `x86_64-link.c`, `i386-asm.c`, `tcc.h`, `elf.h`, `stab.h`, `stab.def`, `tcctok.h`, ...) plus the `nyxshim/` shims; `<stdarg.h>` etc. resolve from the already-shipped `/usr/lib/tcc/include` and `libc.h` from `/usr/src/nyx`. **Verified** live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults, 117 initramfs files: `cc -c /usr/src/nyx/tcc/tcc.c -I/usr/src/nyx/tcc -I/usr/src/nyx/tcc/nyxshim -o /mnt/tcc_self.o` runs to **exit 0** -- the entire compile pipeline (lexer, preprocessor, parser, x86_64 code generator, ELF writer) runs on 40K lines of the compiler's own source, in-OS, with no out-of-memory and no hang; the output's ELF header is a valid `x86-64 REL`. So the operating system's own compiler now compiles the compiler itself. **KNOWN LIMITATION (next increment):** the ~496 KB object truncates when written to the ext2 mount at 274432 bytes -- root-caused to `vfs_pwrite` (`kernel/fs/vfs.c`) reallocating the whole in-memory buffer AND re-flushing the whole file to disk on every 4 KB `write()`, an O(n^2) churn whose growing `kmalloc` fails on heap fragmentation around 274 KB (the ext2 writer itself allows up to 4 MB). v6.4.10 will make large mount writes grow geometrically + flush on close so the full object persists, then link a runnable `tcc_self.elf` (tcc built by tcc). Shipped kernel boots clean, 117 initramfs files, 0 faults; `nyx-kernel.bin` 3090.7 KB (+~950 KB for the shipped tcc sources). **No new syscalls (still 57).** Files: `tools/mkinitramfs.py`, `core/kernel.h`. **NEXT: v6.4.10 -- fix the large mount-file write, persist the full object, and link tcc_self.elf.** |
| v6.4.8 | **Phase 2 (tcc-compiles-tcc arc, step 1): the tcc port is now HOST-HEADER-FREE -- `tcc.c` compiles with `-nostdinc` (496 KB object), which unblocks the in-OS self-host build.** v6.4.7 proved our tcc can compile `tcc.c`, but the compile leaked host headers; this makes the port self-contained. METHOD: `tcc -nostdinc` disables the host `/usr/include` search (mimicking in-OS, where none exists) and so enumerates every host-header dependency; iterate, adding one `user/tcc/nyxshim/` shim per gap. Added 4 shims -- **stdint.h** (defines `int8_t..uint64_t` DIRECTLY, guarded by `__int8_t_defined` exactly like tcc's own `stddef.h`, plus `intptr_t`/`uintptr_t` via the `__PTRDIFF_TYPE__`/`__SIZE_TYPE__` builtins; it must NOT `#include <stddef.h>`, because the tcc.elf GCC build's `-I` set lacks `user/tcc/include`, so there `<stddef.h>` is the host's minimal one with no fixed-width types -- that mistake first broke the GCC build), **inttypes.h** (`PRIx64` etc., LP64 `"l"`), **assert.h** (routes to nyxshim's `__assert_fail`), **sys/mman.h** (`PROT_*`/`MAP_*` + `mmap`/`mprotect` decls) -- and **disabled tcc's dead -run/JIT path**: `tcc.h` auto-`#define`s `TCC_IS_NATIVE` when host==target (x86_64), which drags in `tccrun.c`'s `<signal.h>`/`<sys/ucontext.h>`; a new `#define TCC_NO_NATIVE 1` in `nyxshim/config.h` + a `#if !defined(TCC_NO_NATIVE)` guard on that block turns it off (the in-OS tcc never JITs; this also matches config.h's already-documented "TCC_IS_NATIVE undefined" intent). **Verified** on host BOTH ways (`tcc -nostdinc` -> 496 KB object, the real `gcc` tcc.elf build -> 285 KB object), and in-OS via the compositor harness, 0 kernel faults: after the changes the in-OS tcc still compiles+links+RUNS a program -- `cc /mnt/rt.c -o /mnt/rt` then running it prints `rt: ok 42` and `exit(42)`. Shipped kernel boots clean, 82 initramfs files, 0 faults; `nyx-kernel.bin` 2141.3 KB (a touch smaller -- `TCC_NO_NATIVE` drops tcc.elf's -run/backtrace code). **No new syscalls (still 57).** Files: `user/tcc/nyxshim/{stdint,inttypes,assert}.h` + `sys/mman.h` (new), `user/tcc/nyxshim/config.h`, `user/tcc/tcc.h`, `core/kernel.h`. **NEXT (v6.4.9): ship the tcc source tree onto the NyxOS FS + in-OS `cc -c tcc.c` -> a valid object (the self-host object milestone).** |
| v6.4.7 | **Phase 2: the in-OS tcc rebuilds two more real coreutils (grep + sort) byte-identically -- and a host scout confirms our tcc can compile tcc.c itself, so true self-host is feasible.** `tools/mkinitramfs.py` now ships `user/grep.c` + `user/sort.c` into the initramfs at `/usr/src/nyx/` (beside sh.c), so `cc /usr/src/nyx/grep.c -o /mnt/grep2` rebuilds them in-OS from source. **Verified** live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults: `grep` (substring match via `strstr`, multi-file `file:` prefixing) and `sort` (a static-`.bss` line buffer + insertion sort over `strcmp`), each built by the in-OS tcc, produced **byte-identical** output to the shipped GCC-built `/grep.elf` / `/sort.elf` -- `grep foo /mnt/gstest.txt` both printed `apple foo` + `date foo`, and `sort /mnt/gstest.txt` both printed the five lines in `strcmp` order (`apple bar`, `apple foo`, `banana`, `cherry`, `date foo`). So the in-OS toolchain rebuilds more of the OS's real tools, output-for-output the same as the reference compiler. **Scout milestone (host):** our vendored tcc COMPILES `tcc.c` itself -- the whole compiler via ONE_SOURCE -- to a 516 KB object (`tcc -c -DNYX_LIBC_NO_SYSCALL -I user/tcc/include ... user/tcc/tcc.c`), proving tcc's language + codegen coverage is sufficient for the TRUE self-host milestone (tcc compiling tcc); the remaining in-OS groundwork (ship the tcc source tree + resolve `<sys/mman.h>` and any other host-header includes via nyxshim) is mapped for a future dedicated arc. Shipped kernel boots clean, **82 initramfs files** (adds grep.c + sort.c), 0 faults; `nyx-kernel.bin` 2150.3 KB. **No new syscalls (still 57).** Files: `tools/mkinitramfs.py`, `core/kernel.h`. **NEXT: start the tcc-compiles-tcc arc (ship tcc sources + in-OS `tcc -c tcc.c`), or keep widening coverage.** |
| v6.4.6 | **Phase 2: floating-point now works in the in-OS tcc -- `strtod` was a stub, so every float/double literal compiled to ZERO; a real `strtod` fixes it.** tcc's lexer (`tccpp.c` parse_number) turns every floating constant into a value by calling `strtof`/`strtod`/`strtold`, and `user/tcc/nyxshim/nyxshim.c` had those as M0 stubs that `return 0.0` -- so the in-OS tcc compiled EVERY float/double constant to zero, both in user code and inside `libtcc1.c` (its `18446744073709551616.0` = the 2^64 unsigned<->float bias). That -- **not** the v6.4.5 "no SSE/FPU context save" guess (a misdiagnosis, reverted) -- is why in-OS float programs computed wrong. FIX: implemented a real `strtod` (+ `strtof`/`strtold`/`ldexp`) in nyxshim.c -- self-contained, parsing decimal (19-significant-digit-exact u64 mantissa + base-10 scaling, so big powers of ten like 2^64 stay accurate) AND hexadecimal floats (`0x1p4`); host-tested byte-exact against glibc on 3.0/2.0/1.0/2^64/1.5e10/0x1p4/negatives/fractions. **Verified** live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults, STABLE across 3 preemption-heavy runs: `cc /mnt/flt2.c -o /mnt/flt2` (native double constants: `3.0*2.0+1.0`) runs to `flt2: t1=7 t2=1`, and `cc --self-libc /mnt/flt.c -o /mnt/flt` (unsigned-64<->float round-trips through the tcc-built libtcc1 helpers) runs to `selfrt2: dok=1 fok=1 hi=12` + `exit(2)`. So the in-OS tcc now handles floating-point constants correctly and the tcc-built float runtime executes correctly. Diagnosis gold: extracting the in-OS binary via debugfs + `readelf -x .data` showed the double constants were literally `0x00` in the file while the host build had them -- the discriminating variable was float-literal-through-tcc-static-link, not NyxOS-vs-host. Shipped kernel boots clean, 80 initramfs files, 0 faults; `nyx-kernel.bin` 2146.3 KB. **No new syscalls (still 57).** Files: `user/tcc/nyxshim/nyxshim.c`, `core/kernel.h`. **NEXT: keep widening the self-host reach (more real programs / another tcc source), toward tcc compiling tcc.** |
| v6.4.5 | **Phase 2: the in-OS tcc now compiles tcc's MAIN runtime library (`libtcc1.c`) -- `cc --self-libc` self-compiles the WHOLE tcc runtime -- and this surfaced a real latent kernel bug, root-caused, whose fix is queued as v6.4.6.** On top of v6.4.2 (which self-compiled tcc's `va_list.c`), `tools/mkinitramfs.py` now also ships `user/tcc/lib/libtcc1.c` into the initramfs at `/usr/src/nyx/libtcc1.c`, and `cmd_cc`'s `--self-libc` loop tcc-compiles all THREE runtime sources `{libc.c, va_list.c, libtcc1.c}` (→ `<out>.slo`/`.vlo`/`.tlo`) and links the program against all three, so the only part of a program's runtime still built by the host GCC is the tiny `crt0.o`. `libtcc1.c` is header-free; on x86_64 it provides the float↔64-bit-int conversion helpers `__floatundi{sf,df,xf}` / `__fixuns{sf,df,xf}di` / `__fix{sf,df,xf}di` that tcc's OWN codegen emits for unsigned-64↔float conversions. **Verified** live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults: `cc --self-libc /mnt/flt.c -o /mnt/flt` tcc-compiled `libc.c` + `va_list.c` + `libtcc1.c` (each `exit 0`) and linked -- the extracted `/mnt/flt.tlo` is a valid `ELF REL x86-64` with 9 GLOBAL FUNC helper symbols (matching host tcc's output), and `/mnt/flt` is a static `EXEC` (entry 0x4000b0) = the whole tcc-built runtime linked. So the in-OS tcc now compiles tcc's complete runtime library. **However, running the float program in NyxOS returns a WRONG result** (`dok=0`; the identical program is correct on host) -- root-caused to a **real latent kernel bug: NyxOS never saves/restores SSE/FPU (XMM) state across context switches** (there is no `fxsave`/`fxrstor` anywhere), so userspace floating-point is corrupted by preemption. Isolation proved the tcc-compiled `libtcc1` helpers and tcc's conversion codegen are both CORRECT on host -- only NyxOS's FP path fails (the native integer `big>>60` was right). So v6.4.5's milestone is the tcc COMPILE + LINK of the runtime (proven), not the float RUN. Shipped kernel boots clean, **80 initramfs files** (adds `libtcc1.c`), 0 faults; `nyx-kernel.bin` 2146.3 KB. **No new syscalls (still 57).** Files: `tools/mkinitramfs.py`, `core/kernel.c`, `core/kernel.h`. **NEXT (v6.4.6): FIX the FPU/SSE context bug** -- a per-process 16-byte-aligned `fxsave` area + `fxsave`/`fxrstor` at the context-switch chokepoint -- then the tcc-built float runtime actually runs correctly. |
| v6.4.4 | **Phase 2: the in-OS tcc now compiles the OS's OWN SHELL -- the operating system's own compiler builds its 1042-line userspace shell, and the tcc-built shell RUNS a real command line byte-identical to the shipped GCC build.** `user/sh.c` is the biggest, most complex real program in the OS (fork/execve/pipe/dup2/waitpid, `;`/`&&`/`||` lists, quoting, `$(...)` command substitution, jobs, tab-completion). Now `tools/mkinitramfs.py` ships it into the initramfs at `/usr/src/nyx/sh.c` (beside the libc/va sources), so `cc /usr/src/nyx/sh.c -o /mnt/sh2` rebuilds the shell IN-OS from its shipped source (its `#include "libc.h"` resolves against that same dir; no source edits). Host-repro first: `tcc -c` compiles sh.c to a 45 KB object, and the full NyxOS-style static link (`crt0.o + libc.o + va_list.o`) yields a static `EXEC` with **0 undefined symbols**. **Verified** live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults: the in-OS `cc /usr/src/nyx/sh.c -o /mnt/sh2` builds the shell (exit 0), and a ladder of command lines run through BOTH `/mnt/sh2` (tcc-built) and the shipped GCC `/sh.elf` produced **byte-identical** output at every rung -- `pwd` (pure builtin) both `/mnt/home/nyx`; `echo hello world` (fork/execve/waitpid a child `/echo.elf`) both `hello world`; `echo hello world \| wc` (pipe + dup2 + two forked children) both `1 2 12`. So the OS's own compiler rebuilds the OS's own shell, and the rebuilt shell drives the full ring-3 process toolkit exactly like the reference build. Shipped kernel boots clean, **79 initramfs files** (adds sh.c), 0 faults. **No new syscalls (still 57).** Files: `tools/mkinitramfs.py`, `core/kernel.h`. **NEXT: keep widening -- more real OS programs / another tcc source, ultimately tcc compiling tcc end to end (true self-host).** |
| v6.4.3 | **Phase 2: the in-OS tcc now compiles REAL, unmodified NyxOS programs -- a coreutil built by the OS's own compiler produces byte-identical output to the shipped GCC build.** Real NyxOS sources `#include "libc.h"` (which in turn includes `"syscall.h"`), and those headers ship in the initramfs at `/usr/src/nyx/` (v6.4.0). Adding `-I/usr/src/nyx` to the `cc` builtin's default include path makes that quote-include resolve, so an actual OS source compiles as-is -- no hand-declared prototypes, no edits. Proven live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults: `cc /mnt/wc.c -o /mnt/wc2` compiles the real, unmodified `user/wc.c` (the `wc` coreutil: `#include "libc.h"`, `read`/`open`/`close`/`printf`, `argc`/`argv`, a `long` counting state machine) with the in-OS tcc, and running the result on a test file prints `3 6 29 /mnt/wctest.txt` -- **byte-identical** to the shipped GCC-built `/wc.elf` on the same file (both emit the same line, both `exit(0)`). So the in-OS toolchain rebuilds the operating system's own real tools, output-for-output the same as the reference compiler. `libc.h` was already tcc-friendly (v6.4.0's `#ifdef __TINYC__` va_list guard), so no source changes were needed beyond the include path. Host-repro first: tcc compiles `user/wc.c` (headers via `-I`) to a valid REL object and links it (crt0 + libc + va_list) to a static ELF with 0 undefined symbols. Build 0 warnings; `nyx-kernel.bin` 2087.6 KB; shipped kernel boots clean, 78 initramfs files, 0 faults. **No new syscalls (still 57); no new files.** Files: `core/kernel.c`, `core/kernel.h`. **NEXT: keep widening -- more/bigger real OS programs, or another tcc source, ultimately tcc compiling tcc end to end.** |
| v6.4.2 | **Phase 2: a self-host slice -- the in-OS tcc now compiles a piece of tcc ITSELF (its own va-runtime), so `cc --self-libc` builds a program whose ENTIRE C runtime is tcc-produced.** On top of v6.4.1 (which self-compiled the OS's libc), `--self-libc` now ALSO compiles tcc's own `lib/va_list.c` (the libtcc1 helper that provides `__va_start`/`__va_arg`, which tcc's `<stdarg.h>` lowers `va_start`/`va_arg` to) with the in-OS tcc, and links the program against THAT object instead of the prebuilt (GCC-built) `/va_list.o`. | v6.4.1 | **Phase 2: PROVED the tcc-built libc actually RUNS (not just compiles) -- and fixed the two real bugs that stood in the way.** New `cc --self-libc` shell builtin compiles `/usr/src/nyx/libc.c` with the in-OS tcc and links the program against THAT object instead of the prebuilt (GCC-built) `/libc.o`, so the program's `printf`/`snprintf`/`malloc`/`strlen` are all served by code the OS's own compiler produced. Getting a clean run surfaced two genuine defects: **(1) SYS_READ capped every read at 4096 bytes** (`core/syscall.c`) while tcc's object loader (`tccelf.c` `load_data` + the SHF_ALLOC section read) does a single unchecked `read()` -- so reading the ~17 KB self-libc object back to link truncated at 4 KB and zero-filled the rest of `.text`, so a call fell into zero-padding and faulted. Fixed: a regular-file `read()` now returns the FULL requested count by looping `vfs_pread` in page-size chunks through a bounce buffer (no large kernel allocation); the streaming fds (stdin/pipe/socket) stay capped. General fix -- any program that `read()`s a >4 KB file in one call now works, and the vendored tcc stays upstream-faithful. **(2) tcc never filled a static executable's GOT** (`user/tcc/tccelf.c`): stock tcc 0.9.27 calls `fill_got()` AFTER `tidy_section_headers()` has dropped the `SHT_RELX` sections, so `fill_got` iterates nothing and every static GOT slot stays 0 -- a `-nostdlib -static` libc that reaches its own globals through `R_X86_64_GOTPCREL` (`heap_base`, `environ`) then dereferences a NULL GOT entry and faults. Fixed by filling the static GOT BEFORE tidy (symbol addresses are already final there, after `final_sections_reloc` -> `relocate_syms`) -- the data-reference analogue of the v6.3.0 PLT32->PC32 call fix. Also: `run_foreground_elf` now returns the child's exit code, so `cc --self-libc` aborts if the libc self-compile fails. **Verified** live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults: `cc --self-libc /mnt/selfprog.c -o /mnt/selfprog` compiles libc.c with tcc, links the program against the result, and running it prints `selflibc: [self-hosted len=11] n=20 cmp=0 ok=1` and `exit(11)` -- `printf`/`snprintf`/`malloc`/`free`/`strcpy`/`strlen`/`strcmp` all executing from tcc-compiled libc code. The shipped kernel (temp harness reverted) boots clean, 77 initramfs files, 0 faults; a normal `cc` link vs the GCC `/libc.o` still produces a valid static ELF (no regression). Build 0 warnings; `nyx-kernel.bin` 2085.5 KB. **No new syscalls (still 57); no new files.** Files: `core/syscall.c`, `core/kernel.c`, `user/tcc/tccelf.c`, `core/kernel.h`. **NEXT: widen toward tcc compiling more of the OS, ultimately tcc compiling tcc (true self-host).** |
| v6.4.0 | **Phase 2 -- NEW MINOR: SELF-HOSTING milestone (M4) -- TinyCC, running INSIDE NyxOS, compiles the NyxOS libc.** The in-OS `cc -c /usr/src/nyx/libc.c -o /mnt/libc_self.o` compiles the OS's OWN 727-line `user/libc.c` to a valid relocatable object, in-OS, exit 0, 0 kernel faults -- a real piece of the operating system built by the operating system's own compiler. Reaching it needed three source tweaks that make the libc tcc-compilable while keeping the GCC build byte-for-byte identical (each guarded or assemble-identical): **(1)** `user/libc.h` -- `va_list` was `typedef __builtin_va_list` (a GCC builtin tcc lacks); now `#ifdef __TINYC__ #include <stdarg.h> #else <the __builtin_va_list defs> #endif`, so tcc reads its own `<stdarg.h>` from the v6.3.4 `/usr/lib/tcc/include`. **(2)** `user/libc.c` -- `longjmp`'s naked asm used `jmpq *56(%rdi)`; tcc's assembler has no `jmpq` opcode, so it is now plain `jmp *56(%rdi)` (identical machine code under GCC). **(3)** `user/syscall.h` -- `syscall6` used `register long r10 asm("r10")` bindings + `"r"` constraints tcc can't satisfy; a `#ifdef __TINYC__` path instead passes a4/a5/a6 as memory operands and `movq`s them into r10/r8/r9 inside the asm (the GCC path is untouched, so every syscall in the OS stays byte-identical). `tools/mkinitramfs.py` now ships `libc.c`/`libc.h`/`syscall.h` into the initramfs under `/usr/src/nyx/` (one dir, so the quote-includes resolve) via the v6.3.4 mkdir-p loader. **Verified** live in QEMU: the in-OS tcc emits `/mnt/libc_self.o` = `ELF 64-bit LSB relocatable, x86-64` with 88 GLOBAL FUNC symbols (memset, malloc, printf, snprintf, fopen, ...), matching the host tcc's output; and the shipped kernel (temp hooks reverted, `syscall6` exercised throughout boot) boots clean to the desktop, 77 initramfs files, 0 faults -- confirming the guarded syscall change is transparent. So the NyxOS toolchain is now self-hosting-capable: tcc compiles the C the OS is written in. Build 0 warnings; `nyx-kernel.bin` 2084.7 KB. **No new syscalls (still 57); no new files.** Files: `user/libc.h`, `user/libc.c`, `user/syscall.h`, `tools/mkinitramfs.py`, `core/kernel.h`. **NEXT: link the tcc-built `libc_self.o` into a running program (prove it WORKS, not just compiles), then widen toward tcc compiling more of the OS / itself.** |

With no `-o`, the output name is `<src>` minus a trailing `.c`. **Verified** end-to-end by driving the REAL `cmd_cc` from the harness: `cc /mnt/hello3.c -o /mnt/hello3` compiles (tcc `exit(0)`, reaped by the foreground wait) and the built program runs to `cc builtin works: sq(9)=81` + `exit(3)`, 0 faults; the shipped kernel (temp hooks reverted) boots clean to login, 0 faults. | v6.3.0 | **Phase 2 -- NEW MINOR: a program COMPILED IN-OS by tcc now RUNS in-OS -- compile -> link -> run is CLOSED (M3 complete).** `int add(int a,int b){return a+b;} int main(void){return add(2,3);}` written to the ext2 mount, built with `tcc -nostdlib -static /crt0.o /libc.o /mnt/hello.c -o /mnt/hello` (tcc.elf + crt0.o + libc.o all from the initramfs), spawned from `/mnt`, RUNS and `exit(5)` -- **0 kernel faults**. Two things had to be understood. **(1) the ELF base.** tcc 0.9.27 rounds the first PT_LOAD's `p_vaddr` DOWN to `p_align` (2 MB) in `layout_sections` (tccelf.c), so the `-Wl,-Ttext=0x10000` used at v6.2.3 forced base 0 (`0x10000 & ~0x1FFFFF`), which NyxOS `elf_load` rejects (a PT_LOAD must sit at >= `USER_SPACE_MIN` 0x1000). DROPPING `-Ttext` lets tcc use its DEFAULT `ELF_START_ADDR` (0x400000), so the whole image bases at 0x400000 (entry 0x4000b0) and loads unchanged -- no kernel loader change. And `spawn_user_path` from `/mnt` was NEVER the blocker the v6.2.3 note feared: `vfs_open` already pre-loads a mount file's bytes into the node (via `me->read_file`), so `vfs_fdata` returns them; the -2 hypothesis was wrong. **(2) a real tcc static-link bug -- the actual fix.** tcc's x86_64 backend routes a call to a GLOBAL default-visibility function through a PLT, and for a `-nostdlib -static` link it CREATES the PLT but never fills the PLT's GOT slot (`put_got_entry` sets `plt_offset`/`plt_sym` but leaves `attr->got_offset` 0, so `fill_got_entry` early-returns on the 0 offset) -- leaving the GOT all zeros, so every such call jumped through the GOT to NULL (observed: `[fault] RIP 0x0 err 0x14`, even the local `add`). Fix = the FIRST edit to tcc's own sources: in `build_got_entries` (tccelf.c) extend the existing "downgrade R_X86_64_PLT32 to a direct PC32" rule (which already fires for LOCAL/hidden symbols) to ALSO fire for a symbol already DEFINED in a static executable (`output_type == TCC_OUTPUT_EXE && static_link && st_shndx != SHN_UNDEF && st_shndx < SHN_LORESERVE`) -- exactly what a real static linker does: a defined function has a final address, so call it directly. Result: the exe has NO `.plt`/`.got` at all, every call is a direct PC32, and it runs. Root-caused with a host build of the same vendored tcc (the empty GOT reproduces on the host too, proving it is a tcc-logic issue, not an in-OS one; the one-line patch fixes both). **Verified** by extracting `/mnt/hello` (`readelf`: static `EXEC`, entry 0x4000b0, no `.plt`/`.got`) and running it in QEMU to `exit(5)`, 0 faults. Build 0 warnings; `nyx-kernel.bin` 2013.8 KB. **No new syscalls (still 57); no new files** (edited `user/tcc/tccelf.c`, `core/kernel.h`). **NEXT (v6.3.1): an in-OS `cc` wrapper so a user compiles+runs C in one command, then widen C coverage toward self-hosting (M4).** |
| v6.2.3 | **Phase 2 (tcc M3, part 1): tcc LINKS a static executable ELF in-OS.** `tcc -nostdlib -static -Wl,-Ttext=0x10000 /crt0.o /libc.o hello.c -o /mnt/hello` runs in-OS (exit 0, 0 kernel faults) and writes a real static executable -- exercising tcc's ELF linker (`tccelf.c`) across our objects. To feed it, `tools/mkinitramfs.py` now also ships `crt0.o` (848 B, provides `_start`/`environ`) and `libc.o` (19 KB, statically linked so the exe is self-contained) into the initramfs alongside the `.elf`/`.so` it already embedded. **Verified** by extracting the output from the ext2 image (host `debugfs dump` + `readelf`): `/mnt/hello` is `ELF 64-bit LSB executable, x86-64, statically linked` -- Type `EXEC`, entry `0x10000` (so `-static` dropped the `PT_INTERP` that a default tcc exe carries, and `-Wl,-Ttext=0x10000` placed the entry where NyxOS expects it). The compiler now goes preprocess -> parse -> x86_64 codegen -> ELF object -> LINK to a static executable, all in-OS. **RUN is deferred to M3 part 2 (v6.2.4):** the tcc exe's first `PT_LOAD` is based at vaddr 0 (tcc puts the ELF header at 0 and only relocates `.text`), and `spawn_user_path` loads a binary via `vfs_fdata` (in-memory ramdisk/initramfs only, NOT the ext2 `/mnt` mount) -- so running a disk-built binary needs a load path that reads via `vfs_read` (or routes tcc's output to the ramdisk) plus handling the vaddr-0 layout. Build 0 warnings; `nyx-kernel.bin` 2013.8 KB (crt0.o + libc.o added to the image). **No new syscalls (still 57); no new files.** Files: `tools/mkinitramfs.py`, `core/kernel.h`. **NEXT (v6.2.4): make the tcc-built executable RUN in-OS -- closing compile -> link -> run.** |

So the compiler front-to-back (preprocess -> parse -> x86_64 codegen -> ELF object) works inside NyxOS. This increment is the verified milestone + version bump (no functional source change was required beyond it). Build 0 warnings; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1994 KB. **No new syscalls (still 57); no new files.** Files: `core/kernel.h`. **NEXT (M3): `tcc hello.c -o hello` -- link a full executable + run it in-OS (needs a crt/entry and our libc as the target runtime).** |
| v6.2.1 | **Phase 2 (tcc M1): `tcc -E` (preprocess) runs in-OS -- unblocked by fixing a real `strchr` bug in the libc.** `user/libc.c`'s `strchr` looped `while (*s) { ... } return 0;`, so it returned NULL at the terminator and NEVER matched `c == '\0'` -- but C requires `strchr(s, 0)` to return a pointer to the terminating NUL (the NUL is part of the string). TinyCC's `tcc_basename()` does exactly `strchr(name, 0)` to find end-of-string; with the buggy strchr it got NULL, returned NULL, and `tcc_fileextension()` then called `strrchr(NULL, '.')` -> a NULL-deref page fault (#14 at fault-addr 0x0) that killed `tcc -E` immediately. Fixed strchr to `for (;; s++) { if (*s == ch) return s; if (!*s) return 0; }`, which matches the NUL when `c == 0` and is correct for every caller, not just tcc (this was a latent libc defect for any `strchr(s, 0)` end-of-string idiom). **Verified** live in QEMU (compositor-spawn harness: write a `/hello.c` containing `#define GREETING 42` + `int main(void){ return GREETING; }`, then `spawn_user_path_args("/tcc.elf", {"tcc","-E","/hello.c"}, 3)`): tcc emits `# 1 "/hello.c"` and the macro-EXPANDED `int main(void) { return 42; }`, then `exit(0)` with 0 kernel faults -- so the preprocessor (lexer + macro expansion + `# line` markers + file open/read over the nyxshim OS adapter + output to stdout) works in-OS. The `/hello.c` lives on the ephemeral ramdisk, so nothing to clean. The port is a thin shim layer, NOT a fork of tcc: (1) `user/tcc/nyxshim/` supplies the standard headers tcc's sources `#include` (`<stdio.h>`/`<stdlib.h>`/`<string.h>`/`<ctype.h>`/`<errno.h>`/`<setjmp.h>`/`<fcntl.h>`/`<unistd.h>`/`<time.h>`/`<sys/time.h>`/`<math.h>`), most just re-exporting `libc.h`; plus a `config.h` replacing tinycc's `./configure` output (TCC_TARGET_X86_64/ELF, CONFIG_TCC_STATIC, no -run/JIT). (3) `user/libc.h` gains a backward-compatible `#ifndef NYX_LIBC_NO_SYSCALL` guard around its `#include "syscall.h"`, so the tcc shims pull in the C-library declarations without the clashing syscall wrappers; every other program is byte-for-byte unaffected (the macro is only defined for the tcc build). | v6.1.5 | **Phase 2: the standard streams + `fgets`/`fputs`/`fseek`/`ftell` -- the last stdio brick, so the libc is now compiler-ready.** `user/libc.c` gains the three predefined streams `stdin`/`stdout`/`stderr` as static `FILE` objects wrapping fds 0/1/2 (stdin can_read, stdout/stderr can_write; static storage zero-inits every other field). stdout/stderr carry a new `flush_each` flag (added to `struct FILE`) that makes `fputc` flush after every byte, so `fprintf(stdout,...)`/`fputc(c,stdout)` reach the terminal immediately and interleave with `printf`'s direct `write(1,...)` -- while `fopen`'d streams stay fully buffered (fopen now explicitly clears the flag, since malloc does not zero). Four functions ride the existing buffered core: `fgets` (reads at most size-1 bytes, stops after a kept newline or at EOF, always NUL-terminates, returns NULL only when EOF/error hits before any byte); `fputs` (writes the string, no added newline); `fseek` (first syncs the fd to the logical position -- flush a pending write buffer, or rewind the fd past any unconsumed read-ahead so `SEEK_CUR` is exact -- then discards the buffer, `lseek`s, and clears EOF); `ftell` (the fd offset adjusted for buffered bytes: pending writes sit past it `+len`, unconsumed read-ahead before it `-(len-pos)`). Declared in `user/libc.h`. `user/libctest.c` grows a streams group: a two-line file round-tripped via `fputs` then read back line-by-line with `fgets`, `ftell` checked at each step, `fseek` to 0 (rewind) and to an absolute offset, plus direct checks that the `stdin`/`stdout`/`stderr` globals wrap fds 0/1/2 with the right direction, and visible `fputs`/`fprintf` onto the unbuffered stdout. **Verified** in QEMU (compositor-spawn harness): all 10 libctest groups `PASS`, `ALL PASS`, 0 kernel faults; the `fputs->stdout OK` and `fprintf->stdout n=42 OK` lines appear on the serial console (proving the unbuffered standard stream reaches the terminal), and host `debugfs` confirmed the `fputs` round-trip wrote `/mnt/libctest_streams.txt` at exactly 21 bytes (`alpha line\nbeta line\n`) before it was cleaned. Build 0 warnings; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1805.6 KB. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**. **NEXT: the libc is compiler-ready -> present tcc-vs-minimal-compiler to the user via AskUserQuestion before starting the port.** Files: `user/libc.c`, `user/libc.h`, `user/libctest.c`, `core/kernel.h`. |
| v6.1.4 | **Phase 2: the `fprintf` family + a unified printf formatter.** The three previously-separate format switches in `user/libc.c` (printf was the rich one -- `-`/`0` flags, field width, %d/%i/%u/%o/%x/%X, %l* variants, %p/%s/%c/%%; snprintf/sprintf were a basic %d/%s/%x/%X/%p/%u/%c subset) are refactored into a single `format_core(emit, ctx, fmt, va_list)` that drives every consumer through an output "sink": each formatted char is handed to `emit(c, ctx)`. `printf` (stdout sink) is byte-for-byte identical to before; `vsnprintf`/`snprintf`/`sprintf` (bounded-buffer sink) now inherit the FULL formatter (a strict superset of their old basic switch -- existing specifiers render identically, and they gain flags/width/%l), with `vsnprintf`/`snprintf` returning the C99 would-be length; and the new `fprintf`/`vfprintf` (FILE* fputc sink, from v6.1.3) format straight onto a stream. Declared in `user/libc.h`. `user/libctest.c` grows a printf-family check: `snprintf("%d/%s/%x/%c/%05d", -7,"hi",255,'Z',42)` == `"-7/hi/ff/Z/00042"` (return 16), a 5-byte-buffer truncation to `"abcd"` returning the would-be 8, and `fprintf(f,"x=%d y=%s\n",100,"end")` round-tripped through a `/mnt` file. **Verified** in QEMU (compositor-spawn harness): all 9 libctest groups `PASS`, `ALL PASS`, 0 kernel faults; every `LIBCTEST:` line (itself printed via the refactored `printf`) is well-formed, and the desktop Terminal banner still reads `NyxOS Terminal v6.1.4` (rendered via `snprintf`) -- confirming the refactor is transparent to existing callers; shipped kernel boots clean. **NEXT (v6.1.5):** the `stdin`/`stdout`/`stderr` FILE\* globals + `fgets`/`fputs`/`fseek`/`ftell`, after which the libc should be compiler-ready. | v6.1.3 | **Phase 2: the FILE\* stdio CORE lands in the userland libc -- `fopen/fclose/fread/fwrite/fgetc/fputc/fflush/feof/ferror` over the existing fd syscalls (the last big libc layer before a C compiler).** New `typedef struct FILE` (in `user/libc.h`, with `EOF`/`NULL`) wraps an fd + a single 1 KB buffer used for read-ahead OR pending writes at a time, tracked by a `mode` field; the buffer refills on read and flushes on write, and an r+/w+/a+ read<->write switch rewinds the fd (lseek) or flushes first so the file offset stays consistent. `fopen` maps mode strings onto NyxOS's open() flags (bit0 = O_CREAT, no O_WRONLY): `"w"`->O_CREAT|O_TRUNC, `"a"`->O_CREAT|O_APPEND, `"r"`->O_RDONLY, and a `+` marks the stream read+write. `fread`/`fwrite` are implemented over the buffered `fgetc`/`fputc` (correct + buffered; a bulk fast-path can come later). Build 0 warnings; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1783 KB. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**. Files: `user/libc.c`, `user/libc.h`, `user/libctest.c`, `core/kernel.h`. |
| v6.1.2 | **Phase 2 libc brick: string/stdlib helpers a C compiler leans on -- `memchr`, `strrchr`, `strncat`, `strdup`, and `qsort`.** Added to `user/libc.c` and declared in `user/libc.h` (verified each was genuinely absent first, unlike the v6.1.0 mis-scout). `memchr` scans n bytes (NUL not special); `strrchr` returns the LAST match (matches the terminating NUL for c==0); `strncat` appends up to n bytes and always NUL-terminates; `strdup` returns a `malloc`'d copy (caller frees). `qsort` is a **shell sort** (Knuth gaps) over `size`-byte elements via the comparator -- no recursion and no scratch memory, and far better than plain insertion sort on the larger symbol/reloc tables a compiler hands it (byte-wise element swap; `gap` is `size_t` so the loop exits cleanly at gap 1). `user/libctest.c` grows a check exercising all five (memchr find/absent, strrchr last '/', bounded strncat "foo"+"ba"=="fooba", strdup round-trip + free, and qsort of an 8-int array verified fully ordered with min/max at the ends). **Verified** in QEMU (libctest spawned from the compositor per the established harness): `realloc/calloc/memmove/fileio/ctype/strtol/str-stdlib` all `PASS`, `ALL PASS`, 0 kernel faults; shipped kernel boots clean to login. The libc now has the string/stdlib surface a C front-end needs; **the remaining big brick before a compiler is a `FILE*` stdio layer.** Build 0 warnings; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1766.8 KB. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**. | v6.1.0 | **PHASE 2 opens (in-OS toolchain, MINOR bump): the userland libc gains `realloc`, `calloc` and `memmove` -- the reallocation + overlap-safe copy primitives every C compiler (and most nontrivial programs) need -- plus a `libctest.elf` toolchain self-test.** Scouting first mapped the port surface: NyxOS builds userland `.elf` with host GCC (`-ffreestanding -nostdlib -m64 -mno-red-zone`, `crt0.o` + `--just-symbols=libc.so` at a fixed base), and the **kernel already exposes every syscall a compiler needs** (`SYS_OPEN/READ/WRITE/CLOSE/LSEEK/STAT/FSTAT/MMAP/MUNMAP/SBRK/MPROTECT/UNLINK/RENAME`) with a working ELF loader/exec path -- so the gap to hosting a compiler is **purely the libc** (`user/libc.c` was 494 lines: `malloc/free`, basic mem/str, `printf`, `setjmp`, but no `realloc/calloc/memmove`, no `FILE*` stdio, no `qsort/strtol`). This increment lands the three most universally-required missing primitives as the first brick: `realloc` reads the block header's payload size (shrink/same returns the same pointer; grow = malloc+copy+free; `realloc(NULL,n)=malloc`, `realloc(p,0)=free`), `calloc` is malloc+zero with an overflow check, `memmove` copies backward when `dest>src`. Declared in `user/libc.h`; all userland ELFs relink against the new `libc.so`. `user/libctest.c` (new, wired into the Makefile + initramfs) exercises them and prints one PASS/FAIL line each -- it will grow as the libc grows toward the compiler. **Verified** in QEMU: `libctest` prints `realloc PASS / calloc PASS / memmove PASS / ALL PASS`, and the shipped kernel (no test auto-run) boots clean to the login screen, 0 kernel faults. (Observation, not shipped: auto-spawning a short-lived process during *very early* boot tripped a latent GPF in the exit/reap path after `[USER] exit(0)` -- a boot-timing fragility in the test harness only; nothing in the shipped image auto-runs `libctest`, which is invoked from the shell like the other `*test` programs. | v6.0.10 | **The Text Editor's Save now actually works, and a new note defaults into the user's persistent home (`/mnt/home/<user>`) so it survives a reboot (`kernel/gui/apps/editor_win.c`).** Two bugs: (1) `editor_save` tested `vfs_write_file(...) == 0` for success, but `vfs_write_file` (and the ext2/ramdisk backends under it) return the **number of bytes written** on success (`== pos` here) and a negative value on failure -- it never returns 0 for a non-empty write, so EVERY save of a non-empty buffer took the else branch and reported `Save failed` even though the bytes were written (and `modified` stayed set, and the filename was never recorded). (2) A never-named buffer defaulted to the hardcoded, ephemeral RAM path `/home/user/untitled.txt` -- wrong user, and the RAM tree that is rebuilt every boot, so the note was lost on restart. Fix: the success check is now `== pos`, and the default filename is built from `g_login_home` (the persistent home the session roots at, `/mnt/home/<user>` on the ext2 disk; falls back to `/home` if unset). The File Manager already opens files by absolute path, so opening-then-saving was unaffected; this fixes the blank-editor "type a note and Save" flow. Eleventh increment of the v6 loop (Phase 1), rounding out the persistent-home story (v6.0.6/6.0.8/6.0.9) across the core content app. **Verified** (headless QEMU, auto-login + a temp self-test that runs the REAL `editor_save` on a fresh editor, all reverted before commit): the status line read `Saved: /mnt/home/nyx/untitled.txt (34 bytes)` (was `Save failed`), the toolbar showed the `/mnt/home/nyx/untitled.txt` path, and host `debugfs` confirmed `/home/nyx/untitled.txt` = 34 bytes on the disk image. Build 0 warnings; 0 kernel faults. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1713.9 KB. Files: `kernel/gui/apps/editor_win.c`, `core/kernel.h`. |
| v6.0.9 | **Relative file operations issued from a mount-backed directory (the persistent home) now hit the mounted FS, so `touch`/`mkdir`/`rm`/`mv`/`cp`/write in `/mnt/home/<user>` persist across reboot instead of vanishing into an ephemeral ramdisk node (`kernel/fs/vfs.c`).** The shell builtins pass their argument verbatim, so `touch notes.txt` in the home called `vfs_touch("notes.txt")` -- a RELATIVE path. The mount-aware VFS entry points decided mount-vs-ramdisk purely by string prefix (`vfs_find_mount(path)` matches `/mnt...`), so a relative name never matched the mount and fell through to the ramdisk tree, attaching the new file to the v6.0.8 CWD *stub* -- invisible to `ls` (which lists the ext2 dir) and gone on the next boot. This was exposed by v6.0.8 (the Terminal now opens *in* the home, so relative creates there are the common case). Fix: a new `vfs_abs()` canonicalizes a relative path against `vfs_getcwd()` **only when the CWD is on a mounted FS** (reusing v6.0.8's `join_mount_relative`, incl. `.`/`..`), applied at the top of the seven create/write/remove/copy entry points (`vfs_open`, `vfs_mkdir`, `vfs_touch`, `vfs_write_file`, `vfs_unlink`, `vfs_rename`, `vfs_cp`). Absolute paths and ramdisk-relative paths are byte-unchanged (the File Manager, which already builds absolute paths, is unaffected; ramdisk `touch`/`mkdir` behave exactly as before). Tenth increment of the v6 loop (Phase 1), completing the persistent-home story with v6.0.6/6.0.8. **Verified** (headless QEMU, auto-login + serial probe + host `debugfs`, all reverted before commit): BEFORE -- a relative `mkdir`/`touch` in the home did NOT appear on the ext2 mount (debugfs showed only the absolute-path dir landed); AFTER -- relative `mkdir`, `touch` and a 28-byte `write` ALL landed on disk (debugfs: `relmk_a9/`, `reltouch_a9.txt` 0 B, `relwrite_a9.txt` 28 B), and on a SEPARATE fresh boot the File Manager at `/mnt/home/nyx` listed them with correct sizes -- proving persistence across reboot. Build 0 warnings; 0 kernel faults. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1713.9 KB. Files: `kernel/fs/vfs.c`, `core/kernel.h`. |
| v6.0.8 | **The Terminal now opens in -- and can navigate -- the user's persistent home directory (`/mnt/home/<user>`) on the ext2 mount, fixing a regression from v6.0.6 (`kernel/fs/vfs.c`).** Since v6.0.6 put the home on the ext2 disk, a new Terminal opened at `/` instead of the home: `login_home_node()` -> `vfs_path_node()` -> `resolve_path()` walks only the ramdisk tree, so it can't resolve `/mnt/home/<user>` (its `home`/`<user>` components live on ext2, not as ramdisk children), returned NULL, and `term->cwd` fell back to root. (The File Manager was unaffected -- it stores its cwd as a string, not a node.) Root fix: a new `mount_dir_stub()` materializes a mount-backed directory path as a chain of ramdisk "stub" dir nodes so the node-based CWD model can hold it (`vfs_getcwd()` rebuilds the path by walking the stubs' parents, while directory *contents* still come from the backing FS by path). `vfs_path_node()` now falls back to it (gated by `vfs_isdir`) so the Terminal starts in the home; `vfs_chdir()`'s mount branch routes through it too, and a new `join_mount_relative()` resolves a relative target (incl. `.`/`..`) against the CWD when the CWD is under a mount, so `cd <subdir>` / `cd ..` work in the home -- and a deep `cd /mnt/a/b/c` now succeeds in one step (the old branch only stubbed a single level). Ramdisk `cd` is byte-unchanged (the relative-join only triggers when getcwd is under a mount), and a `cd` onto a mounted *file* is still rejected (`vfs_isdir`). Ninth increment of the v6 loop (Phase 1); surfaced by booting the real desktop and reading the Terminal prompt. **Verified** (headless QEMU, auto-login + serial cwd probe, reverted before commit): the Terminal prompt went from `nyx:/$` (before) to `nyx:/mnt/home/nyx$` (after); the probe logged `term-cwd=/mnt/home/nyx`, `cd Documents -> /mnt/home/nyx/Documents`, `cd .. -> /mnt/home/nyx`, `cd / -> /`, and `cd bin -> /bin` (ramdisk unaffected). Build 0 warnings; 0 kernel faults. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1713.4 KB. Files: `kernel/fs/vfs.c`, `core/kernel.h`. |
| v6.0.7 | **Fixes `vfs_isdir` treating every mounted (ext2) path as a directory, which made the File Manager show `0 B` for files on the disk and let `cd` descend into a file (`kernel/fs/vfs.c`).** `vfs_isdir` short-circuited with `if (vfs_find_mount(path)) return 1;` -- true for ANY path under a mount point, including regular files. Since `vfs_stat` begins `if (vfs_isdir(path)) { *size = 0; return; }`, every file on the ext2 mount (e.g. the persistent home added in v6.0.6) reported size 0 -- the Size column showed `0 B` even though the bytes were on disk (host `debugfs` showed the home's `welcome.txt` = 249 bytes). Now, for a path inside a mount, `vfs_isdir` asks the backing FS: the mount root is a directory, and a deeper path is a directory only if `me->readdir` succeeds (>=0) -- the same probe `vfs_open` already uses (a regular file returns -1). The ramdisk / `/proc` branch is unchanged, so non-mount listings (`/`, etc.) are byte-identical. This also fixes the latent bug where `cd /mnt/<file>` was accepted (`vfs_isdir` backs SYS_CHDIR and a create-parent check in `syscall.c`). Eighth increment of the v6 loop (Phase 1); the bug was surfaced by v6.0.6's persistence work. **Verified:** build 0 warnings; QEMU boot 0 kernel faults; the File Manager at `/mnt/home/nyx` now shows `welcome.txt 249 B` (was `0 B`) with Documents/Downloads still `<DIR>`, and a `/` listing still shows correct sizes (`doom.elf 449 KB`, the coreutils ~10 KB) and directories -- no regression. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1712.5 KB. Files: `kernel/fs/vfs.c`, `core/kernel.h`. |
| v6.0.6 | **The logged-in user's home now lives on the persistent ext2 disk (`/mnt/home/<user>`), so files created there survive a reboot -- the first real persistence for user data (`kernel/auth/login.c`, `setup_user_home`).** Until now the whole VFS was a RAM tree rebuilt every boot, so anything a user made under `/home` was lost on restart; meanwhile the ext2 disk (mounted read-write at `/mnt`, write-through + crash-safe per file) already persisted but nothing user-facing used it. `setup_user_home()` now probes for the disk (`vfs_stat("/mnt")`) and, when present, roots the session home at `/mnt/home/<user>` (created via the mount's mkdir, which flushes to disk), falling back to the RAM `/home/<user>` only when no disk is mounted. The existing first-login seeding (Documents/, Downloads/, welcome.txt) now writes to disk on the first ever login and is skipped on later boots -- the on-disk home already exists so `fresh` is 0, and the user's own files are preserved untouched. This reuses the proven ext2 write path with no new persistence machinery. It is the user's choice at the persistence-design checkpoint (RAM-tree snapshot and whole-tree write-through were the alternatives). `/mnt` stays the mount, so DOOM (`-iwad /mnt/doom1.wad`) is unaffected. Seventh increment of the v6 loop (Phase 1). **Verified with a TWO-BOOT test against the same disk image:** boot 1 logged `path=/mnt/home/nyx fresh=1` and created the home; boot 2 logged `fresh=0` (home found on disk, seeding skipped) yet the File Manager still showed Documents/Downloads/welcome.txt -- and host `debugfs` confirms `/home/nyx/welcome.txt` on the image is 249 bytes with the real welcome text. Build 0 warnings; 0 kernel faults on both boots. **Known follow-up:** the Size column shows `0 B` for files on the ext2 mount (a `vfs_stat` size-report quirk for mount-backed files -- the content is on disk, only the reported size is wrong); to be fixed next. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1712.4 KB. Files: `kernel/auth/login.c`, `core/kernel.h`. |
| v6.0.5 | **The File Manager gains a Properties panel -- context menu -> Properties shows a modal with the selected entry's name, type, size and full path (`gui/apps/fileman_win.c`, `gui/apps/fileman_win.h`).** The context menu had Rename/Copy/Cut/Paste/Delete/New but no way to inspect an item. A "Properties" item is appended to the context menu (after a separator); choosing it snapshots the selected entry (name, is-dir, byte size, and the `fileman_get_path` full path) into new `props_*` fields and raises a centered modal drawn last in `fileman_win_draw` so it overlays the list. The panel shows Name, Type (File/Directory), Size (human-readable via the v6.0.3 `fileman_fmt_size` plus the exact byte count for files, `--` for directories) and Path, with a "(click to close)" hint; any click in the window dismisses it (handled at the top of `fileman_win_click`). Reading from a snapshot rather than the live selection keeps the panel stable while open.
