Files: `gui/apps/fileman_win.c`, `gui/apps/fileman_win.h`, `core/kernel.h`. |
| v6.0.4 | **The File Manager now sorts its listing -- directories first, then case-insensitive alphabetical -- instead of raw readdir order (`gui/apps/fileman_win.c`).** The list was shown in whatever order `vfs_readdir` returned: at `/` the directories came in creation order (`home, tmp, etc, var, opt, dev, bin, proc, sys`), which reads as unsorted. `fileman_refresh` now runs a `fileman_sort` pass over the parallel name/type/size arrays via a `fm_entry_less` comparator (dirs before files, then case-insensitive name order; selection sort, run only on refresh, n<=512 so O(n^2) is fine). A fresh read now also clears `sel_index` (a stale index no longer points at the same entry after a re-sort; `scroll_offset` was already being reset). The change is confined to `fileman_refresh` -- click/drag/rename/search/paste all operate on the same arrays and are unaffected beyond seeing them ordered. Fifth increment of the v6 loop (Phase 1); a follow-on to v6.0.3's Size column, found in the same live file-manager audit. **Verified:** build 0 warnings; QEMU boot 0 kernel faults; the File Manager opened at `/` now lists directories `bin, dev, etc, home, mnt, opt, proc, sys, tmp, var` alphabetically (was creation order) followed by the files alphabetically, with the Size column intact. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1710.7 KB. Files: `gui/apps/fileman_win.c`, `core/kernel.h`. |
| v6.0.3 | **The File Manager now shows a Size column, so files list their byte size (B/KB/MB) like a real file manager (`gui/apps/fileman_win.c`, `gui/apps/fileman_win.h`).** The listing previously had only a Name column (name + a leading `/` for directories) with no size information at all. A right-aligned Size column is added: `fileman_refresh` now `vfs_stat`s each file entry (building `<cwd>/<name>`) into a new `entry_sizes[]` field on the context, and the row renderer draws a compact human-readable size via a new `fileman_fmt_size` helper (integer-only B / KB / MB -- the kernel printf has no `%f`) right-aligned per row, with directories shown as `<DIR>`. A "Size" header sits above the column. Sizes are computed once per refresh (not per frame), so scrolling stays cheap, and the change is additive (no existing behaviour, click/drag/rename/search paths, changes). Fourth increment of the v6 loop (Phase 1); the gap was found by auditing the file manager live -- opening it headless at `/` and reading the list, which showed names only. **Verified:** build 0 warnings; QEMU boot 0 kernel faults; the File Manager opened at `/` shows every file's size right-aligned (e.g. `doom.elf` 449 KB, `edit.elf` 11 KB, the coreutils ~10 KB each) and each directory as `<DIR>`, under a Name/Size header. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1710.4 KB. Files: `gui/apps/fileman_win.c`, `gui/apps/fileman_win.h`, `core/kernel.h`. |
| v6.0.2 | **Restores the bottom margin of the Wallpaper picker window that v6.0.0 had eaten (`gui/core/compositor.c`).** v6.0.0 added a 3-button "Estilo:" row above the colour grid and pushed the grid origin down 52 px (WP_OY 40 -> 92) but left the picker window at its old 360x340 size -- so the last colour row (Rosa / Pizarra / Carbon) rendered flush against the window's bottom border with no padding (verified by opening the picker headless and zooming the bottom edge: the labels touched the frame). The window is enlarged to 360x380 so the full style row + the 11-swatch colour grid sit inside with a proper bottom margin that matches the top spacing. One-line change to the single `window_create(..., "Wallpaper", ...)` call site; the grid geometry in `wallpaper_win.c` was already correct -- only the container was too short, so nothing in the picker's own layout math changes. Third increment of the v6 loop (Phase 1, clean UI); the regression was caught by actually OPENING the picker (which v6.0.0 shipped without ever screenshotting) instead of re-reading the diff -- a reminder to screenshot the exact surface a change touches. **Verified:** build 0 warnings; QEMU boot 0 kernel faults; before/after screenshots of the open picker show the bottom row gaining a clean margin below "Rosa/Pizarra/Carbon". **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1709.8 KB. Files: `gui/core/compositor.c`, `core/kernel.h`. |
| v6.0.1 | **The About window and the Terminal banner now show the real kernel version instead of a stale hardcoded "0.2.0" (`gui/core/compositor.c` about_draw_fn, `gui/apps/terminal_win.c`).** On the default desktop the About window read "Version 0.2.0" and every Terminal opened with "NyxOS Terminal v0.2.0", while the rest of the OS (uname, the Settings > Info panel, the boot banner, nyxfetch) already reported the real `KERNEL_VERSION` -- so the two most visible identity surfaces disagreed with the actual build (now 6.0.1). Both are now built from the macros: the Terminal banner is `snprintf("NyxOS Terminal v%s", KERNEL_VERSION)`, and the About box draws `Version <KERNEL_VERSION>` plus a `<KERNEL_NAME> <KERNEL_CODENAME>` line ("NyxOS GUI Suite") in place of the old hardcoded "NyxOS Nightfall" tagline (also stale -- the codename is "GUI Suite", and the default wallpaper style is no longer Nightfall as of v6.0.0). The strings now derive from `core/kernel.h`, so they can never drift again on a version bump. Second increment of the v6 loop (Phase 1, clean UI); the defect was found by booting the real default desktop headless and reading the welcome windows, not by grepping. **Verified:** build 0 warnings; QEMU boot 0 kernel faults; the About window now reads "Version 6.0.1" and "NyxOS GUI Suite", and a freshly opened Terminal reads "NyxOS Terminal v6.0.1" (both previously showed "...0.2.0"). **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1709.8 KB. Files: `gui/core/compositor.c`, `gui/apps/terminal_win.c`, `core/kernel.h`. |
| v6.0.0 | **NyxOS v6 era begins -- the desktop gains a wallpaper STYLE axis and now defaults to a clean, minimal look (`gui/core/wallpaper_win.c`, `gui/core/wallpaper_win.h`, `gui/core/compositor.c`, `core/kernel.h`).** This is the pivot commit that opens the v6 line -- the long-horizon push toward a fully-functional daedalOS-style desktop OS -- and starts Phase 1 (clean desktop + filesystem + clean UI). Until now the Wallpaper app offered 11 base hues, but every hue was painted as the SAME busy "Nightfall" scene (a vertical gradient overlaid with a glowing moon and 110 seeded stars); there was no way to get a plain desktop. A render-STYLE axis is added, orthogonal to the hue: `WP_STYLE_CLEAN` (a pure vertical gradient of the chosen hue -- the new v6 default), `WP_STYLE_NIGHTFALL` (the classic gradient + moon + stars, preserved one click away), and `WP_STYLE_FLAT` (a single solid fill). `draw_background()` now reads `wallpaper_style()`: FLAT fills one solid colour and returns; CLEAN and NIGHTFALL share the existing gradient loop, after which `if (style != WP_STYLE_NIGHTFALL) return;` gates the moon+star pass so CLEAN stops at the gradient. The Wallpaper app grows a 3-button "Estilo:" row above the colour grid (window enlarged 360x340 -> 360x408; the grid shifts below the style row), with matching draw + click hit-testing. The NIGHTFALL path (moon halos, craters, seeded-LCG star field) is byte-for-byte the classic v5.9.x scene, only wrapped in the style gate, so selecting Nightfall reproduces the old desktop exactly. **Versioning:** a NEW ERA -- KERNEL_VERSION jumps 5.9.205 -> 6.0.0 (patch per increment, minor bump at phase boundaries); v5.9.x is the previous era and only v5.9.0 was LTS. **Verified:** build 0 warnings; QEMU boot 0 kernel faults; a headless desktop screendump shows the clean purple gradient desktop by default (no moon or stars), and a temporary default-flip to Nightfall screendumps the classic moon+star sky intact -- both boot clean with auto-login. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1709.7 KB. Files: `gui/core/wallpaper_win.c`, `gui/core/wallpaper_win.h`, `gui/core/compositor.c`, `core/kernel.h`. |
| v5.9.205 | **Selene decodes HTML entities in the page `<title>`, so a title like `Q&amp;A` shows as `Q&A` in the tab label and status bar instead of the raw `&amp;` (`gui/apps/selene_win.c`).** The `<title>` extractor copied the element's bytes verbatim into `s->title`, with no entity decoding -- while the body flow already decodes entities -- so a real page whose title carries `&amp;` / `&#39;` / `&quot;` (very common: `Tips &amp; Tricks`, `AT&amp;T`, `Ben &amp; Jerry&#39;s`) displayed the literal entity in the tab and the status line. The extractor now runs each `&...;` through the existing `decode_entity()` helper (the same one the body uses -- named table + numeric `&#nn;`/`&#xhh;`), copying the decoded bytes and advancing past the entity; a stray `&` with no valid entity still copies literally, and the 95-char clamp + tab/CR/LF->space rule are preserved. Purely additive and gated on an `&` appearing in the title: a title without one is byte-for-byte unchanged. Fourth increment of the bounded Selene feature loop; found by driving Selene live against a served docs/FAQ page titled `Docs &amp; FAQ` and reading the tab + status bar, not by grepping. (Same page also confirmed already-correct rendering of `<dl>`, `<ol start type>` -> `d./e./f.`, and `<details>/<summary>` open/closed toggles, so none of those needed a change.) **Verified:** build 0 warnings; QEMU boot 0 kernel faults; the served page's tab + status bar now read `Docs & FAQ` (was `Docs &amp; FAQ`); and a `<td>`-only styled-table anchor whose title has no entity screendumps byte-identical with the feature on vs off (0 differing pixels content-area + full-frame excluding the clock). **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1709.0 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. |
| v5.9.204 | **Selene renders a `<textarea>` as a multi-line editable field box and no longer dumps its default text into the page (`gui/apps/selene_win.c`).** Selene had NO `<textarea>` arm at all, so the tag was stripped and its content leaked into the layout as ordinary flow text with no field affordance -- on a real form (a comment/message/bio box) the placeholder text ran inline like a paragraph. A new arm, placed ahead of the inline-CSS emphasis stack (so a styled `<textarea style=..>` cannot leave an unbalanced colour-stack push when its close tag is skipped), registers a `SEL_FLD_TEXT` field and emits `rows` (default 2, clamped [1,5]) stacked box strips of `SEL_FIELD_W` field cells sharing one field id -- the existing per-line field-box draw then renders each strip as a bordered box, so the whole thing reads as a multi-line text area consistent with the text `<input>` boxes. The element's text content is skipped up to and including `</textarea>` (RCDATA-style: only a literal `</textarea>` ends it), so the default value no longer leaks. Purely additive and gated on the `<textarea>` tag: a page with none is byte-for-byte unchanged. Third increment of the bounded Selene feature loop; the gap was found by driving Selene live against a served signup form and reading the render (the bio `<textarea>` showed as bare text between the labelled inputs). **Verified:** build 0 warnings; QEMU boot 0 kernel faults; the served form's `<textarea rows=3>` now renders as a 3-row bordered field box under its label (was: the default sentence dumped as plain text); and a `<td>`-only styled-table anchor with no textarea screendumps byte-identical with the feature on vs off (0 differing pixels content-area + full-frame excluding the clock). **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1708.9 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. |
| v5.9.203 | **Selene keeps a broken/undecoded `<img>`'s `[img: ...]` caption INSIDE its placeholder box, truncating a long alt/filename with a trailing `...` (`gui/apps/selene_win.c`).** An image that has not decoded (missing file, or a format Selene does not draw) renders as a fixed 24-col framed placeholder box whose label is the image's alt text (or its src filename). The label was capped at 29 characters -- far wider than the 24-col box -- so a real-world long alt (e.g. "Build time comparison chart") overflowed two ways at once: the flow-text copy of the label leaked past the box's right edge as a stray fragment above the box, and the in-box redraw spilled past the box border. The caption is now capped to `SEL_IMG_BOX_W - 9` cols so the whole `[img: ...]` string (the `[img: ` prefix + `]` suffix + a 1-col inset each side) fits the box; a source longer than that is cut with a trailing `...` to signal truncation. Because the emitted label now never exceeds the box width, the box's own grey fill fully covers the flow-text copy (no leak) and the centred in-box label no longer crosses the border. Purely additive and gated on the `<img>` tag: a page with no image is byte-for-byte unchanged. Second increment of the bounded Selene feature loop; the defect was found by driving Selene live against a served release-notes page containing a `<figure>` with a long-alt `<img>` and reading the render. **Verified:** build 0 warnings; QEMU boot 0 kernel faults; the served `<figure>` placeholder now shows `[img: Build time c...]` cleanly within its border with no stray fragment (was: label overflowing on both sides); and a `<td>`-only styled-table anchor with no images screendumps byte-identical with the feature on vs off (0 differing pixels content-area + full-frame excluding the clock). **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1708.1 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. |
| v5.9.202 | **Selene renders a `<th>` table-header cell in BOLD by default, like a real browser (`gui/apps/selene_win.c`, `render_table`).** Selene already UPPER-CASES header cells to set them apart, but a browser's default `th { font-weight: bold }` was missing, so a data table's header row read at the same weight as its body -- on a real page (a benchmark/spec/pricing table) the header did not stand out. render_table's Phase A extracts each cell's text plus its per-character style arrays via `sel_cell_text_styled`; the bold channel is `xd` (bit0). The fix ORs bit0 across the whole extracted run for a header cell only: `if (cells[found].th) for (int b = 0; b < w; b++) xd[b] |= 1;`. It is purely additive and gated on the cell being a `<th>`: a `<td>` cell, and any non-table text, is byte-for-byte unchanged, and a header whose own inline style already bolds a span just keeps the bit set (idempotent). First increment of the bounded Selene feature loop resumed after the maintenance pass; found by driving Selene live against a served article page and reading the render, not by grepping for missing tags. **Verified:** build 0 warnings; QEMU boot 0 kernel faults; a served page with a `<thead>` benchmark table screendumps with the `PAGE/BYTES/RENDER` header row visibly bold above the normal-weight body rows; and a `<td>`-only styled-table anchor screendumps byte-identical with the feature on vs off (0 differing pixels content-area + full-frame excluding the clock), proving no collateral change to non-header content. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1708.1 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. |
| v5.9.201 | **Maintenance (security): bounded the DNS query-name encoder against a kernel stack buffer overflow (`net/dns.c`, `encode_dns_name`).** The encoder wrote length-prefixed labels into the caller's buffer with NO size parameter and no bound on its write cursor, trusting an unenforced assumption that the hostname is short. Its caller `dns_resolve` uses a fixed 512-byte stack buffer, and some callers pass an UNCAPPED hostname -- the shell network commands (`ping`/`nslookup`/`wget`-style in `kernel.c`) hand `argv[1]` straight to `dns_resolve`, and unlike Selene's `parse_url` (which caps a host to 128 bytes) a command-line argument longer than ~498 bytes overflowed the `pkt[512]` stack array with attacker-influenced bytes -- a kernel stack smash. Fix: `encode_dns_name` now takes a `cap` (buffer capacity) and checks it before every write (`label-length byte + label + room for the terminating 0`), returning -1 when the name would not fit; the caller already handles a negative return (`if (nlen < 0) return 0;`), so an over-long hostname now fails resolution gracefully instead of corrupting the stack. `pos` widened to `uint32_t` to match `cap` (values are tiny -- no truncation). Behaviour is byte-for-byte identical for every hostname that already fit (the new guards fire only where the old code overflowed). Second increment of the general MAINTENANCE pass; the network-stack audit of `tcp.c`/`udp.c`/`ip.c` and the DNS response parser found them already hardened (bounds-checked, checksum-validated, length-clamped), so this encoder was the one genuine gap. **Verified:** build 0 warnings; QEMU boot 0 kernel faults; the DNS path re-exercised live (a real hostname still emits a valid query -- ARP to the DNS server observed, no fault); and the non-DNS fetch+render path screendumps byte-identical to v5.9.200 (0 differing pixels content-area + full-frame excl clock). **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1708.1 KB. Files: `net/dns.c`. |
| v5.9.200 | **Maintenance (security): fixed a remotely-triggerable heap buffer overflow in the HTTP chunked-transfer decoder (`net/http.c`, `http_parse_response`).** The chunk-copy clamp read `if (olen + sz > body_avail) sz = body_avail - olen;`, but `sz` is the chunk size parsed from an attacker-controlled hex field with no cap (`sz = sz*16 + digit`). With `olen > 0` (accumulated from a prior chunk) a huge `sz` (e.g. `FFFFFFFF`) makes the uint32 sum `olen + sz` WRAP to a small value, so `> body_avail` is false and the clamp is skipped; the copy loop then runs with the huge `sz` bounded only by the raw buffer end and writes past `out` (which was `kmalloc(body_avail + 1)`) when `body_avail` had been clamped down to a small `Content-Length` -- i.e. a malicious/compromised server (or MITM) sending both `Content-Length: <small>` and `Transfer-Encoding: chunked` plus an oversized chunk size overflows the heap with attacker-controlled data. Fix is one line, overflow-safe: `if (sz > body_avail - olen) sz = body_avail - olen;` -- since `olen <= body_avail` is invariant at that point, `body_avail - olen` is a safe non-negative remaining capacity, and comparing `sz` against it is mathematically identical to the old test for every non-overflowing (benign) response while never performing the wrapping addition, so a huge `sz` is correctly clamped. Total decoded bytes stay <= `body_avail`; `out[olen] = '\0'` stays in bounds. Behaviour is byte-for-byte unchanged for all benign responses. **First increment of the general MAINTENANCE pass** (features paused at the v5.9.200 milestone per the user's directive). **Verified:** build 0 warnings; QEMU boot 0 kernel faults; the browser's normal (non-chunked) fetch+render path re-tested on the styled-cell page screendumps byte-identical to v5.9.199 (0 differing pixels content-area + full-frame excl clock), confirming no fetch/render regression. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1708.0 KB. Files: `net/http.c`. |
| v5.9.199 | **Feature: Selene now honours the CSS `vertical-align: super` and `vertical-align: sub` declarations on flow text -- an inline `style="vertical-align:super"` renders raised above the baseline and `vertical-align:sub` renders lowered, matching the `<sup>`/`<sub>` tags, so CSS-driven superscript/subscript (footnote references, trademark marks, ordinals, math exponents -- commonly done with a styled span rather than a `<sup>`/`<sub>` tag) is no longer flattened onto the baseline.** The main render loop's inline-style parser already read `font-weight`/`text-decoration`/`text-align`/`text-transform`/`white-space`/`font-style`, but NOT `vertical-align` -- an asymmetry: the `<sub>`/`<sup>` tags set the `nvo` vertical-offset flag (packed into the per-char attribute byte as `(cur_vo<<4)` and drawn shifted), yet `vertical-align:super`/`sub` set via CSS did nothing. It now reads `vertical-align` and maps the `super` and `sub` keywords onto the exact same `nvo` values (`super`->2 raised, `sub`->1 lowered) the tags use -- a clean 1:1 keyword equivalence. Other `vertical-align` values (`baseline`/`middle`/`top`/`bottom`/`text-top`/`text-bottom`/lengths) have no glyph-shift equivalent in a fixed-cell text renderer, so they are deliberately left inherited (no guessing). Scope is the main text flow; the styled-cell extractor tracks only colour/background/bold (it has no vertical-offset channel and does not handle `<sub>`/`<sup>` either), so `vertical-align` in a table cell stays out of scope by the same design. Purely additive + gated: a page with no `vertical-align:super`/`sub` style is byte-for-byte unchanged. **Zero-regression proven:** the styled-cell test screendumps identical to v5.9.198 (0 differing pixels across the content area, and 0 full-frame excluding the clock). **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a page served over http with `E = mc^2` and `H_2O` written both with `<sup>`/`<sub>` tags and with CSS `vertical-align` spans, plus a footnote `[1]`, a `TM` mark and a `vertical-align:middle` control): the CSS super/sub spans render shifted and match the tags, while plain text and the `middle` span stay on the baseline. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1708.1 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. |
| v5.9.198 | **Feature: Selene now honours the CSS `font-style: italic` (and `oblique`) declaration on flow text -- an inline `style="font-style:italic"` renders with the same underline-as-italic fallback that the `<i>`/`<em>` family already uses, so CSS-driven emphasis (blockquotes, figure captions, utility-class italics) is no longer silently dropped to plain text.** The main render loop's inline-style parser already read `font-weight` (-> bold), `text-decoration`, `text-align`, `text-transform` and `white-space`, but NOT `font-style` -- an asymmetry: an `<i>` tag underlines (the fixed font has no italic glyphs, so italic falls back to underline) and `font-weight:bold` bolds, yet `font-style:italic` set via CSS did nothing. It now reads `font-style` and maps `italic`/`oblique` onto the exact same `nul` underline flag the `<i>`/`<em>`/`<cite>`/`<var>`/`<dfn>`/`<address>` tags use, fully consistent with Selene's established text-mode italic rendering. There is deliberately NO `font-style:normal -> clear` branch: `nul` doubles as the real underline flag, so clearing it on `normal` could strip an inherited `<u>`/`text-decoration:underline` (unlike `font-weight:normal`, which is safe because bold is not conflated). Scope is the main text flow; the styled-cell extractor tracks only colour/background/bold (it has no underline channel and does not handle `<i>` or `font-weight` either), so `font-style` in a `<td>` stays out of scope by the same design. Purely additive + gated: a page with no `font-style:italic` style is byte-for-byte unchanged. **Zero-regression proven:** the styled-cell test screendumps identical to v5.9.197 (0 differing pixels across the content area, and 0 full-frame excluding the clock). **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a page served over http with an `<i>` tag, a `font-style:italic` span, a `font-style:oblique` span and a whole `font-style:italic` paragraph): the CSS italics now render underlined and match the `<i>` tag, while plain text and a `font-style:normal` span stay plain. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1708.0 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. |
| v5.9.197 | **Feature: Selene now honours the HTML5 `<dialog>` element's visibility rule -- a `<dialog>` WITHOUT the `open` attribute is `display:none` by default, so its markup (a modal / pop-up that is present in the DOM but not shown) is now HIDDEN instead of having its text dumped into the page flow; a `<dialog open>` renders as a block on its own lines like a real modal.** Real pages routinely ship a login / newsletter / cookie modal as a hidden `<dialog>`, and Selene had no arm for the tag, so it fell through to plain inline text and leaked the modal's contents mid-page (a visible artifact). A dedicated `<dialog>` arm in the main render loop now: on an open tag WITHOUT `open`, scans (nesting-aware, bounded by the buffer length -- the same machinery the `<details>` collapsed path uses) to the matching `</dialog>` and skips the whole subtree, emitting nothing and leaving the surrounding sentence uninterrupted; on `<dialog open>` (and the `</dialog>` close) it inserts a block break so the shown dialog lays out as a block. The main loop's post-statement is empty, so the skip's `i = k; continue;` resumes exactly past `</dialog>` with no character skipped. Purely additive + gated on the literal `<dialog>` tag appearing: a page with none is byte-for-byte unchanged. Scope is the main text flow (where dialogs actually live -- top-level modal containers); a `<dialog>` nested inside a table cell is left to the cell extractor as before (they do not occur in real layouts). **Zero-regression proven:** the styled-cell test screendumps identical to v5.9.196 (0 differing pixels across the content area). **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a page with a hidden `<dialog>` between two sentences and a separate `<dialog open>`, served over http): the hidden dialog's title/body do NOT appear and the two sentences read continuously, while the open dialog renders as its own block. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1707.8 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. |
| v5.9.196 | **Feature: Selene now recognises the HTML5 block elements `<main>`, `<aside>` and `<address>` as blocks (and renders `<address>` italic), so a page that structures its content with them lays out on separate lines like a browser instead of mashing every region into one running paragraph.** These common sectioning tags were missing from `is_block_tag` (which already had `<section>`/`<article>`/`<header>`/`<footer>`/`<nav>`), so text inside/around a `<main>`, `<aside>` or `<address>` ran inline with no paragraph break; they are now in that list, so each gets the same block break as its siblings -- in BOTH the main render loop and the in-cell extractor (`is_block_tag` drives both). `<address>` is additionally added to the italic family (rendered underlined, Selene's fixed-font italic fallback, like `<i>`/`<em>`/`<cite>`), matching the browser default. Purely additive + gated on the tag appearing: a page with none of these tags is byte-for-byte unchanged. **Zero-regression proven:** the styled-cell test screendumps identical to v5.9.195 (0 differing pixels across the content area). **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a page of `<main>`/`<aside>`/`<address>` regions served over http, screenshotted): each region now starts on its own line as a block (was one continuous inline paragraph) and the `<address>` block is underlined. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1707.6 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. |
| v5.9.195 | **Feature: a `<fieldset>`'s `<legend>` (its group title) now renders in BOLD, so the caption stands out from the fieldset body -- as a browser emphasizes it -- instead of reading as ordinary text.** `<legend>` was an unhandled tag (silently stripped); it is now mapped onto the SAME inline style stack that already renders `<b>`/`<strong>`: an open `<legend>` sets the bold bit and pushes it, and the matching `</legend>` pops it, so only the legend's own text becomes bold and it nests/closes correctly. It reuses the existing synthetic-bold draw (a second glyph pass shifted 1px), so no new state or draw path. Purely additive: a page with no `<legend>` never sets the flag, so it stays byte-for-byte unchanged. **Zero-regression proven:** the styled-cell test screendumps identical to v5.9.194 (0 differing pixels across the content area). **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a page of `<fieldset>` cards served over http, screenshotted): the `Account` and `Preferences` legends now render bold above their card bodies, while the body text (`Name:`, `Email:`, ...) stays normal weight. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1707.6 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. |
| v5.9.194 | **Feature: a TOP-LEVEL bordered `<div>`/`<fieldset>` box now gets the same subtle background TINT as an in-cell panel, so the whole card reads as a filled panel (not just an outline on the bare page).** v5.9.193 tinted only in-cell panels (via `render_table`'s background band); a top-level box is drawn by the box-depth draw pass and had no fill. This is done purely in the DRAW loop (no parse-side change): the panel tint pixel is factored out of `sel_panel_tint` into a pure `sel_tint_px(bg)` (a touch lighter on a dark page, darker on a light one) that the draw loop computes once from the resolved page background -- the SAME colour the in-cell band interns, so the two look identical. While `box_depth > 0` (inside a top-level box) the draw fills the box interior with that tint on every marker + content row (behind the outline), and the un-styled base text and links on those rows draw with the tint as their background so the glyphs blend seamlessly into the fill (an inline `background` inside the box still wins). Everything is gated on `box_depth > 0`, so a page with no top-level bordered box is byte-for-byte unchanged. **Zero-regression proven:** the styled-cell test screendumps identical to v5.9.193 (0 differing pixels across the content area). **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (pages served over http, screenshotted): a page of `<fieldset>` cards (grey + blue-bordered) now shows each filled with the subtle panel tint out to its border, while the flow text between them and a border-less `<div>` stay on the plain page background. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1707.5 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. |
| v5.9.193 | **Feature: a bordered panel that lives in a table cell now gets a subtle background TINT, so the card reads as a FILLED panel (like a real repo page's cards) instead of just an outline on the bare page background.** When `render_table` draws a cell whose content is a bordered `<div>` (the in-cell box added in v5.9.191) and that cell has no background of its own, it now paints the cell's span with a panel tint -- a colour derived from the page background itself (a touch lighter on a dark page, a touch darker on a light one, via a luminance test on the page-bg palette entry; the low 24 bits of a palette entry are RGB whatever the framebuffer bpp), so contrast stays comfortable and the tint suits any page. It reuses the existing per-cell background-band machinery (the same path that paints a `bgcolor` cell), so the cell text draws ON the tint (no per-glyph gaps) and any inline background inside the cell still wins over it. The tint colour is interned LAZILY -- only the first time a boxed, bg-less cell actually needs it -- so a table with no such cell never touches the palette and stays byte-identical. **Zero-regression proven:** the styled-cell test screendumps identical to v5.9.192 (0 differing pixels across the content area). **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (pages served over http, screenshotted): the repo reconstruction's `README`/file-list and `About`/`Releases` panels now show a subtle filled-card background inside their outline, matching the GitHub card look, while a page with no in-cell panel is unchanged. (Top-level bordered `<div>`/`<fieldset>` boxes are still outline-only -- tinting those needs a parse-side background and is a later refinement.) **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1707.3 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. |
| v5.9.192 | **Feature: Selene renders `<fieldset>` as a bordered CARD -- a form-grouping `<fieldset>` (with its `<legend>` as the heading) now draws inside a rectangle outline like a browser, instead of its content running as plain flow text with the tags dropped.** `<fieldset>` was previously an unknown tag, silently skipped; it is now handled by a dedicated arm (placed before the generic block-tag arm) that gives it a paragraph break and reuses the v5.9.189/190 top-level box machinery: it is treated as an ALWAYS-bordered block, pushing/popping the same per-`<div>` box stack and emitting the `SEL_BOX_TOP`/`SEL_BOX_BOT` marker lines, so the existing draw pass strokes the outline (top+bottom rule + side verticals) around the whole fieldset -- honouring a CSS `border-color` if the fieldset sets one, else the theme grey default. The `<legend>` has no special tag handling, so it renders as ordinary text at the top of the box (a reasonable simplification of the browser's on-the-border legend). Purely additive: a page with no `<fieldset>` never hits the new arm, so it stays byte-for-byte unchanged. **Zero-regression proven:** the styled-cell test screendumps identical to v5.9.191 (0 differing pixels across the content area). **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (pages served over http, screenshotted): a page of fieldsets renders the default one inside a grey card and a `border-color:#1f6feb` one inside a blue card, while un-bordered flow text and a plain `<div>` draw no outline. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1707.0 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. |
| v5.9.191 | **Feature: Selene draws a border box around a table CELL whose content is a bordered `<div>` -- so a panel / card that lives INSIDE a table cell (the common two-column page layout: a sidebar and a main column, each a bordered `<div>` in a `<td>`) finally renders as an outlined card, not just loose text.** v5.9.189/190 only boxed a TOP-LEVEL `<div>`; a cell's tags are stripped by the cell extractor, so an in-cell panel had no outline. Now `render_table` detects, per cell, whether its body holds a bordered `<div>` (`sel_cell_bordered_div`, same border/colour rules as the top-level box) and, in its line-emitting phase, records that cell's outline as a rectangle: the txt-stream index of the cell's first and last row-line plus its column span, into a small per-page `cell_boxes[]` list. `wrap_text` gained one optional output param -- a `line_start[]` side table (txt index -> final line number) that it only WRITES, never reads, so every wrapped line and per-char attribute is byte-for-byte unchanged; after wrap those txt indices are resolved to real line numbers (a box that fails to map is dropped, never mis-drawn). The draw pass then strokes each rectangle -- top+bottom rule and side verticals, in the panel's border colour -- clipped to the scroll viewport. It is purely additive and gated on a cell actually containing a bordered div. **Zero-regression proven:** a page with no such cell (the styled-cell test) screendumps identical to v5.9.190 (0 differing pixels across the content area), confirming the `wrap_text` change is inert. **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (pages served over http, screenshotted): the repo-page reconstruction's two-column layout now shows its nav, `README`/file-list and `About`/`Releases` panels each inside a drawn outline box, matching the boxed-card look of a real repo page (together with the blue links, pill buttons and stacked file list of earlier increments). **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1706.1 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. |
| v5.9.190 | **Feature: the drawn `<div>` border box now honours the CSS border COLOUR -- a `border:1px solid #e5534b` card is stroked in red, a `border-color:#1f6feb` card in blue, etc. -- instead of always the fixed grey of v5.9.189, so coloured callouts / alert boxes (red error, blue note, yellow warning) render in their own colour.** At a bordered `<div>` open the parser now resolves the border colour -- an explicit `border-color`, else the first colour token in the `border` shorthand (`#hex` or a named colour) -- interns it into the page palette, and carries that palette index on both the box-TOP and box-BOTTOM marker lines (in the marker's colour slot) and on a small per-`<div>` stack so the matching `</div>` closes in the same colour; the draw loop reads the marker's colour for the top/bottom edges and keeps a per-nesting-level colour stack (rebuilt by the same off-screen pre-scan as the depth) so the side verticals down the card draw in the box's colour too. A bordered div with no resolvable colour still falls back to the theme grey, so v5.9.189's plain cards are unchanged. **Zero-regression proven:** a page with no bordered div screendumps identical to v5.9.189 (0 differing pixels across the content area). **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (pages served over http, screenshotted): a page of callout cards renders the grey card grey, the red-bordered card's outline red, the blue-bordered card's outline blue and its nested green-bordered card green, while un-bordered flow text draws no outline. (Bordered divs INSIDE a table cell -- the repo reconstruction's in-cell panels -- are still not boxed; that remains the larger deferred item, needing a per-char box channel through render_table.) **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1703.9 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. |
| v5.9.189 | **Feature: Selene draws a real BORDER BOX around a bordered top-level `<div>` -- a `<div style="border...">` (a card / callout / panel) now renders inside a drawn rectangle outline (top rule, left+right verticals, bottom rule) like a browser, instead of its content just running as plain flow text.** The outline reuses the existing `<hr>` marker-line machinery with no new per-char channel and no `wrap_text` change: an opening bordered `<div>` emits a box-TOP marker line and its `</div>` a box-BOTTOM marker line, carried as two free sentinel values (200/201) in the same `trule`->`line_rule` byte the horizontal rule uses. The parse loop keeps a small div stack (every `<div>` pushes whether its CSS declares a non-zero `border`/`border-width`/`border-top`/`border-style`, so the matching `</div>` closes the right box), and the draw loop tracks the box-nesting depth (with a pre-scan so a box scrolled off the top still draws), painting the top/bottom edges on the marker rows and the side verticals down every row in between (each nested level inset a few px). It is purely additive and gated: a `<div>` with no border -- and every page with no bordered top-level div -- emits no marker, so nothing is drawn and the layout is byte-for-byte unchanged. **Zero-regression proven:** a table page with no bordered div screendumps identical to v5.9.188 (0 differing pixels across the content area). **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (pages served over http, screenshotted): a page of bordered `<div>` cards (About / Releases, plus a nested card) renders each inside a rectangle box with the correct inset for the nested one, while un-bordered flow text between the cards draws no outline. (Bordered divs INSIDE a table cell -- e.g. the repo reconstruction's in-cell panels -- are not boxed yet: render_table strips their tags; that and `<fieldset>` are later refinements.) **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1703.5 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. |
| v5.9.188 | **Feature: Selene styles the content INSIDE a nested table's cells -- so a file list, directory or data table nested in another table's cell now renders each nested cell's own inline colour, background, bold and (most visibly) HYPERLINKS, instead of flattening every nested cell to plain grey text.** Building on v5.9.187 (which lays a nested `<table>` out one row per line): `sel_flatten_nested` now also receives the page context and the parallel per-char attribute arrays (colour / background / bold / link). On the STYLED cell path it extracts each nested `<td>`/`<th>` via `sel_cell_text_styled` -- the same styled extractor used for top-level cells -- straight into those attribute arrays at the current offset, so an `<a href>` inside a nested cell registers as a real clickable link and a coloured / `<b>` / `<code>` span renders; the synthesized folder glyphs and the row/cell separators carry no styling. The PLAIN path (used for Pass-1 column-WIDTH measurement and `<caption>` text) passes NULL attribute pointers and takes the exact old plain-text copy, so column widths -- and any page without a nested-in-cell table -- are byte-for-byte unchanged. A table nested one level deeper still collapses to `[..]`. **Zero-regression proven:** a table with no nested table screendumps identical to v5.9.187 (0 differing pixels across the whole content area). **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (pages served over http, screenshotted): a nested 4-row file list whose names (`.github`, `docs`, `kernel`, `README.md`) are each an `<a href>` now renders those names as blue underlined clickable links (status bar `0 links` -> `4 links`), and the repo reconstruction's file-list panel shows its file/dir links blue (status bar `19 links` -> `23 links`). **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1702.5 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. |
| v5.9.187 | **Feature: Selene renders a NESTED `<table>` inside a cell as ONE ROW PER LINE -- so a file list, directory or data table nested inside another table's cell shows its rows stacked vertically instead of being flattened onto one truncated line `[ a b / c d ]`.** Building on the v5.9.184 multi-line cell + v5.9.186 hard-break machinery: `sel_flatten_nested` gains a `break_rows` flag -- the STYLED cell path passes 1, so between nested rows it emits the non-rendering break sentinel `0x01` (and drops the `[ ]` wrapper) instead of the ` / ` separator, and the parent cell's word-wrap (which already treats `0x01` as a forced break) then lays each nested row on its own line. The PLAIN path (used for Pass-1 column-WIDTH measurement and `<caption>` text) still passes 0 and produces the exact old `[ a b / c d ]` one-liner, so column widths -- and any page without a nested-in-cell table -- are byte-for-byte unchanged. **Zero-regression proven:** a short-cell table (no nested table) screendumps identical to v5.9.186 (0 differing pixels across the whole window). **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (pages served over http, screenshotted): a two-column table whose left cell holds a 4-row file-list sub-table now renders those four rows stacked (each wrapping to the column width) instead of one `[ ... ]` blob, and the repo reconstruction's file-list panel reads as a real list. (The nested cells' own text is still plain -- inline colour/links inside a nested table are a later refinement.) **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1701.8 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. |
| v5.9.186 | **Feature: Selene honours HARD LINE BREAKS inside a table cell -- a `<br>`, and the boundary of a block element (`<p>`, `<div>`, `<li>`, `<h1>`-`<h6>`, `<ul>`/`<ol>`, `<hr>`, ...) -- so multi-line cell content that intends distinct lines actually breaks instead of collapsing into one run of spaces.** Building on the v5.9.184 multi-line cell engine: `sel_cell_text_styled` now emits a non-rendering sentinel byte (`0x01`) into the cell text wherever it crosses a `<br>` or a block-tag boundary (`is_block_tag`, open OR close), suppressing a leading break and collapsing consecutive ones; `render_table`'s Phase-A word-wrap treats the sentinel as a FORCED line break (ends the current wrapped line before it, starts a new one after, and never emits the sentinel into a line), while still soft-wrapping the rest to the column width. The per-column colour/background/bold/link painting is unchanged, so a `<b>`/colour/`<a>` run keeps its styling across the break (a wrapped `<a>` stays blue). **Zero-regression proven:** a cell with no `<br>`/block tags produces no sentinel and wraps byte-for-byte as v5.9.184 -- a short-cell table screendumps identical to v5.9.185 (0 differing pixels across the whole window). **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a bordered table served over http, screenshotted): a cell of `line one<br>line two<br><a>line three</a>` renders three stacked lines (the third blue), a cell of two `<p>` paragraphs renders them on separate lines (its `<b>` bold preserved), a cell of three `<li>` renders one per line, and a plain no-break cell still word-wraps exactly as before. This is also the groundwork for rendering a nested `<table>` (the file list) one row per line. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1701.7 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. |
| v5.9.185 | **Feature: Selene treats an in-page anchor `<a href="#">` / `<a href="#section">` as a REAL link -- rendered blue, underlined and clickable, like a browser -- instead of dropping it, so pages that use `#` hrefs for their nav/menu (very common on JS-driven sites) show their links instead of flat grey text.** `selene_resolve` previously returned an empty URL for any href starting with `#`, and both the main-loop and table-cell `<a>` handlers drop a link whose resolved URL is empty -- so every `href="#"` anchor rendered as plain text. It now resolves a `#`-anchor to the CURRENT page's URL (`scheme://host[:port]/base_path`), which is non-empty, so the link registers and draws in the link colour. A truly hrefless `<a>` (e.g. a `<a name=..>` named target) is still NOT a link (returns empty), and `mailto:` / `javascript:` are still dropped; the http(s) / absolute / protocol-relative / relative resolution paths are byte-for-byte untouched, so ordinary links resolve exactly as before (zero regression). **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (pages served over http, screenshotted): the repo reconstruction's whole top nav bar (`Code` `Issues` `Pull requests` ... `Settings`) and its `kazah-png / nyx-os` header, all `href="#"`, now render as blue underlined links -- the status bar goes from `0 links` to `19 links` -- while a control page of real path links still reports its same `9 links` (normal hrefs unchanged). **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1701.4 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. |
| v5.9.184 | **Feature: Selene renders MULTI-LINE table cells -- a cell's content word-wraps to the column width and the row grows as tall as its tallest cell -- so a table used for layout or holding paragraphs (a repo page's nav bar, an About panel, a description column) shows its full content instead of being truncated to one line.** This is the unlock the v5.9.170-183 cell features were blocked on: `render_table`'s Pass 2 previously emitted exactly ONE text line per `<tr>`, each cell cut to its column width. It now runs two phases per row: Phase A extracts each cell's FULL styled text (via `sel_cell_text_styled`, cap raised from the column width to `SEL_CELL_CAP`) into per-column pools and word-wraps it to the column width (break at the last space, hard-break an over-long word) into up to `SEL_CELL_MAXLINES` lines, tracking the row height H = the tallest cell; Phase B emits H stacked text lines, each cell showing its line h (blank once it runs out), with border pipes, cellpadding, per-line alignment and the per-column colour/background/bold/link painting carried on every line exactly as before. **Zero-regression is proven, not asserted:** when every cell already fits its column the wrap yields a single line and the bytes are unchanged -- a bordered short-cell table screendumps BYTE-FOR-BYTE identical to v5.9.183 (0 differing pixels across the whole window). Pools are kmalloc'd once per table (freed with cells/occ; on OOM the table degrades like the existing allocation guards). **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (pages served over http, screenshotted): a two-column table whose right cells hold sentences wraps each across several aligned lines with its `<b>`/colour/`<a>` styling preserved (a wrapped `<a>Selene</a>` stays blue), and the repo reconstruction's top nav bar (which used to cut at "Type / to s") now shows every item and the About panel renders its full description. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1701.2 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. |
| v5.9.183 | **Feature: Selene renders `<a>` hyperlinks INSIDE a table cell -- blue, underlined and clickable -- so a data/navigation table whose cells hold links (a file list, a nav row, a directory) shows real links instead of flat text.** Building on v5.9.182's styled cell extractor: `sel_cell_text_styled` now also tracks a per-char hyperlink id (`olink`) -- on an `<a href>` inside a `<td>`/`<th>` it registers the link in the page's link table (`extract_href` + `selene_resolve`, exactly like the main parser's `<a>` arm) and marks the cell text until `</a>`; `render_table` (which already paints per-column colour/background/bold after each row is emitted) now also paints `tlink` for those columns, so the existing draw link overlay renders them blue + underlined and they are clickable. It reuses the page's link registry with the same `SEL_MAX_LINKS` guard; a nested `<table>`'s flattened text carries no link, and a table whose cells contain no `<a>` is byte-for-byte unchanged (zero regression). **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a bordered table served over http, screenshotted): a status table whose Host column (`web-01`..`cache`) and Docs column (`guide` x4) are `<a href>` links renders all eight as blue underlined links (status bar: 9 links, incl. one outside the table), coexisting with the v5.9.182 coloured/bold/pill cells. (Links only register within a cell's single truncated line; multi-line / nested-table cell content -- which is what the GitHub reconstruction's cells need -- remains a separate future step.) **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1700.0 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. |
| v5.9.182 | **Feature: Selene renders the inline styling of text INSIDE a table cell -- coloured text, background "pills", bold, `<code>`/`<mark>` -- instead of flattening every `<td>`/`<th>` to plain text, so status/data tables (a green OK, a red DOWN, a coloured badge, a bold field) render the way real pages present them.** Until now `render_table` extracted each cell via `sel_cell_text`, which strips all inner tags to plain text -- the cell content never flowed through the main loop's inline-CSS machinery, so a `<span style="color:..">` or `<b>` in a cell was lost. A new `sel_cell_text_styled` does the same plain-text extraction but ALSO records, per output char, the colour / background / bold from the cell's own tags (`<b>`/`<strong>`, `style="color:"/"background[-color]:"`, `<font color/bgcolor>`, `<mark>`, and the monospace family `<code>`/`<kbd>`/`<samp>`/`<tt>`), interning colours into the page palette exactly like the main parser. `render_table` (now also handed the `tcolor`/`tbold` arrays) captures those per-column attributes as it lays the cell text into the row buffer and, right after the row is emitted 1:1 into the text stream (the same point v5.9.170 paints cell backgrounds), writes them into `tcolor`/`tbgcol`/`tbold`; an inline background overrides the cell-level `<td bgcolor>` band where both are present. `wrap_text` then carries them into the draw exactly like inline `<span>` styling. It is additive and confined to the table path: the width-measurement, caption and nested-table passes still use the plain `sel_cell_text`, and a table whose cells have no inline styling emits byte-for-byte as before (zero regression). **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a bordered status table served over http, screenshotted): `<span style="color:#3fb950">OK</span>` renders green, `#f85149` DOWN red, `#d29922` WARN amber, a `<span style="background:#1f6feb;color:#fff">busy</span>` and a `<span style="background:#238636">live</span>` render as filled pills, `<b>primary</b>` bold, `<code>retry</code>` on grey, and `<mark>hot</mark>` black-on-yellow -- with the box-drawing borders and header row intact. (A cell's content is still laid out on a single truncated line; multi-line / nested-table cell content is a separate future step.) **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1699.4 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. |
| v5.9.181 | **Feature: Selene renders `<button>` as a filled pill that honours the button's own CSS `background`/`color` -- so a page's action buttons (a `Sponsor`/`Fork`/`Star` row, a green `<> Code` button) draw in their real colours instead of one hard-coded box, the way buttons look on a real site.** Selene already drew a `<button>` / submit `<input>` as a solid pill through the form-field draw overlay, but that overlay painted every button the same theme-purple and ignored the element's style. The submit-field draw now reads the button's interned CSS background and text colour -- carried in `bgcolor_of`/`color_of` at the pill's first cell, the same per-char inline-CSS channel a `<span style>` uses -- and fills the pill with them, falling back to the theme-purple pill with light text when the button is unstyled; so a `<button style="background:#238636;color:#fff">` renders as a green pill with white text while a plain `<button>` is unchanged. On the parse side the `<button>` arm was reworked to emit a clean one-space pill padding on each side (carrying the button's field id and colours) in place of the old `[ ` / ` ]` text brackets. It reuses the existing palette + field machinery with no new render state; an unstyled button renders as before and a page with no `<button>` is byte-for-byte unchanged (zero regression). **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a chrome-heavy repo page served over http, screenshotted): a header row of `Sponsor`/`Unpin`/`Watch`/`Fork`/`Star` buttons draws as theme-purple pills while the styled `<> Code` button draws as a green (#238636) pill with white text -- pixel-sampled 927 green cells where the old build painted the purple default. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1696.5 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. |
| v5.9.180 | **Feature: Selene renders `<select>` as an inline dropdown badge -- `[ option v]` -- showing the chosen `<option>` (the one carrying `selected`, else the first) plus a CP437 down-triangle marker, so a form's drop-downs appear with their current value instead of being dropped along with the whole option list.** A new self-contained `<select>` open handler (modelled on the `<audio>`/`<progress>` arms) scans the element's content to its matching close tag: it walks the `<option>` tags, takes the text of the first one that carries the boolean `selected` attribute (detected via `sel_attr_present`), or the first option's text if none is selected, trims surrounding whitespace, and emits `[` + the option text + the down-triangle `0x1F` + `]` inline, with the badge's internal spaces written as the non-breaking sentinel `0xFF` so it never word-wraps apart. The remaining `<option>` list is skipped up to `</select>`. It is self-contained -- one tag arm that only emits characters and advances the parse cursor, no wrap/draw/table change -- so every other page renders byte-for-byte as before (zero regression). **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a page served over http, screenshotted): a `<select>` whose second `<option selected>` is `Medium` draws `[ Medium v]` (the selected one, not the first), a select with `<option selected>Spain</option>` draws `[ Spain v]`, and a select with no `selected` falls back to `[ First v]`. This is the 25th and final increment of the v5.9.156-180 Selene real-page-fidelity batch. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1696.3 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. |
| v5.9.179 | **Feature: Selene renders `<kbd>` as a bracketed keycap -- `[Key]` -- so keyboard shortcuts in documentation look like keys and are visually distinct from inline `<code>`, which shares the same grey background but is not bracketed.** `<kbd>` already got the subtle grey code background (shared with `<code>`/`<samp>`/`<tt>`); the change adds a small paired-emit arm (modelled on the `<q>` quotation-mark handler) that writes a `[` when `<kbd>` opens and a `]` when it closes. The brackets are emitted with the default background (`tbgcol` 0) while the key text between them keeps the grey background from the style stack, so a key reads as a plain-bracketed grey cap `[Ctrl]`. It is confined to a single new tag arm that only emits one character and advances the parse cursor -- no wrap, draw or state change beyond the two brackets -- and a page with no `<kbd>` renders byte-for-byte as before (zero regression). **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a page served over http, screenshotted): `Press <kbd>Ctrl</kbd>+<kbd>C</kbd>` draws `[Ctrl]+[C]`, `<kbd>Esc</kbd>`/`<kbd>Enter</kbd>`/`<kbd>Alt</kbd>`/`<kbd>Tab</kbd>` all draw as bracketed grey caps, and an adjacent `<code>make build</code>` shows the same grey background WITHOUT brackets -- keeping code and keycaps distinct. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1695.4 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. |
| v5.9.178 | **Feature: Selene renders the italic/emphasis family -- `<i>`, `<em>`, `<cite>`, `<var>`, `<dfn>` -- with an underline, the classic text-mode substitute for italics (as used by man/less/w3m), so emphasized words, book titles, variables and defined terms are now visibly set off instead of being indistinguishable from body text.** The NyxOS font has no italic glyphs, so these five tags previously rendered as plain text and their emphasis was lost. A one-line addition to the inline style-parse arm maps them onto the existing underline render-state (`cur_ul`), pushed and popped on the style stack exactly like `<u>`/`<ins>`. Because links carry both an underline AND a distinct blue colour, an emphasised run (underline, default colour) stays visually distinct from a hyperlink (underline, blue) and from `<strong>` (bold). It reuses the underline machinery with no new state and no wrap/draw change, and a page without these tags renders byte-for-byte as before (zero regression). **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a page served over http, screenshotted): `<em>`, `<i>`, `<cite>`, `<var>` and `<dfn>` runs all draw underlined, and a comparison line shows `<strong>` bold, `<em>` underlined, a link underlined-and-blue and plain text all clearly different. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1694.9 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. |
| v5.9.177 | **Feature: Selene draws a vertical bar (CP437 `0xB3` "│") down the left margin of a `<blockquote>`, the classic browser quote-border, with one bar per nesting level -- so quoted passages read as quotes at a glance and nested quotes show their depth, instead of relying on indentation alone.** The `<blockquote>` left margin is synthesized in `wrap_text` (each fresh or wrapped line at nesting level L is prefixed with L x `SEL_QUOTE_INDENT` spaces); the change makes that margin loop write a `0xB3` bar at the first column of every level's `SEL_QUOTE_INDENT`-wide block instead of a space (and clears those synthesized cells' colour so the bar draws in the default text colour). The bar therefore appears on every line of a quote including soft-wrapped continuation lines, and a level-2 quote shows two bars. It is a one-line change confined to the margin-fill loop -- no new state, no draw change -- and a page with no `<blockquote>` (indent level 0) renders byte-for-byte as before (zero regression). Note `<figure>`, which shares the same indent mechanism, likewise gains a left bar. **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a page served over http, screenshotted): a single `<blockquote>` shows one `│` bar on all three of its lines (including two wrapped lines), a nested `<blockquote>` shows two bars, and the surrounding normal paragraphs have no bar. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1694.7 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. |
| v5.9.176 | **Feature: Selene renders `<input type=checkbox>` and `<input type=radio>` as a state glyph -- `[x]`/`[ ]` for a checkbox and `(*)`/`( )` for a radio, filled when the `checked` attribute is present -- so a form's tick-boxes and option buttons are visible with their state instead of being silently dropped.** Until now the `<input>` handler only rendered text fields (an underline run) and submit buttons; checkbox/radio/file/etc. fell into the `kind == -1` branch and emitted nothing. A short arm added before the kind logic detects `type=checkbox`/`type=radio`, reads the boolean `checked` attribute via `sel_attr_present`, and emits a space-padded ASCII glyph inline (`[x]`/`[ ]` or `(*)`/`( )`) then advances past the tag. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. |
| v5.9.175 | **Feature: Selene renders `<audio>` and `<video>` as a small inline placeholder box -- `[<music-note> audio: name]` / `[<play-triangle> video: name]` -- so a page with an embedded media player shows a labelled, named badge (with the clip's file name) instead of silently dropping the element and its fallback text.** A new `<audio>`/`<video>` open handler (self-contained, modelled on `<progress>`/`<details>`) reads the element's `src`; if it has none it scans its children for the first `<source src>`; it takes the basename after the last `/`, then emits `[` + the CP437 glyph (music note `0x0E` for audio, play triangle `0x10` for video) + the label + `: name` + `]`, inline in the text flow, bold-inheriting the current colour, with the internal spaces written as the non-breaking sentinel `0xFF` so the badge never word-wraps apart. The element's fallback content (and any `<source>`/`<track>` children) is then skipped up to the matching close tag. | v5.9.173 | **Feature: Selene renders the HTML5 `<progress>` and `<meter>` elements as inline CP437 block bars with a percentage, so a page that shows a download bar, a disk-usage gauge or a score now draws a real filled/empty bar instead of dropping the element.** A new `<progress>`/`<meter>` open handler (modelled on the self-contained `<details>` arm) reads the `value`, `max` and (for `<meter>`) `min` attributes -- parsed by a new `sel_parse_milli` helper that turns a decimal like `70` or `0.7` into thousandths, so fractional values work without floating point -- clamps the value into range, computes the filled fraction in per-mille, and emits a 16-cell bar (filled = the full block `0xDB`, empty = the light shade `0xB0`) followed by ` NN%`, inline in the text flow and inheriting the current colour. The element's fallback text is then skipped up to its matching close tag. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. |
| v5.9.172 | **Feature: Selene's HTML-entity table gains the Greek letters (`&alpha;&beta;&pi;&mu;&Sigma;&sigma;&Omega;&delta;&phi;&tau;&Theta;&Gamma;&epsilon;`) and the common accented Latin-1 letters (`&aacute;&eacute;&iacute;&oacute;&uacute;&ntilde;&Ntilde;&agrave;&egrave;&ccedil;&Ccedil;&uuml;&ouml;&auml;&Uuml;&Ouml;&Auml;`), each rendered as its real CP437 glyph -- so science/maths pages and Spanish/French/German pages that spell accented words with entities now render the actual letters instead of the old `?` fallback.** The NyxOS console font is the standard CP437 VGA set, whose 0xE0-0xEE block holds the Greek glyphs and whose 0x80-0xA5 block holds the accented Latin letters, so each new `sel_entity_t` maps the entity's Unicode code point to the matching CP437 byte (e.g. `pi`->0xE3, `Omega`->0xEA, `ntilde`->0xA4, `eacute`->0x82, `uuml`->0x81). Because `decode_entity` looks the table up by both name and code point, the numeric forms (`&#960;`, `&#241;`) resolve to the same glyph as the named ones. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. |
| v5.9.171 | **Feature: Selene honours `<col bgcolor>` / `<colgroup bgcolor>` (and their `style="background:..."`), giving a whole table column a default background, and completing the background cascade cell > row > column to mirror the alignment cascade Selene already had (cell align > row align > column align).** Building straight on v5.9.170's cell/row backgrounds and v5.9.163's `<col>` alignment plumbing: Pass 1 now keeps a `col_bg[]` array alongside `col_align[]`; the leading `<col>`/`<colgroup>` scan parses a `bgcolor` (or `style` background) exactly as it already parsed `align`, interns it into the page palette and fills the same span of columns (a `<colgroup>` with neither align nor background stays a transparent container whose `<col>` children fill). | v5.9.164 | **Feature: Selene treats `&nbsp;` (and the numeric `&#160;` / `&#xA0;`) as a true non-breaking space -- it renders as a blank but word-wrap never splits on it, so units like "10 kg", "Mr. Smith" or "price is 42 EUR" stay together on one line.** Until now `&nbsp;` decoded to an ordinary 0x20 space, so the wrapper broke on it exactly like any other space. Now `decode_entity` maps it to byte 0xFF, whose font glyph is blank (all-zero, verified in `drivers/video/font.c`) and which `wrap_text` treats as an ordinary word character (its break scan only splits on 0x20 / newline) -- so an `&nbsp;`-joined run is one unbreakable "word" that renders as spaced text. The draw is safe because `font_draw_char` indexes the 256-glyph table through `unsigned char`, so 0xFF maps to the blank glyph with no out-of-bounds. It is a one-line data change (plus the matching U+00A0 numeric fallback); no wrap or draw code changed, so ordinary spaces are unaffected (zero regression). **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a page served over http, screenshotted): `10&nbsp;kg` / `Mr.&nbsp;Smith` / `section&nbsp;3.2` render with visible spaces, and at a wrap boundary an `&nbsp;`-joined `price is 42 EUR` moves to the next line as a whole unit while the identical text with normal spaces splits "42" from "EUR" across two lines. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1687.3 KB (unchanged). Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. |
| v5.9.163 | **Feature: Selene honours column-default table alignment -- `<col align="right"|"center">` (or `<colgroup align>`) sets a default alignment for every cell in that column, completing the alignment cascade cell > row > column > left.** Building on per-cell (v5.9.159) and per-row (v5.9.161) alignment, render_table's Pass 1 now scans the leading `<col>`/`<colgroup>` elements into a `col_align[]` array: each `<col>` is positional (consuming its `span`, default 1), a `<colgroup align>` with its `span` fills that many columns, and a plain `<colgroup>` with no align is a transparent container whose `<col>` children fill the columns. A cell's alignment then starts from its column's default, an explicit `<tr align>` (center/right) overrides the column, and an explicit `<td>`/`<th>` align= overrides both -- so the precedence is cell > row > col. It stays confined to Pass 1 (the emit is unchanged, reading the resolved per-cell `align` byte), so geometry/borders are untouched and a table with no col/row/cell alignment renders byte-for-byte as before (zero regression). **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a page served over http, screenshotted): a table with `<colgroup><col><col align=right><col align=center></colgroup>` renders its name column left, price column right (3 / 250 flush right) and qty column centred, while a final `<tr align="center">` row centres its price cell (99) instead of right-aligning it -- proving the row overrides the column. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1686.6 -> 1687.3 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. |
| v5.9.162 | **Feature: Selene renders `<details>`/`<summary>` disclosure widgets -- a closed `<details>` shows only its `<summary>` line (content hidden, `[+]` marker), while `<details open>` shows the summary plus its content (`[-]` marker), matching a browser's collapsed/expanded state.** Modern pages (docs, FAQs, changelogs) lean on `<details>`; Selene used to dump the summary and all the hidden content as a run-on. Now a `<details>` open tag checks for the boolean `open` attribute (via `sel_attr_present`): without it, the element is collapsed -- a nesting-aware scan finds the matching `</details>`, the first `<summary>`..`</summary>` text is extracted (via `sel_cell_text`, default `Details` if none) and emitted BOLD behind a `[+]` marker, and the entire subtree is then skipped (the same technique `display:none` uses), so the hidden paragraphs/lists never reach the stream and list/quote/style bookkeeping stays balanced. With `open`, the element renders normally and its `<summary>` gets a bold `[-]` marker on its own line; the content follows. Block breaks wrap the widget. **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a page served over http, screenshotted): a collapsed `<details>` shows `[+] Collapsed section` with its paragraph and `<ul>` correctly hidden, an adjacent `<details open>` shows `[-] Open section` followed by its visible paragraph, and the text around both renders normally. SCOPE: static render (no click-to-toggle); the marker is a text glyph pair, not a triangle. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1685.6 -> 1686.6 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. |
| v5.9.161 | **Feature: Selene honours row-level table alignment -- `<tr align="right"|"center">` (or `<tr style="text-align:...">`) sets a default alignment for every cell in that row, which an individual `<td>`/`<th>` can still override.** Building on v5.9.159's per-cell alignment, `render_table`'s Pass 1 now reads a `<tr>`'s `align`/`text-align` into a `cur_row_align`, and each cell in the row starts from that default; a cell's own `align=`/`text-align` (including an explicit `left`) overrides it, and a plain `<tr>` leaves the default at left. It is confined to Pass 1 (the cell-collection loop) -- Pass 2's emit is unchanged and reads the already-resolved per-cell `align` byte, so the geometry, borders and cellpadding are untouched and a table with no row/cell alignment renders byte-for-byte as before (zero regression). **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a page served over http, screenshotted): a 5-row table renders a centred header row, a right-aligned row (1 / 22 / 333 flush right), a left default row, a `style`-centred row, and a right-aligned row whose middle cell overrides back to left -- all correctly. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1685.3 -> 1685.6 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. |
| v5.9.160 | **Feature: Selene renders `text-decoration:overline` -- a rule along the TOP of the text, completing the decoration family (underline at the baseline, line-through at mid-height, overline at the top).** The per-char text-flags byte (bit0 bold, bit1 underline, bit2 line-through, bit3 dotted, bits4-5 sub/sup) had bits 6-7 free; overline takes bit6. A new `cur_ol` render-state plus a matching `ol` field on the style stack are pushed / popped / inherited exactly like underline and strike, and `text-decoration:overline` (as well as `none`, which now also clears overline) is parsed in the same arm as the existing decorations. The draw adds one `fb_fill_rect` rule at the glyph-cell top per overlined run. It composes with colour and the other decorations (an overlined crimson span renders both the hue and the rule), and because it is a pure additive bit, un-decorated text is byte-for-byte unchanged. **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a page served over http, screenshotted): an inline overlined span shows a top rule, an `underline` / `line-through` / `overline` trio renders three rules at three distinct heights, and an overlined crimson span carries both the colour and the top rule. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1685.1 -> 1685.3 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. |
| v5.9.159 | **Feature: Selene aligns table cells -- `<td>`/`<th>` with `align="right"|"center"` or `style="text-align:right|center"` now place their text within the column instead of always flush-left, so numeric columns line up on the right.** `render_table`'s cell struct (`sel_tcell_t`) gains an `align` byte; Pass 1 reads each cell's `align=` attribute (or a `text-align` declaration in its inline `style`) into it, and Pass 2 distributes the column's spare width accordingly -- left (default) pads on the right (unchanged), right pads entirely on the left, center splits the padding. It composes with everything render_table already does (colspan span-width, cellpadding, borderless, caption): the alignment only changes WHERE the text sits inside the cell's content width, never the geometry, so borders/pipes/rules stay aligned and a table with no aligned cells renders byte-for-byte as before (zero regression). Per-cell only (a `<tr align>` / `<col align>` column default is not read). **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a page served over http, screenshotted): a 3-column price table renders the Item column left, the Price column right-aligned (3 / 250 / 1500 flush right), and the Qty column centered -- with both the `align=` attribute (two rows) and `style="text-align"` (one row) exercised. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1685.2 -> 1685.1 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. |
| v5.9.158 | **Feature: Selene's CSS colour parser grows from ~28 named colours to ~90 -- real pages that use `color:tomato`, `steelblue`, `rebeccapurple`, `hotpink`, `chocolate`, `khaki`, ... now render in the right hue instead of falling back to the default text colour.** `sel_parse_css_color`'s `NAMED[]` table gains ~60 standard CSS colour keywords across greys/neutrals (lightgray, dimgray, slategray, gainsboro, whitesmoke...), blues (skyblue, dodgerblue, steelblue, royalblue, cornflowerblue, midnightblue...), greens (lightgreen, forestgreen, limegreen, seagreen, chartreuse, olivedrab...), reds/oranges/pinks (tomato, orangered, coral, salmon, hotpink, deeppink...), yellows/browns/tans (khaki, tan, wheat, chocolate, sienna, goldenrod, saddlebrown...), purples/violets (violet, orchid, plum, blueviolet, mediumpurple, rebeccapurple, lavender...) and cyans/teals (turquoise, aquamarine, darkcyan, lightseagreen...). It is pure data -- the lookup loop and the palette-interning path are unchanged, so there is no control-flow risk, and an unknown keyword still falls through to the default colour. The keywords apply anywhere the inline-CSS path already reads `color`/`background`, so both `<span style="color:X">` and `<span style="background:X">` benefit. **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a page served over http, screenshotted): a dozen new keywords (tomato / steelblue / forestgreen / hotpink / goldenrod / turquoise / dodgerblue / chocolate / rebeccapurple / crimson / darkorange / mediumseagreen) render as distinct correct hues, four background swatches (khaki / lightblue / salmon / palegreen) fill correctly, and an unknown name renders in the default colour. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1683.2 -> 1685.2 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. |
| v5.9.157 | **Feature: Selene hides `display:none` / `visibility:hidden` / `hidden`-attribute elements -- their entire subtree is dropped from the render instead of leaking as visible text.** Real pages carry hidden templates, screen-reader-only text and JS-toggled blocks; Selene used to dump all of it into the page. Now, right after the `<script>`/`<style>` skip, an open non-void tag whose inline `style` declares `display:none` or `visibility:hidden` (or which carries the boolean `hidden` attribute) triggers a nesting-aware scan to its matching close tag -- the whole subtree is skipped in one pass (the same technique `<script>`/`<style>` already use), so no text, list, table or style state from inside it is ever emitted. A new `sel_is_void_tag` guard means an `img`/`input`/`br`/`hr`/... is never treated as a hideable container (a void element has no close tag, so skipping to one would swallow the rest of the page), and a self-closed tag is left alone. Because the skip is symmetric (the hidden open AND its matching close are both consumed), the surrounding `listdepth`/`quote_depth`/style-stack bookkeeping stays balanced -- a hidden `<div style="display:none"><ul><li>...</ul></div>` leaves the following content rendering normally. Inline hides work too: a `<span style="display:none">` drops out of the line without breaking the flow. SCOPE: inline `style=` / the `hidden` attribute only -- Selene has no `<style>`-block cascade, so a class-based `.hidden{display:none}` is not caught. **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a page served over http, screenshotted): a `display:none` `<p>`, a `hidden` `<div>`, a `visibility:hidden` `<p>` and a nested `display:none` block (with its `<ul>`/`<li>`) all vanish, while the paragraphs around them and an inline `visible-A ... visible-B` line (with an `INLINE-HIDDEN` span removed) render correctly. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1682.1 -> 1683.2 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. |
| v5.9.156 | **Feature: Selene honours `<ol reversed>` -- an ordered list numbers DOWNWARD (3, 2, 1) instead of upward, the first item equal to the list's item count.** The `<ol>` open arm now detects the boolean `reversed` attribute (new word-bounded `sel_attr_present` helper, so a `class="reversed-x"` is not a false match) and, when present, pre-counts the list's top-level `<li>` (new `sel_count_li`, scanning the tag stream while tracking `<ol>`/`<ul>` nesting so items in a nested list are excluded) to seed the counter; each `<li>` then steps the counter DOWN rather than up (clamped at 1, never wrapping below). It composes with the existing ordered-list machinery: `reversed start="10"` counts 10, 9, 8; `reversed type="A"` gives C, B, A; a `<li value="N">` inside a reversed list restarts the descent at N. A plain (non-reversed) `<ol>` is byte-for-byte unchanged -- the forward `++counter` path is untouched, so zero regression -- and a `<ul>` ignores `reversed`. **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a page served over http, screenshotted): a plain reversed list renders 3/2/1, `reversed start="10"` renders 10/9/8, `reversed type="A"` renders C/B/A, and a normal control list still counts up 1/2/3. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1681.2 -> 1682.1 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. This opens the v5.9.156+ Selene real-page-fidelity batch (safe, one-code-path, verifiable increments). |
| v5.9.155 | **Feature: Selene honours `text-align` on a `<figcaption>` -- `center`/`right` place the figure's caption line, completing the caption-alignment pair with v5.9.154.** v5.9.149 always left-aligned the figcaption (its own line under the figure body); now the `<figcaption>` open arm reads a `text-align` declaration off the tag's `style` and sets the current alignment for the caption's extent -- `center` -> `cur_align=1`, `right` -> `cur_align=2` -- so its line(s) carry that alignment into `wrap_text`'s per-line `line_align`, and the draw shifts the caption line to the centre or right of the content width; the matching close restores left alignment so the figure body and following text stay flush. It reuses the same per-line `talign`/`line_align` machinery `<center>`/`text-align` already drive, and the figure body keeps its block indent, so only the caption moves. **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a page served over http, screenshotted): three figures render a left (default), a centred, and a right-aligned `<figcaption>` while each figure body stays left-indented as a block. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1681.0 -> 1681.2 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`.
