This closes the v5.9.129-155 Selene styling batch; the next larger step (deferred) is real `<h1>..<h6>` sizes, which needs a scaled-font blit primitive + variable line-height touching scroll/layout. |
| v5.9.154 | **Feature: Selene honours `text-align` on a table `<caption>` -- `left`/`right` override the centred default, placing the title at the box's left or right edge.** v5.9.145 always centred the caption over the table (falling back to left when it was wider than the box); now `render_table` reads a `text-align` declaration off the same `<caption>` `style` it already scans for `caption-side`, and picks the caption's leading pad from it: `text-align:left` -> pad 0 (flush left), `text-align:right` -> pad `total-w` (flush right against the box's right edge), and `center`/unset -> the existing `(total-w)/2` centre. It reuses the caption line the code already builds, so the choice composes with `caption-side:top`/`bottom` (the same padded line is emitted above or below the box), and a caption wider than the table still clamps to the left as before. Confined to render_table's caption emit -- rows, rules and cells are untouched. **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a page served over http, screenshotted): over one wide four-column table shape, a `text-align:left` caption sits at the left edge, the default caption is centred, and a `text-align:right` caption sits at the right edge. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1680.9 -> 1681.0 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. Next feature ideas: `<figcaption>` text-align; `<ol reversed>`; real `<h1>..<h6>` sizes (deferred -- needs a scaled font + variable line-height). |
| v5.9.153 | **Feature: Selene honours `<li value="N">` -- an ordered-list item can restart the numbering at N mid-list, and the items after it keep counting from there.** This is the per-item companion to v5.9.152's `<ol start>`: in the ordered-list branch of the `<li>` open handler, Selene now reads the `value` attribute off the `<li>` tag and, if present, sets the list's running counter to `N-1` (clamped 1..9999) just before the `++` that produces the marker -- so that item shows N and each following item increments from N. Because it drives the same single counter, `<li value>` composes with everything already there: a `value="10"` item in a plain list jumps to 10 then continues 11, 12; it stacks on `<ol start="100">` (a mid-list `value="200"` then continues 201); and it respects the list's `type` (in a `type="A"` list, `value="5"` shows E). It is confined to the ordered arm of the `<li>` handler (a `<ul>` item ignores `value`) and reuses the existing `extract_attr` + counter, so nothing else changes. **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a page served over http, screenshotted): a list numbers 1, 2, 10, 11, 12; a `start="100"` list jumps to 200 then 201; and a `type="A"` list jumps from A to E to F. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1680.8 -> 1680.9 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. Next feature ideas: `<ol reversed>` (needs an item pre-count); `<caption>`/`<figcaption>` explicit text-align; real `<h1>..<h6>` sizes (deferred -- needs a scaled font + variable line-height). |
| v5.9.152 | **Feature: Selene honours `<ol start="N">` -- an ordered list can begin its numbering at N instead of 1.** The `<ol>` open handler already parsed `list-style-type`/`type=` and seeded the list's running counter to 0; now, for an ordered list, it also reads the `start` attribute off the same tag and seeds the counter to `N-1` (clamped to a sane 1..9999, otherwise the default), so the first `<li>` shows N and each subsequent item increments from there. Because the marker is produced from that one counter, `start` composes automatically with every `<ol>` numbering style: `start="5"` on a decimal list begins at 5, `type="A" start="3"` begins at C, and `type="i" start="4"` begins at iv. It is confined to the `<ol>` open arm (a `<ul>` ignores `start`), touches nothing else, and reuses the existing `extract_attr` + counter machinery. **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a page served over http, screenshotted): a plain list numbers 1/2/3, a `start="5"` list numbers 5/6/7, a `type="A" start="3"` list letters C/D/E, and a `type="i" start="4"` list begins at iv. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1680.6 -> 1680.8 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. Next feature ideas: `<ol reversed>` (needs an item pre-count); `<li value="N">`; real `<h1>..<h6>` sizes (deferred -- needs a scaled font + variable line-height). |
| v5.9.151 | **Feature: Selene honours a table's `cellpadding` attribute -- the space inside each cell widens by that many columns per side.** Selene tables were fixed at one space of padding on each side of a cell; now the `<table>` dispatch reads `cellpadding="N"` (clamped 0..8) and threads it into `render_table` alongside the v5.9.150 `has_border` flag. Inside, a single `pad` value replaces the hard-coded padding everywhere the geometry is computed: the per-column total width (`colw + 2*pad + 1`), the `+--+` rule dash count (`colw + 2*pad`), the colspan span widths (`(2*pad+1)*(cs-1) + cols`), and the per-row left/right in-cell spaces are emitted as `pad`-length runs -- so the rules, pipes and cell text all stay aligned at any padding. Crucially `pad` defaults to 1, at which every one of these expressions reduces to the previous constant, so a table with no `cellpadding` renders byte-for-byte as before (no regression); it composes with borderless tables and colspans unchanged. **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a page served over http, screenshotted): the default planets table is unchanged, the same table with `cellpadding="4"` shows visibly wider cells with the `+--+` rules and `\|` separators still perfectly aligned, and `cellpadding="0"` packs the cells tight. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1680.2 -> 1680.6 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. Next feature ideas: `<caption>`/`<figcaption>` explicit text-align; `<ol start=N>`; real `<h1>..<h6>` sizes (deferred -- needs a scaled font + variable line-height). |
| v5.9.150 | **Feature: Selene draws a `<table border="0">` (or `style="border:none"`) as a borderless table -- the same aligned columns, but with no `+--+` rules and no `\|` separators.** Until now every table was boxed. The `<table>` dispatch now reads the tag's `border` attribute (any all-zero value = borderless) and its `style` (a `border:none`/`border:0` declaration) and threads a `has_border` flag into `render_table`. Inside, the three horizontal rules (top, header separator, bottom) are emitted only when `has_border`, and the per-row column separator is a single character `bch` that is `\|` when boxed and a space when borderless -- so a borderless row is exactly the same column-aligned layout with the pipes turned into spaces, keeping every column width and the caption centring identical. A table with no `border` attribute stays boxed exactly as before, so nothing regresses; `<th>` header cells still upper-case, marking the header row without a rule. **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a page served over http, screenshotted): a default planets table renders its full box, the same table with `border="0"` renders as clean aligned columns with no box or pipes, and a `style="border:none"` table is borderless too. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1679.9 -> 1680.2 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. Next feature ideas: `<figcaption>`/`<caption>` centring toggles; `cellpadding`; real `<h1>..<h6>` sizes (deferred -- needs a scaled font + variable line-height). |
| v5.9.149 | **Feature: Selene lays out `<figure>` and `<figcaption>` properly -- the whole figure is indented as a block and its caption sits on its own line beneath the content.** Previously `<figure>` was only a plain paragraph break (lumped into `is_block_tag`) and `<figcaption>` had no handling at all, so it ran on inline right after the figure's content with no separation. Now `<figure>` has a dedicated arm that, like `<blockquote>`, brackets the block with a paragraph break and bumps `quote_depth` for its extent, so `wrap_text` indents every line of the figure by `SEL_QUOTE_INDENT` -- mirroring the left/right margin a browser gives a `<figure>`. `<figcaption>` gets its own arm that forces a single line break, so the caption drops onto its own line directly under the figure body while still inheriting the figure's indent (its content carries the same `quote_depth`). `<figure>` was removed from the `is_block_tag` list since its dedicated arm now runs first; nothing else in layout or wrapping changes and the mechanism is the proven blockquote-indent path. **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a page served over http, screenshotted): two figures each render their body indented as a block with the `<figcaption>` on its own line beneath, while the ordinary paragraphs before, between and after them stay flush at the left margin. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1679.8 -> 1679.9 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. Next feature ideas: table `border="0"` (borderless spacing-only layout); `<figcaption>` centring via style; real `<h1>..<h6>` sizes (deferred -- needs a scaled font + variable line-height). |
| v5.9.148 | **Feature: Selene honours CSS `caption-side` on a table `<caption>` -- `caption-side:bottom` moves the title below the box instead of above it.** v5.9.145 always drew the caption above the top rule (HTML's default `caption-side:top`); now, when the `<caption>` tag carries `style="caption-side:bottom"`, the same centred title is emitted just below the table's bottom `+---+---+` rule instead. `render_table` reads the property off the `<caption>` tag (its byte range is captured during the existing caption scan) via `sel_css_get`/`sel_ci_streq` -- both forward-declared before `render_table`, since they are defined later in the file. The centred caption line is now built once into a `capline` buffer and emitted at whichever end `caption-side` selects (top by default, bottom when requested), so the two placements share one formatting path and the centring/left-align-when-wider logic is unchanged. Everything else about the table (rows, header rule, spans) is untouched. **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a page served over http, screenshotted): a default table shows its caption above the box while a second table with `caption-side:bottom` shows the identical caption below the box, and the following paragraph returns to the left margin. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1679.3 -> 1679.8 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. Next feature ideas: table `border="0"` (borderless spacing-only layout); `<figure>`/`<figcaption>`; real `<h1>..<h6>` sizes (deferred -- needs a scaled font + variable line-height). |
| v5.9.147 | **Feature: Selene honours CSS `text-indent` on block elements -- the block's first line is pushed in, and only that line.** A block that carries `style="text-indent:Npx"` or `Nem`/`Nrem` (e.g. `<p style="text-indent:4em">`, or on a `<div>`) now begins its content with that many blank cells; a new helper `sel_parse_indent` maps the length to whole 8px character cells (px or unitless rounded to the nearest cell; `1em`/`1rem` = the 16px font = 2 cells; a fractional part is ignored and a negative value clamps to 0). The spaces are emitted at the block open right after its paragraph break -- a hard line start -- and `wrap_text` deliberately keeps leading spaces there (`bol==1`) while dropping them on soft-wrapped continuations, so the indent lands on the first line only and every wrapped line returns to the left margin, exactly like a browser. It reuses the existing literal-leading-space mechanism (`<li>`/`<dd>` already do this) and is confined to the `is_block_tag` open arm, so nothing else in layout/wrap/click handling changes; the indent spaces inherit the current colour/background and the blockquote margin so they compose with a quoted or coloured block. **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a page served over http, screenshotted): a plain paragraph stays flush left, a `text-indent:4em` paragraph and a `text-indent:24px` paragraph each indent only their first line (by 8 and 3 cells) with the wrapped remainder flush left, and an `8em` `<div>` indents its first line by 16 cells. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1678.8 -> 1679.3 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. Next feature ideas: `<caption>` `caption-side:bottom`; table `border`/`cellpadding` hints; real `<h1>..<h6>` sizes (deferred -- needs a scaled font + variable line-height). |
| v5.9.146 | **Feature: Selene cycles the bullet glyph of a plain nested `<ul>` by depth -- disc, then circle, then square -- matching a browser's default nested-list markers.** Before, an unstyled `<ul>` drew the disc bullet at *every* nesting level, so a two- or three-deep list looked flat. Now, when a `<ul>` carries no explicit `list-style-type`, the `<li>` marker is chosen from the list's nesting depth: level 1 stays the disc (`0xF9`), level 2 becomes the hollow circle (`0xF8`), and level 3 and deeper become the filled square (`0xFE`) -- the same disc/circle/square progression browsers apply through their `ul`/`ul ul`/`ul ul ul` UA rules. An explicit `list-style-type:circle|square|none` still wins on the element it is set on, at every level, and ordered `<ol>` numbering is untouched. The change is confined to the unordered-bullet arm of the `<li>` handler (the glyph pick already read `liststk[listdepth-1].type`; it now also consults `listdepth` for the unset case), so layout, wrapping, indentation and click/link overlays are unchanged. **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a page served over http, screenshotted + zoomed): a three-level food list renders disc at the top level, circle for its sub-items, and square for the deepest items, while a `list-style-type:square` list stays square on the items it styles. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1678.8 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. Next feature ideas: CSS `letter-spacing` (careful -- fixed char grid); `<caption>` `caption-side:bottom`; real `<h1>..<h6>` sizes (deferred -- needs a scaled font + variable line-height). |
| v5.9.145 | **Feature: Selene renders a `<table>`'s `<caption>` as a real title, centred above the boxed table.** Until now the caption text was silently dropped -- `render_table` only ever scanned for `<tr>`/`<td>`/`<th>`, so a `<caption>` and its text vanished, leaving data tables untitled. Now `render_table` scans its byte range for a top-level `<caption>` (its `<table>`-nesting depth is tracked so a caption belonging to a nested table is skipped, and only the caption of *this* table is used), pulls the caption's plain text through the same tag-stripping/entity-decoding/whitespace-collapsing helper the cells use, and emits it on its own line just above the top `+---+---+` rule -- HTML's default `caption-side:top`. The title is centred over the table's computed total width when it is narrower than the box, and falls back to a left-aligned line at the margin when it is wider, so it always sits cleanly with the table. It is emitted as plain centred text (no change to `render_table`'s signature, so the colour/field/image grids are untouched), which keeps the whole feature confined to one code path. **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a page served over http, screenshotted): a "Quarterly sales by region" table and a "NyxOS release milestones" table each render their caption as a centred title above the box, and the text after the tables returns to the left margin. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1678.0 -> 1678.8 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. Next feature ideas: CSS `letter-spacing`; nested-list marker inheritance; real `<h1>..<h6>` sizes (deferred -- needs a scaled font + variable line-height). |
| v5.9.144 | **Feature: Selene renders `<dl>`/`<dt>`/`<dd>` as a real definition list — the term at the left margin, its description indented beneath it.** Until now `<dt>` and `<dd>` were lumped with `<br>`/`<tr>` as a plain line break, so a glossary read as a flat list. Now an open `<dd>` starts its description on a fresh line and prefixes it with a `SEL_QUOTE_INDENT` (4-space) left indent, while `<dt>` (and `</dd>`) stay a plain break so the term sits at the left margin -- giving the classic term-over-indented-definition look. `<dl>` was added to `is_block_tag` so the whole list gets a paragraph break around it (it was previously ignored, an inline no-op). The indent is emitted as literal leading spaces at the `<dd>` open (the same mechanism `<li>` uses), so it needs no matched close tag and never leaks state -- HTML's optional `</dd>` is handled correctly. **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a test page served over http, screenshotted): a `<dl>` glossary of "HTML", "CSS" and "Selene" shows each term flush left with its definition indented on the next line, and the paragraph after the list returns to the left margin. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1677.8 -> 1678.0 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. Next feature ideas: CSS `letter-spacing`; nested-list marker inheritance; real `<h1>..<h6>` sizes (deferred -- needs a scaled font + variable line-height). |
| v5.9.143 | **Feature: Selene honours CSS `text-transform` — `uppercase`, `lowercase`, and `capitalize` (plus `none`), applied per span with nesting.** The render-time style stack gained a `tt` field (inherited like the colour/decoration ones); a tag whose inline `style` sets `text-transform` pushes the transform and the matching close restores the parent, so a `<span style="text-transform:uppercase">` inside normal text upper-cases just that span and the text after it is untouched. The transform is applied to each content character as it is emitted (at the same two points the existing `<h1>`/`<h2>` upper-casing runs): `uppercase` upper-cases every letter, `lowercase` lower-cases every letter, and `capitalize` upper-cases only a letter at a word start -- detected with the parser's existing `last_space` flag, so the first letter after any space (or a line/block break) is capitalised and the rest of the word is left as written. **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a test page served over http, screenshotted): a `text-transform:uppercase` paragraph reads all-caps, a `lowercase` one all-lower, a `capitalize` one reads "This Line Capitalizes The First Letter Of Each Word", a plain paragraph is unchanged, and a `text-transform:uppercase` span inside a normal sentence upper-cases only itself and the rest stays mixed-case (nesting restore). **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1677.5 -> 1677.8 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. Next feature ideas: `<dl>`/`<dt>`/`<dd>` indenting; CSS `letter-spacing`; real `<h1>..<h6>` sizes (deferred -- needs a scaled font + variable line-height). |
| v5.9.142 | **Feature: Selene honours CSS `list-style-type` (and the legacy `type=` attribute) on `<ul>`/`<ol>` — real bullet glyphs and alphabetic/roman numbering.** The list-nesting stack gained a `type` byte parsed at each `<ul>`/`<ol>` open (from `style="list-style-type:X"`, falling back to the legacy `type=` attribute). For **`<ul>`** the `<li>` marker is now a real bullet glyph — `disc` (default, a filled bullet), `circle` (a hollow ring), `square` (a solid block), or `none` (no marker, indent only) — using the CP437 glyphs 0xF9/0xF8/0xFE (all printable, safely away from the control-code range so they never collide with `\n`/`\t`). For **`<ol>`** the counter is formatted by type: `decimal` (default), `lower-alpha`/`upper-alpha` (a,b,c... via a base-26 helper), and `lower-roman`/`upper-roman` (i,ii,iii... via a subtractive-notation Roman helper, 1..3999). Two small formatters (`sel_fmt_alpha`, `sel_fmt_roman`) were added; the default `<ul>` bullet changes from the old "- " hyphen to a proper disc. **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (test pages served over http, screenshotted): disc/circle/square bullets each render their glyph, `list-style-type:none` shows no marker; and `lower-alpha` (a. b. c.), `upper-alpha` (A. B. C.), `lower-roman` (i. ii. iii.) and `upper-roman` (I. II. III. IV. -- the IV proving the subtractive Roman logic) all number correctly. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1675.9 -> 1677.5 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. Next feature ideas: CSS `text-transform`; real `<h1>..<h6>` sizes via a scaled font (needs variable line-height -- a bigger design); nested-list marker inheritance. |
| v5.9.141 | **Feature: Selene's `<hr>` now takes a colour — `<hr style="color:red">` (or `border-color`) draws the rule in that colour instead of the default grey.** This completes the `<hr>` styling trilogy (v5.9.136 real rule, v5.9.140 width/align, now colour) and again adds no new per-line storage: a rule line's marker character has an unused `color_of[]` foreground slot, so the parsed colour is interned into the per-page palette and stamped there (`tcolor` on the marker), and the draw reads `color_of[line][0]` — a valid palette index paints the rule in that colour, 0 falls back to the fixed grey. The colour is taken from the `<hr>`'s inline `style="color:.."` (or `border-color`) via the same `sel_css_get` + `sel_parse_css_color` + `sel_intern_color` path every other element uses, so it accepts the ~28 named colours and `#rgb`/`#rrggbb`. It composes with width and alignment (all three live on the same marker char). **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a test page served over http, screenshotted): a default `<hr>` is grey, `color:red` is a red rule, `color:blue; width:50%` centered is a blue half-width rule in the middle, `border-color:green` is a green rule, and a closing `<hr>` is grey again — every colour correct and composing with width/align. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1675.7 -> 1675.9 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. **`<hr>` is now fully styleable** (rule, width, alignment, colour). Next feature ideas: real `<h1>..<h6>` size steps via `font_draw_string_scaled`; a `<small>`/`<big>` relative size; CSS `text-transform`. |
| v5.9.140 | **Feature: Selene's `<hr>` now honours width and alignment — `style="width:N%"` / the legacy `width="N"`/`width="N%"`, and `align=`/`text-align` (left/center/right).** This extends v5.9.136's real rule without adding any new per-line storage: the existing per-line `line_rule` byte (a boolean before) now holds the rule's **width as a percentage** (1-100; a plain `<hr>` = 100 = full width), and the existing `line_align` byte (already threaded through `wrap_text` via the `talign` lane for `text-align`) doubles as the rule's alignment. At the `<hr>` tag, the width is parsed from `style="width:N%"` or the `width` attribute — an `N%` value is the percentage, a bare `N` is treated as pixels and converted to a percentage of the content width — and the alignment from `align=` (or `text-align`); both are stamped onto the single marker character (`trule = width%`, `talign = alignment`) that already carries the rule through wrapping. In the draw pass the rule's pixel width is `avail * pct / 100` and its left edge is offset for centre/right exactly like text alignment, so a narrower rule sits where asked. **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a test page served over http, screenshotted): a default `<hr>` spans full width; `width:50%` left-aligned draws a half rule at the left; `width:50%` centered draws a half rule in the middle; `width="25%"` right-aligned draws a short rule at the right edge; and a closing `<hr>` is full width again — every width and position correct. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1674.9 -> 1675.7 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. Next feature ideas: a `<small>`/`<big>` relative size; real `<h1>..<h6>` size steps via `font_draw_string_scaled`; a `color:`-styled `<hr>` rule. |
| v5.9.139 | **Feature: Selene renders `<sub>` and `<sup>` — subscript and superscript, drawn as a same-size glyph shifted down / up.** This is Selene's first per-character *vertical* effect: bits 4-5 of the text-flags byte (bits 0-3 are bold/underline/strike/dotted) now hold a 2-bit vertical-offset value (0 = normal, 1 = subscript, 2 = superscript). The style-stack frame gained a `vo` field (inherited like the others); `<sub>` sets it to 1, `<sup>` to 2, and the matching close restores the parent. Each content character records `... | (vo<<4)` into the flags lane, so a run of constant flags — which the draw pass already isolates — carries its offset; the draw decodes `vo` and shifts that run's whole draw (glyph, the synthetic-bold pass, and any underline/strike/dotted rule) by `±FONT_HEIGHT/4` (4px on the 16px font): down for a subscript, up for a superscript. The glyph is not shrunk (Selene has one bitmap font), matching how many terminals render sub/sup. **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a test page served over http, screenshotted + a 3x zoom): `H<sub>2</sub>O` and `CO<sub>2</sub>` show the 2 dropped below the baseline; `mc<sup>2</sup>` and `2<sup>10</sup>` show the exponent raised above it; `n<sup>th</sup>` and `x<sub>i</sub>` mix on one line; and plain text stays on the baseline — all correct, no overlap with adjacent lines. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1674.8 -> 1674.9 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. **The text-flags byte now uses 6 of 8 bits** (bold/underline/strike/dotted + a 2-bit vertical offset). Next feature ideas: `<hr>` width/alignment via inline CSS; a `<small>`/`<big>` relative size; real `<h1>..<h6>` size steps via `font_draw_string_scaled`. |
| v5.9.138 | **Feature: Selene renders `<abbr>` with a DOTTED underline — a fourth text decoration, reusing v5.9.132's underline machinery.** `<abbr>` (an abbreviation, usually carrying a `title` tooltip) now draws its text with a dotted underline, the conventional browser cue. This adds **bit 3** to the per-char text-flags byte (bits 0/1/2 were bold/underline/strike from v5.9.131-133), so it costs no new grid: the style-stack frame gained a `du` field (inherited like `ul`/`st`), an open `<abbr>` sets it, and the matching close restores the parent — exactly like the other decorations. Each content character records `bold | (underline<<1) | (strike<<2) | (dotted<<3)` into the flags lane; the draw pass, which already breaks each line into runs of constant *(fg, bg, flags)*, decodes bit 3 and draws a **dashed** rule under the run — a 1px dot every 2px across the run width with `fb_fill_rect` (vs. the solid 1px rule the `<u>` underline uses). The `title` attribute is parsed away with the tag but not shown (Selene has no hover tooltips). **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a test page served over http, screenshotted + a 5x zoom of the comparison line): `<abbr>HTML</abbr>` and `<abbr>CSS</abbr>` get dotted underlines; a side-by-side `<u>solid underline</u>` vs `<abbr>abbr dotted underline</abbr>` shows the solid line continuous and the abbr line clearly dotted; a `<b><abbr>..</abbr></b>` keeps the dotted underline under bold text; and plain text has none. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1674.5 -> 1674.8 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. **The text-flags byte now uses 4 of 8 bits** (bold/underline/strike/dotted). Next feature ideas: `<sub>`/`<sup>` (a small glyph y-offset); `<hr>` width/alignment via inline CSS; real `<h1>..<h6>` size steps via `font_draw_string_scaled`. |
| v5.9.137 | **Feature: Selene renders the `<q>` inline-quotation tag — quotation marks are drawn around quoted text, and nested `<q>` alternate between double and single quotes.** `<q>...</q>` now emits a `"` at its open and a matching quote at its close, recorded into the text stream with the *enclosing* character styling, so a quote inside `<b>` or a coloured span comes out bold/coloured too. Nesting follows the standard typographic convention via a small `qmark_depth` counter: the outermost level uses double quotes (`"..."`), one level in uses single quotes (`'...'`), and so on alternating. The handler sits with the other inline-tag handlers (just before `<a>`): it emits the quote character carrying the current per-char lanes (link/field/colour/bg/text-flags/align/indent) and advances past the tag, so it composes with everything already parsed; a plain `<q>` never touches the style stack (only `<q style=..>` pushes/pops a frame, and it stays balanced). **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a test page served over http, screenshotted): `<q>hello there</q>` shows `"hello there"` in double quotes; a **nested** `<q>He told me <q>go away</q> firmly</q>` shows outer double + inner single quotes; a `<b><q>bold quotation</q></b>` shows **bold** quote marks and a `<q style="color:teal">` teal ones (styling inherited); and plain text with no `<q>` has no quotes at all. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1674.1 -> 1674.5 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. Next feature ideas: `<abbr title>` (a dotted underline reusing the decoration machinery); `<hr>` width/alignment via inline CSS; real `<h1>..<h6>` size steps via `font_draw_string_scaled` (variable line-height). |
| v5.9.136 | **Feature: Selene draws `<hr>` as a real horizontal rule — a thin drawn line across the content width, replacing the old row of 64 dashes.** Until now `<hr>` emitted a text line of sixty-four `-` characters; it now renders as a proper 2px grey rule. Because a rule is a property of the *line*, not of a character, this adds a small per-line mechanism mirroring v5.9.135's `line_align`: a per-line `uint8 line_rule[SEL_MAX_LINES]` array in the ctx, a `trule[]` per-char lane threaded through `wrap_text` (which stamps `line_rule[li]` at the two char-write sites exactly like `line_align`), and — at the `<hr>` tag — a single space marker character carrying `trule=1` between the two paragraph breaks (so the line survives word-wrap but shows no dashes). In the draw pass, before a line's runs are painted, a set `line_rule` short-circuits the row: it fills a 2px `fb_fill_rect` (`#969aa8`) centred vertically across `SELENE_W - 2*SEL_PAD` and `continue`s, so a rule line draws no text, links, form fields or find-highlights. **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a test page served over http, screenshotted): a page with an `<h2>` heading and three paragraphs separated by two `<hr>` tags renders **two clean thin horizontal rules** dividing the three text blocks — no dashes, correct spacing, everything else unchanged. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1673.9 -> 1674.1 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. Next feature ideas: `<hr>` width/alignment via inline CSS; `<abbr title>` / `<q>` (auto quotes); real `<h1>..<h6>` size steps via `font_draw_string_scaled` (variable line-height). |
| v5.9.135 | **Feature: Selene supports text alignment — `style="text-align:center\|right\|left"` and the `<center>` tag, applied per line (every wrapped line of a block aligns individually).** Unlike the v5.9.129-134 styling (all per-character), alignment is per-line, so this adds a new mechanism: a per-line `uint8 line_align[SEL_MAX_LINES]` array in the ctx (0 = left/default, 1 = center, 2 = right). The render-time style-stack frame gained an `al` field (inherited like the colour/bold ones); an open `<center>` — or a tag whose inline `style` sets `text-align` — sets the current alignment, recorded per character into a new `talign[]` lane that `wrap_text` uses to stamp each output line's `line_align` (so a centered paragraph that wraps onto several lines centers each line). In the draw pass, before a line's runs are painted, a `leftpad` is computed from its alignment and pixel width (`center: (avail - linewidth)/2`, `right: avail - linewidth`, `avail = SELENE_W - 2*SEL_PAD`) and added to every x-coordinate — the base-text runs AND the link, form-field and find-highlight overlays — so the whole line, decorations included, shifts together; left (0) adds nothing and is byte-for-byte unchanged. `<center>` was also added to `is_block_tag` so it forces the paragraph breaks a block element should, isolating its text on its own lines. **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a test page served over http, screenshotted): a `text-align:center` paragraph centers, `<center>` centers, `text-align:right` right-aligns, a default paragraph stays left, and a long `text-align:center` paragraph that wraps onto multiple lines has **each wrapped line individually centered** — all correct, left-aligned pages unchanged. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1673.2 -> 1673.9 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. Next feature ideas: real `<h1>..<h6>` size steps via `font_draw_string_scaled` (variable line-height); `<hr>` width/alignment; a simple `<table>` border via CSS. |
| v5.9.134 | **Feature: Selene maps common semantic inline tags onto its existing styling — `<mark>` (highlight), `<ins>` (underline), and `<code>`/`<kbd>`/`<samp>`/`<tt>` (a code background).** These reuse the v5.9.129-133 machinery rather than adding anything new: in the style-block open branch, a handful of tag-name checks set the same fg/bg/underline state the inline-CSS path already sets, so each tag pushes a style-stack frame on open and pops it on close (a highlight or code span nests inside coloured/bold text and restores the parent state exactly like a `<span style=..>`). Specifically **`<mark>`** interns black text on a yellow background (the browser default), **`<ins>`** turns on the underline flag (bit 1 — the counterpart to v5.9.133's `<del>` = strikethrough), and **`<code>`/`<kbd>`/`<samp>`/`<tt>`** intern a subtle grey (`#e6e6e6`) background so inline code stands out (the font is already fixed-width, so no font change is needed). **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a test page served over http, screenshotted): `<mark>` shows black-on-yellow; `<ins>` underlines; inline `<code>`, `<kbd>` and `<samp>` each get the grey code background; and a `<mark>` wrapping a `<b>` keeps the yellow highlight across the bolded word (nesting) — all correct, plain text unchanged. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1672.7 -> 1673.2 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. Next feature ideas: `text-align:center`/`<center>` (a new per-line-alignment mechanism); real `<h1>..<h6>` size steps via `font_draw_string_scaled`; `<abbr title>`/tooltip. |
| v5.9.133 | **Feature: Selene renders STRIKETHROUGH — `<s>`, `<del>`, `<strike>`, and `style="text-decoration:line-through"`, with nesting and a `text-decoration:none` override.** This uses **bit 2** of the per-char text-flags byte introduced in v5.9.131-132 (bit 0 = bold, bit 1 = underline, bit 2 = line-through), so it costs no new grid. The style-stack frame gained an `st` field (inherited like the others); an open `<s>`/`<del>`/`<strike>` — or `text-decoration:line-through` — turns strike on, `text-decoration:none` now clears both underline and strike, and the parent state is restored on the matching close. Content characters record `bold | (underline<<1) | (strike<<2)` into the flags lane; the draw pass decodes each run's flags and, for bit 2, draws a 1px rule through the middle of the run with `fb_fill_rect` at `py + FONT_HEIGHT/2` (the underline sits at the baseline, the strike at mid-height). **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a test page served over http, screenshotted): `<s>` and `<del>` and `text-decoration:line-through` all strike their text; **`<b><s>` is bold + struck** and **`<u><s>` is underlined + struck**; a **red `<s>`** is strike + colour; and a **`<s>` wrapping a `text-decoration:none` span** shows that span clean and resumes striking after it — every combination correct, plain text unchanged. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1672.4 -> 1672.7 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. **The text-flags byte now uses 3 of 8 bits** (bold/underline/strike). Next feature ideas: `text-align:center`/`<center>` (a per-line shift, a new mechanism); real `<h1>..<h6>` size steps via `font_draw_string_scaled`; `<mark>` highlight (a yellow background default). |
| v5.9.132 | **Feature: Selene renders UNDERLINE — `<u>` and `style="text-decoration:underline"`, with nesting and a `text-decoration:none` override.** Rather than add a 4th per-char grid (memory matters — Selene keeps ~8 grids per tab across up to 6 tabs), the v5.9.131 `bold_of` grid was **generalised into a per-char text-flags byte**: bit 0 = bold, bit 1 = underline. The style-stack frame gained a `ul` field (inherited like the fg/bg/bold ones), so an open `<u>` — or any tag whose inline `style` sets `text-decoration:underline` — turns underline on, a nested `text-decoration:none` span turns it off, and the parent's state is restored when that span closes. Each content character now records `bold | (underline<<1)` into the `tbold[]` lane threaded into the flags grid; the draw pass already breaks the base text into runs of constant *(foreground, background, flags)*, and now, per run, decodes the flags: bit 0 → the v5.9.131 synthetic-bold second glyph pass, bit 1 → a 1px underline drawn under the run with `fb_fill_rect` (the same technique the link overlay uses). **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a test page served over http, screenshotted): `<u>` and `text-decoration:underline` both underline their text; **`<b><u>` is bold AND underlined**; a **blue `<u>`** is underline + colour; a **`<u>` wrapping a `text-decoration:none` span** shows that span without an underline and resumes underlining after it (nesting restore); and an underlined white run on a `background:navy` `<p>` is underline + colour + background — every combination correct, plain text unchanged. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1672.0 -> 1672.4 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. **The flags byte now has 6 free bits for future decorations** (strikethrough, overline, italic-slant). Next feature ideas: `<s>`/`<del>`/`text-decoration:line-through` (bit 2, a mid-height rule); `text-align:center`/`<center>`; real `<h1>..<h6>` size steps. |
| v5.9.131 | **Feature: Selene renders BOLD text — `<b>`, `<strong>`, and `style="font-weight:bold"` (700/800/900/bolder) now embolden their text, with nesting and a `font-weight:normal` override.** Building on the v5.9.129-130 inline-CSS machinery, a third per-char grid **`bold_of`** joins `color_of`/`bgcolor_of`, and the render-time **style stack frame now also carries a bold flag** (inherited like fg/bg): an open `<b>`/`<strong>` — or any tag whose inline `style` sets a bold-ish `font-weight` — pushes bold on, and the matching close pops it, so a `<span style="font-weight:normal">` inside a `<b>` un-bolds just that span and the parent's bold is restored when it closes. Bold is recorded per character into a new `tbold[]` lane threaded through `wrap_text` into `bold_of`; the draw pass now breaks the base text into runs of constant *(foreground, background, bold)* and, for a bold run, draws the glyphs a second time shifted +1px — **synthetic bold** via the new `font_draw_string_trans` (a transparent-background glyph pass added to `font.h`, already implemented in `font.c` and used elsewhere) so the second pass thickens the strokes without disturbing the background. A page with no bold is unchanged (one pass). **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a test page served over http, screenshotted): `<b>`, `<strong>`, and `font-weight:bold`/`font-weight:700` all render visibly heavier than the surrounding text; **`<b style="color:red">` is bold AND red**; a **`<b>` wrapping a `font-weight:normal` span shows that span at normal weight and returns to bold after it**; and `<b>` white text on a `background:navy` `<p>` is bold + coloured + on its background — every combination correct, non-bold text unchanged. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files** (font.h gained one prototype); `nyx-kernel.bin` 1670.9 -> 1672.0 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`, `drivers/video/font.h`. **Next feature ideas:** `text-align:center`/`<center>`; `<u>` / `text-decoration:underline` (reusing the link-underline draw); or `<h1>..<h6>` real size steps. |
| v5.9.130 | **Feature: Selene now also honours inline CSS `background` / `background-color` (and the legacy `<font bgcolor=..>`) — the symmetric other half of v5.9.129's foreground `color`.** A second per-char grid **`bgcolor_of`** joins `color_of` (both indexing the same per-page deduped palette), and the render-time **colour stack frame now carries both a foreground and a background index**: an open tag whose inline `style` sets `color` and/or `background`/`background-color` (or `<font color=/bgcolor=>`) pushes a frame that *inherits the property it does not set* from the enclosing frame, so a `<span style="background:orange">` inside a `<p style="background:teal">` keeps the parent's default text colour while overriding the background, and the parent's teal background is restored when the span closes. `sel_css_get` looks up `background-color` first, then falls back to the `background` shorthand and lets `sel_parse_css_color` read the colour token out of it (a `url(...)`/gradient value simply fails to parse and is ignored — fail-closed). The in-effect background is recorded per character into a new `tbgcol[]` lane threaded through `wrap_text` into `bgcolor_of`; the draw pass now breaks the base-text into runs of constant *(foreground, background)* and paints each with `font_draw_string(sub, fg, bg)`, so `bg` fills the character cells behind the text (a page with no inline colour is still one run in the original ink on the page background — behaviour-preserving). **SCOPE:** the background paints the text's own cells (a highlight), not the element's full block box; only colour values are taken from the `background` shorthand; `<style>` rules remain dropped. **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a test page served over http, screenshotted): a `<p>` with **`background-color:yellow`** shows black-on-yellow, **`background:navy` + `color:white`** shows white-on-navy, an inline **`background:#ffd700`** span is a gold highlight amid default text, **`color:yellow; background-color:#800000`** is yellow-on-maroon, and a **teal-background `<p>` wrapping an `background:orange` span returns to teal** after the span — every combination correct, and the v5.9.129 foreground `color` (red heading) still works. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1669.6 -> 1670.9 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. **`style=` colour is now complete (fg + bg).** Next feature ideas: `font-weight:bold` / `<b>` / `<strong>` (a bold glyph pass or the h1/h2 upper-case trick); `text-align:center`; or a `.wav` player on SB16 (still deferred — audio is not headless-verifiable in this QEMU). |
| v5.9.129 | **Feature: Selene now interprets inline CSS text colour — `style="color:.."` on any element (and the legacy `<font color=..>`) colours that element's text, honouring nesting.** Until now Selene dropped `<style>` blocks and ignored `style=` attributes entirely, so every page rendered in one dark ink. The renderer already carried parallel per-char attribute grids (`link_of`/`field_of`/`img_of`); this adds a **`color_of`** grid (a per-char palette index, 0 = the default text colour) plus a small **per-page, deduped colour palette** (`palette[255]`/`npalette`, stored as framebuffer pixels). New parsing: `sel_css_get` pulls a named declaration out of a `;`-separated inline style string (matching the property exactly, so `color` does not also catch `background-color`), and `sel_parse_css_color` resolves a value to `0xRRGGBB` — `#rgb`, `#rrggbb`, or one of ~28 common named colours (red/green/blue/purple/teal/orange/gold/…). In `render_html` a **colour stack** now runs for every tag before the specific handlers: a non-void open tag carrying a `color:` (or `<font color=>`) interns the colour and pushes it, the matching close pops it, so a `<span>` inside a coloured `<p>` restores the parent colour when it ends. The in-effect colour is recorded per character into a new `tcolor[]` lane (alongside `tlink`/`tfield`/`tindent`) at every content-append site and threaded through `wrap_text` into `color_of`; the draw pass then paints the base text of each line in per-`color_of`-run spans (a page with no inline colour is one run in the original ink — behaviour-preserving). Link/form/find overlays still draw on top, so links keep their blue. **SCOPE: foreground `color` only** — `background`/`background-color` is deferred (it needs a second per-char grid + a fill pass) as are non-inline `<style>` rules; malformed/oversized styles fail closed to the default colour. **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a test page served over http, screenshotted): a **named-colour `<h2>` renders red**, a whole `<p>` in **hex `#0080ff` blue**, a line reading *default → `green span` → default → `orange hex span` (#ff8800) → default* shows each run in its own colour, a **purple `<p>` wrapping a short-hex `#e00` red `<span>` returns to purple** after the span closes (nesting pop-restore), **`<font color=teal>`** colours its text, and a **gold `<p>`** finishes — every colour correct, default text unchanged. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1667.0 -> 1669.6 KB. Files: `gui/apps/selene_win.c`. **Next feature ideas:** inline `background`/`background-color` (the symmetric second half, using `font_draw_string`'s bg parameter); `font-weight:bold` via the h1/h2 upper-case trick or a bold glyph pass; or a `.wav` player on SB16 (deferred — audio can't be verified headless in this QEMU). |
| v5.9.128 | **Feature: the File Manager now opens image files in the Image Viewer (double-click or Enter), the Image Viewer auto-fits a picture to its window, and the File Manager gained a `files [path]` shell launcher — wiring together the two apps from v5.9.127.** Until now `gui/apps/fileman_win.c` sent *every* activated file to the Text Editor, so double-clicking a PNG dumped binary bytes into an editor. A new `fileman_is_image(name)` (case-insensitive `.png`/`.gif`/`.jpg`/`.jpeg`/`.bmp` suffix test) + `fileman_activate_file(fm, path, name)` now dispatch on type — image -> `launch_imageview(path)`, everything else -> `compositor_open_editor(path)` — and both the double-click handler and the Enter-key handler call it, so the two activation paths stay identical. The single-click preview was also made type-aware: an image shows a `"(image) - double-click to view"` hint instead of splattering its binary header into the status bar. In the Image Viewer (`gui/apps/imageview_win.c`), a new **fit-to-window** pass runs in `imageview_win_draw` (the only place the live window size is known): on load, and on the new **`f`** key, it picks a zoom so the whole image is visible — `min(areaW/imgW, areaH/imgH)`, never upscaling a small image past 1:1 — while `r` (reset to 1:1) cancels a pending fit. A one-field struct addition (`fit_pending`) drives it. To make the File Manager reachable from the shell for scripting/testing (every other GUI app already has a launcher), a new **`files [path]`** command + `launch_fileman(path)` (mirrors `launch_selene`/`launch_imageview`) open it, optionally at a starting directory. **Verified live in QEMU by keyboard, 0 kernel faults** (a `/mnt` ext2 image built with `mke2fs -d` holding `gui.png`): `files /mnt` opens the File Manager there; arrowing to `gui.png` and pressing Enter opens it in the **Image Viewer** (the File Manager's status bar reads **"Opened in Image Viewer: gui.png"**, confirming the dispatch picked the viewer, not the editor), and the 1024x768 image renders **fully visible at `zoom: 47%`** — the fit-to-window scale — instead of the v5.9.127 behaviour of clipping it at 100%; no `[fault]`/`[PANIC]`/`SLABCORRUPT`/`MAPCORRUPT`. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1665.4 -> 1667.0 KB. Files: `gui/apps/fileman_win.c`, `gui/apps/imageview_win.c`, `gui/apps/imageview_win.h`, `gui/core/compositor.c`, `core/kernel.c`, `core/kernel.h`. **Next feature ideas:** a `.wav` player on the SB16 driver; Selene inline-CSS (`style="color:..."`, currently `<style>` is just dropped); or a File-Manager thumbnail/preview pane for images. |
| v5.9.127 | **Feature (first increment after the v5.9.100-126 maintenance pass): the Image Viewer app now opens REAL image files (PNG / BMP / GIF / JPEG) from the filesystem and plays ANIMATED GIFs — previously it could only show a hardcoded test pattern.** `gui/apps/imageview_win.c` was a 153-line stub that drew a generated colour-bar/gradient pattern with zoom/pan and never touched a decoder; NyxOS already had four image decoders (`png.c`/`bmp.c`/`gif.c`/`jpeg.c`) and the animated-GIF machinery (`gif_decode_anim`, v5.9.88/93) but nothing outside Selene used them. New **`imageview_open_file(iv, path)`** reads the file the way the Text Editor does (`vfs_open` -> `vfs_fdata`/`vfs_fsize`, valid for both the ramdisk root and a mounted `/mnt`), dispatches by magic bytes (`\x89PNG`/`BM`/`\xFF\xD8`/`GIF`) to `png_decode`/`bmp_decode`/`jpeg_decode` for a static image or `gif_decode_anim` for a GIF (static or animated), and installs the result — freeing any previous image first, so a failed decode leaves the old one intact. New **`imageview_win_tick`** drives animated-GIF playback on the compositor's ~30fps tick, mirroring Selene's proven `sel_img_t` timing: accumulate tick time on the current frame, step when its `delay_cs` elapses, repoint `pixels` at the new frame, and honour the NETSCAPE loop count (a finite loop freezes on the last frame); it returns 1 only when the frame actually flips, so a static image never forces a repaint. Ownership is explicit: for an animated GIF the ctx owns the frame array (`gif_anim_free`) and `pixels` merely aliases the current frame; for a static image `pixels` is owned directly. To free the decoded pixels/frames on window close, a small **`on_close` hook was added to the compositor's `window` struct** — `window_destroy` now calls it (if set) before `kfree`-ing `reserved`; it is NULL for every existing app (no behaviour change) and, wired here as `imageview_win_close`, also fixes the pre-existing leak of the viewer's own pixel buffer. Exposed via a new **`imageview <path>`** shell command + `launch_imageview(path)` (mirrors `launch_selene`), and the desktop "Image Viewer" icon is now wired for the tick + close cleanup too. **Verified live in QEMU, 0 kernel faults** (a `/mnt` ext2 image built with `mke2fs -d` holding real files): `imageview /mnt/anim.gif` (a 6-frame ffmpeg GIF) renders and its status bar reads **`120x120 GIF, 6 frames`**, and two screendumps 1.4 s apart differ by ~940 pixels in the image region — **the frames advance**; `imageview /mnt/gui.png` (a real 21 KB PNG) renders the desktop screenshot at **`1024x768`**; the `giftest` **7/7** and `pngtest` **5/5** decoder KATs still pass (regression); no `[fault]`/`[PANIC]`/`SLABCORRUPT`/`MAPCORRUPT`. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files** (imageview_win.c/.h already existed); `nyx-kernel.bin` 1662.6 -> 1665.4 KB. Files: `gui/apps/imageview_win.c`, `gui/apps/imageview_win.h`, `gui/core/compositor.c`, `gui/core/compositor.h`, `core/kernel.c`, `core/kernel.h`. **Next feature ideas:** open images from the File Manager (double-click -> Image Viewer) and auto-fit / fit-to-window zoom; a `.wav` player on the SB16 driver; or Selene inline-CSS (`style="color:..."`) since Selene currently only drops `<style>`. |
| v5.9.126 | **Maintenance: dropped the NIC's promiscuous mode (RCR `AAP`) in `drivers/net/rtl8139.c`, so the RTL8139 now filters received frames in hardware — accepting only broadcast, multicast, and unicast addressed to our own MAC — instead of DMA'ing every frame on the wire into the RX ring.** This closes the promiscuous-mode story opened in v5.9.121: there the `eth_poll` software `dst_mac` filter was added *because* the driver ran the card in Accept-All-Packets mode; now the card never delivers a foreign unicast in the first place, and that software check becomes a defence-in-depth backstop rather than the primary filter. The RX config was `RTL_RCR_AB | RTL_RCR_AM | RTL_RCR_APM | RTL_RCR_AAP | (MXDMA) | (RXFTH)`; the fix removes **only** the `AAP` bit, keeping `APM` (Accept Physical Match — frames whose destination MAC equals the card's), `AB` (Accept Broadcast), and `AM` (Accept Multicast), plus the unchanged MXDMA=unlimited / RXFTH=no-threshold DMA settings the ring needs to deliver at all. **Why this keeps every protocol working:** a card-filtered NIC still receives (a) broadcasts — ARP "who-has" queries and, crucially, the DHCP `OFFER`/`ACK`, which arrive as Ethernet broadcasts because the DHCP client sets the BOOTP broadcast flag (`pkt[10] = 0x80` in `net/dhcp.c`, bit 15 of the flags field) precisely so the server broadcasts the reply to a client that still has no IP — caught by `AB`; and (b) every unicast reply addressed to our own MAC — ARP replies, ICMP echo replies, and all TCP/HTTP traffic — caught by `APM`. So dropping promiscuous mode removes no legitimate traffic; only foreign unicast (nonexistent on the slirp point-to-point wire) is now filtered by the card instead of by software. **Behaviour-preserving** — every frame the stack legitimately consumes still passes `APM`/`AB`/`AM`. **Verified live, 0 kernel faults**: boot **auto-DHCP leases `10.0.2.15`** end to end (`DISCOVER -> OFFER -> REQUEST -> ACK`, proving `AB` still catches the broadcast reply with `AAP` off); **`ping 10.0.2.2` 4/4, 0% packet loss** (proving `APM` catches unicast ICMP echo replies); **`httpget http://10.0.2.2:8000/` -> `Status: 200 OK`** with the body recovered (proving the full TCP handshake + GET works via `APM` for unicast TCP); no `[PANIC]`/`triple fault`/`SLABCORRUPT`/`MAPCORRUPT`. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1662.6 KB. Files: `drivers/net/rtl8139.c`. **Next maintenance targets:** the network stack's untrusted-input RX audit is now complete end to end AND the NIC filters at the hardware source, so pivot to a fresh subsystem — `fs/initramfs.c` (boot-ramdisk parser), the GUI/compositor input paths, or the audio path; the ext2 writer's missing double-indirect support remains a deferred *feature* rather than maintenance. After ~27 maintenance increments (v5.9.100-126) the high-value untrusted-input surfaces are thoroughly covered, so pausing maintenance to return to features is also a reasonable call. |
| v5.9.125 | **Maintenance: fixed the ext2 writer's disk-full-mid-write block leak (the real bug deferred in v5.9.120) — a write that runs out of space partway no longer orphans the blocks it already allocated.** (This turn also did a second read of the syscall dispatch's user-controlled size/offset math and found it SOUND: read/write/sendto clamp to 4096, poll caps nfds at 64, getdents/getprocs at 256, execve bounds argc/envc, lseek floors negatives — v5.9.124's `fbpresent` was the one real bug there — so the fix landed on the known ext2 leak instead.) In `ext2_write_file`, when `ext2_alloc_block()` fails mid-loop (disk full), the loop `return -1`s **without writing the inode back**, so every block it had already grabbed stays marked used in the bitmap while owned by no inode — an `e2fsck` "Block bitmap differences" that survives a reboot (the same leak class as the >single-indirect case fixed up front in v5.9.120, but here triggered by a genuinely full disk on a within-capacity write). **Fixed** with an up-front free-space check: before allocating anything, count the blocks this write must **newly** allocate — walking the existing block map so an in-place overwrite that reuses its blocks is NOT rejected (only the holes plus a fresh indirect block are counted, reading the existing indirect block once if it is already present) — and return -1 if that exceeds `sb.free_blocks`. Under the FS lock the superblock's free count is stable and consistent with the bitmap, so the check is exact; a write that passes it cannot then fail an allocation. **Behaviour-preserving** — a write that fits allocates and succeeds exactly as before; only a write that genuinely will not fit is now rejected *before* touching the bitmap instead of failing partway and leaking. **Verified live on a deliberately-tiny image (512 blocks, ~476 free), `e2fsck` CLEAN**: 19 `cp /sh.elf` (24 blocks each) succeed and fill the disk, then the next 3 are **rejected up front** (`cp: failed`), and `e2fsck -fn` afterwards reports `494/512 blocks` with **no bitmap differences** — the rejected writes allocated nothing; 0 kernel faults across the 22-write run. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1662.6 KB. Files: `fs/ext2.c`. **Next maintenance targets:** the ext2 writer's missing double-indirect support (a feature, deferred while in the maintenance pass); or drop the NIC's promiscuous `AAP` in `rtl8139.c`; or a fresh subsystem (`fs/initramfs.c`, GUI compositor input, audio). |
| v5.9.124 | **Maintenance: correctness read of the userspace->kernel syscall trust boundary (`core/syscall.c` — user-pointer validation and every `copy_from_user` site) — the validation machinery is SOUND, but `SYS_FBPRESENT` had a ring-3-triggerable integer overflow that became a kernel out-of-bounds read.** The boundary primitives are careful: `user_v2p` walks the user page tables and returns 0 unless the address is in the user half AND every level is `PRESENT|USER` (so a kernel-only mapping is refused); `copy_from_user`/`copy_to_user`/`copy_str_from_user` validate **every byte** through it (a bad user pointer can't reach kernel memory); `user_ptr_ok` rejects wrap-around + out-of-range; and every `copy_from_user` call clamps the user-controlled length to its kernel buffer — `read`/`sendto` cap at 4096 then `kmalloc(len)`, the console path clamps each chunk to `sizeof(kbuf)`, `poll` rejects `nfds > 64` into `kfds[64]`, and `execve` bounds argc/envc to 32/16. **The one real bug was in `SYS_FBPRESENT`:** `uint64_t bytes = (uint64_t)w * h * 4` with `w`,`h` raw user args — for large values (e.g. `w = h = 0x80000000`) the product overflows 64 bits and **wraps to a tiny value (0)**, slipping past the `bytes > 8 MB` cap; the scratch buffer is then sized to that wrapped value while `fb_present_kbuf(fbp_scratch, w, h)` reads the true `w*h*4` bytes from it — an unprivileged **out-of-bounds kernel read blitted to the screen** (or a fault). **Fixed** by bounding each dimension before the multiply (`w == 0 || h == 0 || w > 16384 || h > 16384 -> -1`), so the product stays far inside 64 bits and the 8 MB cap then does its job; 16384 covers any real screen. **Behaviour-preserving** — legitimate dimensions (DOOM / fbtest render sizes) are far below the bound. **Verified live, 0 real faults**: `exec /fbtest.elf` -> `screen 1024x768, 32 bpp` and **`100 frames presented OK`** (SYS_FBPRESENT still works), `jpegtest` 3/3, `formtest` 14/14, `tcploop` PASS, clean boot, no `[fault]`/`[PANIC]`/`SLABCORRUPT`/`MAPCORRUPT`. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1662.4 KB. Files: `core/syscall.c`. **Next maintenance targets:** other size-from-user computations in the syscall dispatch; or `fs/initramfs.c`; or the GUI compositor input paths; or the deferred `rtl8139` `AAP` removal / ext2 disk-full rollback. |
| v5.9.123 | **Maintenance: correctness read of the X.509 certificate-verification path (`crypto/x509.c` + its `crypto/der.c` DER parser + the `crypto/aes_gcm.c` AEAD underneath) — the parse path is SOUND against hostile certificates; the only real defect was a latent static-buffer overflow in the x509 self-test, now bounded.** Read all three against a malicious server's certificate bytes: **der.c** — `der_read` is a correct bounds-checked TLV cursor (checks the tag and every length byte against `end`, rejects indefinite and `>4`-byte lengths, and `(uint32_t)(end - p) < len → reject` before advancing), and every extractor (`der_x509_tbs`/`sig_alg`/`signature`/`pubkey`/`extension`/`validity`) builds on it with strict tag checks; `parse_time` (hardened in v5.9.106) validates each date digit inside the length-checked value. **x509.c** — `int_to_fixed` right-justifies within the fixed field, `host_match` uses an explicit pattern length (no NUL assumption) with a correct single-label wildcard, the EC point-length checks gate the `ec_point+33`/`+49` reads, and `x509_verify_chain` verifies each link's signature under the next key and requires a *pinned* root. **aes_gcm.c** — `aes128_gcm_decrypt` verifies the tag BEFORE decrypting (constant-time compare; plaintext is never released on a forged record — the ideal order), and its one untrusted caller `tls_open_record` already bounds `ct_len` (v5.9.122). So the whole path resists malformed input. **The one real bug** was in `x509_selftest`: `static uint8_t bad_leaf[1003]` was sized to the *current* pinned example.com leaf, with an **unbounded** copy of `TESTCHAIN_LEN[0]` bytes into it — and the pinned chain is re-captured periodically (certs rotate ~quarterly), so the next capture with a larger leaf would silently overflow that static buffer. **Fixed** by enlarging it to 2048 and bounding the copy (reject `l0 > sizeof(bad_leaf)`, and guard the flip index), so no future test certificate can overrun it. **Behaviour-preserving** — the current leaf fits, so the tamper test runs exactly as before. **Verified live, 0 real faults**: `chaintest` 5/5 — real 4-cert chain TRUSTED (anchored to the pinned SSL.com ECC root), **`tampered leaf -> REJECTED` PASS** (the guarded test still catches the forgery), root-less chain NOT anchored, SAN hostname match, validity dates parsed; `dertest` 3/3, `tlsrectest` 5/5, `rsatest` 7/7, `formtest` 14/14, clean boot, no `[PANIC]`/`SLABCORRUPT`/`MAPCORRUPT`. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1662.4 KB. Files: `crypto/x509.c`. **Next maintenance targets:** the TLS/crypto trust path is now audited end to end (der + x509 + aes_gcm + tls record layer), so pivot to a fresh subsystem — `fs/initramfs.c` (boot-ramdisk parser), the `sha256.c`/`sha512.c`/`p256.c` primitives' length handling, or the GUI/compositor input paths; or the deferred `rtl8139` `AAP` removal / ext2 disk-full rollback. |
| v5.9.122 | **Maintenance: correctness read of the TLS record/handshake parser (`crypto/tls/tls.c` — attacker-controlled server bytes over TCP:443) — fixed TWO remote memory-safety bugs: a Certificate-parse out-of-bounds READ and a Finished-decrypt stack-buffer WRITE overflow.** The record walk and the ServerHello/Certificate/SKE parse are otherwise carefully bounded (every wire length is checked against its container before use). **(1) Leaf-cert OOB read:** in the Certificate message the per-cert length `one` is a server-controlled 24-bit field; the *chain* entry was recorded only under `cq + 3 + one <= mlen`, but the **leaf** (`leaf_ptr`/`cert0`) was recorded with no such bound — a malformed Certificate with a huge leaf length left `cert0` huge and `leaf_ptr` pointing into the 16 KB `tls_hd`, and the ServerKeyExchange-signature path then passed `(leaf_ptr, cert0)` to `der_x509_pubkey`/`tls_ecdsa_leaf_verify` as the buffer bound, reading far past `tls_hd`. Fixed by applying the same `cq + 3 + one <= mlen` bound to the leaf assignment. **(2) Finished-decrypt stack overflow:** `tls_open_record()` decrypts `ct_len = record_len - 24` bytes into the caller's `pt` with **no output-size limit**; the encrypted-Finished path handed it a 64-byte stack buffer `fin[64]` while the record length `rl` was bounded only by the received data (~16 KB), so a malicious post-ChangeCipherSpec type-22 record with `rl > 88` overflowed the kernel stack — a remote write primitive (AES-GCM writes the plaintext into `pt` before the tag check). Fixed by adding a `pt_max` parameter to `tls_open_record` and rejecting `ct_len > pt_max`, then passing every caller's real buffer size (`sizeof(fin)`, `cap - pn`, `sizeof(out)`) so no record can write past its output buffer. The application-data path was already guarded (`pn + rl2 > cap`); this makes the function self-safe rather than relying on each caller. **Behaviour-preserving** — a well-formed leaf always satisfies the bound and a legitimate record's plaintext always fits its buffer, so valid handshakes are unchanged. **Verified live, 0 real faults**: `tlsrectest` 5/5 — **`GCM record open PASS (plaintext recovered)`** and `record tamper rejected PASS` (the exact `tls_open_record` path, now with `pt_max`), plus record seal and client/server Finished verify_data; `rsatest` 7/7, `formtest` 14/14, `tcploop` PASS, clean boot, no `[PANIC]`/`SLABCORRUPT`/`MAPCORRUPT`. (A live `posttest` to httpbin.org was DNS-inconclusive — the known first-lookup-after-boot miss, not a TLS issue — so the deterministic record KAT is the proof.) **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1662.4 KB. Files: `crypto/tls/tls.c`. **Next maintenance targets:** the TLS crypto primitives (`aes_gcm.c` / `sha512.c` length handling) or `x509.c` / `der.c` certificate-field walking under a malformed leaf; or drop the NIC's promiscuous `AAP` in `rtl8139.c`; or `fs/initramfs.c`. |
| v5.9.121 | **Maintenance: correctness read of `net/ethernet.c` (the Ethernet frame demux — the last untrusted RX parser in the net stack); memory-safe, and hardened `eth_poll` to drop frames not addressed to us and to never trust the driver's return length past its own buffer.** Read the RX path end to end and it is sound: `eth_poll` rejects `len <= 0` and `len < 14`, and `rtl8139_receive_packet` clamps its copy to the `max_len` it is handed (`data_len = min(length-4, max_len)`) with in-bounds ring-wrap handling over a 256-byte-padded RX buffer, so `payload_len = len - 14` stays inside the 2 KB stack buffer. **Two hardenings, both in `eth_poll`:** (1) the driver leaves the NIC in **promiscuous mode** (RCR `AAP`), so it delivers frames addressed to *other* hosts too, and `eth_poll` processed every one — feeding foreign senders into `arp_handle_packet` (which caches them) and foreign IP into `ip_handle_packet`. It now checks the destination MAC and acts only on our own unicast address or a group address (broadcast/multicast = low bit of the first octet set), using the interface MAC it already receives via `iface_idx` (previously ignored with `(void)iface_idx`). (2) A defensive `if (len > (int)sizeof(buffer)) len = sizeof(buffer);` so a future or buggy driver returning more than the buffer can't run the handlers off the stack — the same "check your own inputs, don't rely on what the caller happens to do" rule the ext2 and mmap paths already follow. **Behaviour-preserving under slirp** — the only traffic on the virtual wire is our own unicast plus broadcast, all of which pass the MAC check; only (nonexistent-under-slirp) foreign unicast is now dropped. **Verified live, 0 real faults**: DHCP still receives `OFFER`+`ACK` (broadcast + unicast RX), `httpget http://10.0.2.2:8000/` -> `200 OK` (TCP unicast RX to our MAC), `ping 10.0.2.2` 4/4 0% loss (ICMP unicast RX), `ping 127.0.0.1` 4/4, `tcploop` PASS, `jpegtest` 3/3, `formtest` 14/14, no `[PANIC]`/`SLABCORRUPT`/`MAPCORRUPT`. **This completes the network stack's untrusted-input RX audit end to end** (Ethernet -> ARP/IP -> UDP/ICMP/TCP -> DNS/DHCP, v5.9.104-121). **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1662.4 KB. Files: `net/ethernet.c`. **Next maintenance targets:** disable the NIC's promiscuous mode at the source (drop RCR `AAP` in `rtl8139.c`, relying on `APM`+`AB`+`AM`) so foreign frames are never DMA'd into the ring at all; or the ext2 disk-full-mid-write rollback; or a correctness read of `fs/initramfs.c`. |
| v5.9.120 | **Maintenance: fixed the ext2 writer's block leak on an over-capacity write — a `cp` of a file larger than the writer supports now fails cleanly (nothing allocated) instead of allocating ~268 blocks, hitting the unsupported double-indirect path mid-loop, and orphaning them (a reboot-surviving `e2fsck` "Block bitmap differences").** Found during the v5.9.119 read: `ext2_write_file` maps only up to single-indirect (12 direct + one indirect block of pointers) — the loop's double-indirect branch just `return -1`s — and on that failure (or any mid-loop `ext2_alloc_block` failure) the blocks already grabbed stay marked used in the bitmap while owned by no inode, because the loop returns before the inode is ever written back. A `cp /doom.elf` (449 KB) reproduced it: `e2fsck` reported `Block bitmap differences: -(4391--4659)` (269 leaked blocks) and "Filesystem still has errors". **Fixed** with an up-front capacity check — `if (blocks_needed > 12 + ext2_fs.block_size/4) return -1;` — placed before the allocation loop, so an oversized write is rejected with the filesystem untouched (`block_size/4` is the pointers-per-block, written inline so it does not shadow the loop's own `ptrs_per_block` under `-Wshadow`). **Behaviour-preserving** — any file within single-indirect capacity (<= 268 blocks on the 1024-byte-block image, ~1036 at 4 KB) writes exactly as before; only an over-capacity write changes, from "partial-alloc + leak + -1" to "clean -1, no allocation". **Verified live with the ext2 disk, e2fsck now CLEAN**: `cp /doom.elf /mnt/toobig.bin` fails (`cp: failed to copy`) and **`e2fsck -fn` shows NO block-bitmap differences** (Pass 1-5, `4676/16384 blocks` = pristine 4651 + 24 for the successful `cp /sh.elf /mnt/ok.bin` + 1 dir — the doom attempt allocated nothing), versus the v5.9.119 run's leaked `-(4391--4659)`; the within-capacity `cp` still works, `mkdir`/`touch`/`ls` fine, `formtest` 14/14, no `[PANIC]`/`SLABCORRUPT`/`MAPCORRUPT`. (A rejected `cp` leaves a harmless 0-byte dest file — cp's create-then-write, no blocks leaked. The disk-full-mid-write leak for a within-capacity file remains a rarer, separate case that needs per-call alloc-tracking to roll back safely.) **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1662.3 KB. Files: `fs/ext2.c`. **Next maintenance targets:** double-indirect WRITE support (a feature — deferred while in the maintenance pass) plus disk-full-mid-write rollback; or `net/eth.c` (frame demux); or a correctness read of `fs/initramfs.c`. |
| v5.9.119 | **Maintenance: hardened `fs/ext2.c`'s block-read path against out-of-range on-disk block pointers (the deeper half of the v5.9.118 hostile-image concern) — a bogus block number no longer becomes an arbitrary/overflowing disk read.** v5.9.118 bounded the inode NUMBER; this bounds the block NUMBERS the inode (and its indirect blocks) point at. `ext2_read_block(block)` computed `block_to_lba(block) = block * sectors_per_block` and read it with no check, so a block pointer read off disk (untrusted on a corrupt/hostile image) that was huge overflowed the uint32 LBA and read an arbitrary wrapped sector. **Fixed** at the single choke point every block read funnels through: `ext2_read_block` now returns -1 for `block >= sb.total_blocks` (a valid FS only references blocks in `[0, total_blocks)`; block 0 is the boot block and callers already check a pointer for 0 first). And `ext2_read_inode_block` now **propagates the two intermediate indirect-block reads** it previously ignored (single- and double-indirect), so a bad indirect *pointer* returns -1 instead of parsing a stale buffer. **Behaviour-preserving** — every valid block is `< total_blocks`, so the bound never fires on a real FS. **Verified live with the ext2 disk attached, 0 real faults, e2fsck CLEAN**: `cp /sh.elf /mnt/big.bin` writes a 23 KB file (direct + single-indirect blocks) and **`cp /mnt/big.bin /mnt/big2.bin` reads it all back through the edited single-indirect path** — the block accounting proves the full read (`e2fsck` reports 4700 blocks vs the pristine image's 4651 = +24 for big.bin + +24 for big2.bin + 1 for the dir, i.e. both are complete 23-block copies), and `e2fsck -fn` passes Pass 1-5 with no bitmap/inode differences; `mkdir`/`touch`/`ls` work, `formtest` 14/14, no `[PANIC]`/`SLABCORRUPT`/`MAPCORRUPT`. (`wc` reports only 2047 bytes for these files — a pre-existing `cmd_wc` limitation, a single 2 KB `vfs_read`, unrelated to ext2.) **Two PRE-EXISTING write-path issues surfaced while testing — NOT introduced here, out of scope for this read-path pass:** (1) `ext2_write_file` never allocates past single-indirect (its own comment: "The writer never goes past singly-indirect"), so writing a file larger than ~268 blocks fails; (2) that failure path leaks the blocks it already allocated (a partial-write `cp /doom.elf` left an `e2fsck` block-bitmap difference on that throwaway image copy). **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1662.3 KB. Files: `fs/ext2.c`. **Next maintenance targets:** the ext2 writer's missing double-indirect support + its block-leak-on-partial-write-failure (a real write-path bug pair, worth its own increment); or `net/eth.c` (frame demux); or `wc`'s single-2KB-read cap. |
| v5.9.118 | **Maintenance: correctness read of `fs/ext2.c` (on-disk structure parsing) — the driver is already impressively hardened, but the inode-number path had one unbounded step: `group = (ino-1)/inodes_per_group` was used to read/write a block-group descriptor without ever checking the group is within the filesystem.** Read the untrusted read path end to end: the superblock validation is thorough (`log_block_size <= 2`, non-zero/bounded `inodes_per_group`/`blocks_per_group`/`inode_size`), the directory-entry walk guards every classic trap (`rec_len < 8` -> break, `off + rec_len > block_size` -> break, `name_len` bounded before the `memcpy`, over-long names skipped), path components are length-capped, and the role-split scratch buffers make nested block reads reentrancy-safe. **The gap:** `resolve_path` takes `cur_ino = de->inode` straight from an on-disk directory entry (untrusted on a corrupt/hostile image), and `ext2_read_inode`/`ext2_write_inode`/`ext2_free_inode` all derive `group` from that inode number and call `read_block_group_bgd(group)` **with no upper bound** — a bogus inode number produced a group far past the descriptor table, so the driver read (and on the write/free paths, *wrote*) a BGD at an arbitrary disk block and then followed its garbage `inode_table`/`inode_bitmap` pointers. **Fixed** at the single choke point every group access funnels through: `read_block_group_bgd` now returns -1 for `group >= block_groups` (the fixed geometry, computed once at mount and now stored in `ext2_fs`), which each caller already treats as "inode unreadable". This matches the file's own stated principle — a corrupt or hostile image is the normal input for a filesystem driver, so validate once and let nothing downstream trust it. **Behaviour-preserving** — a real inode always lands in `[0, block_groups)`, so every valid operation is unchanged; only out-of-range groups from corrupt inode numbers are now rejected. **Verified live with the ext2 disk attached, 0 real faults**: `/mnt` auto-mounts (`[EXT2] Mounted /mnt ... 11733 KB free`), `ls /mnt` lists it, **`mkdir /mnt/v5118d` + `touch /mnt/v5118f.txt` both succeed and appear in a re-`ls`** (the write/alloc path runs through the bounded BGD read), and — decisively — **`e2fsck -fn` on the image afterwards is completely clean** (Pass 1-5, `16/4096 files`, `4652/16384 blocks`, exit 0): the metadata this path touches stays fully consistent. `formtest` 14/14, no `[PANIC]`/`SLABCORRUPT`/`MAPCORRUPT`. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1662.2 KB. Files: `fs/ext2.c`, `fs/ext2.h`. **Next maintenance targets:** validate on-disk block pointers against `total_blocks` in `ext2_read_inode_block` (the deeper half of the same hostile-image concern); or `net/eth.c` (frame demux / length); or a correctness read of `fs/initramfs.c`. |
| v5.9.117 | **Maintenance: correctness read of `net/arp.c` (the last untrusted RX parser in the network stack) — memory-safe, but it acted on every ARP packet without validating the header, so a non-IPv4/non-Ethernet or malformed ARP had its sender fields read from the wrong offsets and cached; added the RFC 826 header check.** `arp_handle_packet` is memory-safe — `len < sizeof(arp_packet_t)` (28 bytes) guards every fixed-offset field read and the reply builder does only fixed-size struct copies — and `arp_cache_add`/`arp_cache_lookup` are bounded. **The gap:** it called `arp_cache_add(sender_ip, sender_mac)` unconditionally and matched `target_ip` **before checking `htype`/`ptype`/`hlen`/`plen`**. The `sender_mac`/`sender_ip`/`target_*` fields sit at fixed struct offsets that assume Ethernet hardware (`hlen=6`) and IPv4 (`plen=4`); a packet with a different hardware/protocol type or address lengths lays those fields out elsewhere on the wire, so caching the "sender" or comparing the "target" acted on **misread bytes** — RFC 826 requires validating the hardware and protocol types first. **Fixed** by rejecting any ARP whose `htype != Ethernet`, `ptype != IPv4`, `hlen != 6`, or `plen != 4` at the top of `arp_handle_packet`, before the cache-add or the request handling. **Behaviour-preserving for real ARP** — every ARP on our links is Ethernet/IPv4 with 6/4 address lengths, so the check passes and resolution/replies work exactly as before; only malformed or non-IPv4 ARP is now dropped. **Verified live, 0 real faults**: a fresh **`ping 10.0.2.2`** (the first unicast to the gateway, so it forces a real ARP resolve) logs `[ARP] Resolving 10.0.2.2... / Resolved` and returns **4/4, 0% loss** — the gateway's real ARP reply passes the new validation and is cached, the direct proof the happy path is intact; `ping 127.0.0.1` 4/4; `tcploop` PASS; and the KAT suite is unaffected — `jpegtest` 3/3, `rsatest` 7/7, `pngtest` 5/5, `bmptest` 4/4, `giftest` 7/7, `formtest` 14/14, clean boot, no `[fault]`/`[PANIC]`. **This completes the network stack's untrusted-input audit** (bar the eth-frame demux): the ARP, IP, UDP, ICMP, TCP, DNS and DHCP RX paths have now all been read and hardened. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1662.2 KB. Files: `net/arp.c`.
| v5.9.116 | **Maintenance: correctness read of `net/icmp.c` — found and fixed a byte-swapped ICMP checksum (missing `htons`) that put an invalid checksum on every outgoing echo request/reply, so `ping` to a real host had never worked; also added the missing RX checksum verification.** `icmp_handle_packet` is memory-safe — it rejects `len < 8` (the header) and the echo-request reply's `kmalloc(len)`+`memcpy(len)` is bounded by the IP-validated `len` (v5.9.114) — but it never verified the incoming ICMP checksum. Adding that check exposed the real bug: `icmp_checksum()` reads the message big-endian and returns the value as a host integer, and both storage sites (`icmp_send_echo` and the echo-reply builder in `icmp_handle_packet`) stored it with a **plain assignment — no `htons`** — so on this little-endian machine the two checksum bytes went out reversed. `ip.c` and `tcp.c` both `htons` their checksums; `icmp.c` was the anomaly.
