Every place that used to special-case the two half-states (the re-flow, the chord handler) now asks `win_is_snapped()` and reads the axes, so adding the corners touched those sites once, not per-state. **`apply_snap_geom` became the single source of geometry for all six** — horizontal picks the left/right column, vertical picks full / top-half / bottom-half, and the far tile on each axis takes the odd pixel (`w = fw - fw/2`, bottom `h = rest`) so the two tiles meet with no gap or overlap. **The chords are a ladder, not a lookup.** When a window is already snapped, Alt+Up climbs `half -> top corner -> maximise` and Alt+Down descends `top -> half -> bottom -> un-snap` (`window_snap_vslide`); Alt+Left/Right either snap a normal window, toggle off if it's already on that side, or **flip sides keeping the vertical zone** so a top-right corner slides straight to top-left. Off the ends the ladder spills cleanly into the neighbouring behaviours: past-top maximises, past-bottom restores, and on an *un-snapped* window Alt+Up/Down keep their v5.9.7 maximise/minimise meaning untouched. **All eight rungs measured, not eyeballed** (title-bar run + accent-frame box, wallpaper-gradient false matches filtered out): left-half x0..511 full-height, Alt+Up -> top-left corner x0..511 top-half, Alt+Right flip -> top-right x512..1023 top-half (vertical zone preserved), Alt+Down -> right-half -> bottom-right x512..1023 y365..731, Alt+Down again un-snaps to the byte-identical x300..941 boot rect. **The corner re-flow is proven, not argued:** a top-left-quarter window driven through `setres 640 480` re-derives to the top-left quarter of 640x480 (x0..319, top half) rather than scaling a seam. The Welcome hint line became `Alt+Arrows tile/snap window` in place (no new line, no window regrow). 0 faults, 0 panics across nine tiling transitions. **No new syscalls** (still 54 — compositor only). |
| v5.9.8 | **Window snapping — Alt+Left/Right half-tiling, built on v5.9.7's chord layer.** Alt+Left tiles the focused window to the left half of the usable area (screen minus taskbar minus title bar), Alt+Right to the right half; snapping to the side it is already on toggles back to the pre-snap geometry, so Alt+Left doubles as a spring-loaded un-snap. Two new window states, `WSTATE_SNAP_LEFT/RIGHT`, tracked exactly like `WSTATE_MAXIMIZED` so the arrangement survives focus changes and mode switches. **The restore-geometry rule is the load-bearing part.** `normal_*` (where un-arranging lands) must be snapshotted only when coming **from** the normal state — a factored `save_normal_geom()` no-ops if the window is already maximised or snapped, so Alt+Left -> Alt+Right -> Alt+Down walks back to the *original* rect, not to a half-screen one. The same guard was retro-fitted onto `window_maximize()` (it used to save unconditionally), which changes nothing today but makes maximise-from-snapped correct for free. **The interaction that actually needed proving is the re-flow.** A snapped window on a resolution change must re-derive to the NEW screen's half, not scale — scaling would round a seam down the middle exactly as it would round one at the edge for a maximised window. `windows_reflow()` now treats both snap states like maximised: re-derive from the framebuffer (via the shared `apply_snap_geom()`, forward-declared since it lives beside `window_snap` further down), after `normal_*` has been scaled. **Alt+Down generalised:** it now restores **any** non-normal arrangement (maximised or snapped) before it will minimise a plain window — a focused window is never minimised, so `state != NORMAL` cleanly means "un-arrange first". **Everything measured, not eyeballed:** snap-left is x 0..511 (exact left half of 1024), snap-right x 512..1023, the toggle-off returns the Terminal to a **byte-identical** x 299..939 boot rect, and after a 1024->640->1024 round trip a snapped-right window re-derives to x 320..639 — the right half of 640, proving the re-flow branch. Alt+Down on a snapped window restores it visibly (not minimised). The Welcome window gained an `Alt+Left/Right snap half` hint and grew 320->360 px so its last line still clears the bottom at 640x480 (checked unobscured — ID/WS both visible). 0 faults, 0 panics. **No new syscalls** (still 54 — compositor + keyboard only). |
| v5.9.7 | **Window-management keyboard shortcuts — and they retire v5.9.6's honest gap.** Four chords, all conventional: **Alt+Tab** cycles focus, **Alt+F4** closes, **Alt+Up** maximises, **Alt+Down** restores (or minimises a normal window). Until now the compositor had exactly one keyboard chord — Ctrl+1..4 for workspaces — and no way to manage a window without the mouse. Three small layers make it work. (1) `keyboard.c` now **tracks left-Alt** (scancode 0x38 was detected and its state *thrown away* — a one-word fix mirroring `ctrl_pressed`), exposed as `is_alt_pressed()`. (2) **Function keys F1-F12 now reach the compositor**: they produce no character, so they were silently dropped, and Alt+F4 needs F4 to arrive — routed as keycodes 0x90-0x9B on the same ring as the arrows, press-edge only, contiguous so `KEY_F1 + n` indexes Fn. (3) The compositor intercepts the chords **before** routing the key to the focused window (each ends in `goto done_click`), so a maximised Terminal never also eats the Tab. **Alt+Tab deliberately does not reuse `focus_next_window()`** — that picks the highest z-order, which is right on destroy but as a cycler would only ever toggle the top two windows, since focusing one bumps it above the other. New `window_cycle_focus()` walks the window array in **slot order** (stable for a window's whole life) so repeated Alt+Tab visits every window in a fixed rotation and wraps — verified Terminal(slot 2) -> Welcome(slot 0). **The payoff beyond the feature: this closes the exact gap v5.9.6 logged as "reasoned, not exercised."** That row admitted the `WSTATE_MAXIMIZED` re-flow branch could not be tested because maximising needed a title-bar click and monitor-only clicking is broken. Alt+Up **is** a headless maximise (the harness sends `sendkey alt-up`), so the test now maximises the Terminal, then `setres 640 480` re-flows a genuinely maximised window, and the screendump confirms it re-derives to fill 640x480 exactly rather than scaling — the branch is now **exercised and correct**, not argued. All four chords proven by screendump: Alt+Up fills the screen, Alt+Down returns to the prior geometry, Alt+Tab moves the accent title-bar + taskbar highlight from Terminal to Welcome, Alt+F4 drops the window count 3->2 and re-focuses by z-order. **Discoverability:** the Welcome window lists the chords (`Alt+Tab switch  Alt+F4 close`, `Alt+Up/Down maximize/min`); it was grown 280->320 px tall so the two added lines still clear the bottom edge at 640x480 — **checked unobscured at 640, all seven lines visible**, not just reasoned from the 0.625 scale. 0 faults, 0 panics across two full sessions (one keyboard, one mode-switch). **No new syscalls** (still 54 — this is all compositor + keyboard driver, no ring-3 surface). |
| v5.9.6 | **Window geometry follows the screen — the follow-up v5.9.5 named as its own honest limitation.** v5.9.5 could only *clamp*, so at 640x480 the Terminal's hardcoded 640x400 was still screen-sized: correct, but "a window" had become "the desktop". The fix names the assumption that was folklore across nineteen `window_create()` call sites — **their literals are authored on a 1024x768 design grid** — and scales that grid onto the live framebuffer, aspect-preserving and **shrink-only**. Terminal now lands at 400x250 and 640x480 gets the same *layout* as 1024x768, just smaller. **Growing is deliberately not done:** on a bigger screen the right answer is more room for windows, not one inflated window with blurrier fixed-pixel content. **The scale runs before the MIN floor and before the clamp**, so the clamp goes back to being the safety net it was meant to be instead of the thing deciding the layout. **The second, more visible half:** both guards so far only ran at *birth*, and nothing had ever revisited an already-open window when the mode changed under it — switching 1024x768 -> 640x480 from the Settings Display tab left **the very window being clicked** 20 px off the right edge and 18 px under the taskbar. New `windows_reflow()` rescales every live window by the OLD->NEW ratio (not from the design grid, so a window the user has since dragged keeps *their* arrangement), carries `normal_*` across so un-maximising cannot strand it, and clamps. **Q16.16, not Q8.8, and that was measured rather than assumed:** at Q8.8 the round trip 1024 -> 640 -> 1024 brought the Terminal back one pixel narrower *every* trip, because 1024/640 = 1.6 is only representable as 409/256 = 1.5977, quietly multiplying by 0.9985 each time. At Q16.16 with round-to-nearest the **size round-trips exactly** (640 -> 400 -> 640). **Position can still shift one pixel, once** — x=300 maps to 187.5 and no precision breaks that tie — but 301 -> 188 -> 301 is a fixed point, **verified identical across three consecutive round trips**, so it is a static ±1 px and not a leak. The mode switch itself is now one function, `display_set_mode()`, shared by the Settings button and a new **`setres` shell command** (whitelisted modes on purpose: `vbe_set_mode()` validates nothing and would happily program a display you can no longer type on). That command exists because the re-flow was otherwise reachable *only* by clicking a button inside the window the re-flow has to rescue — it gives the test a seam through the identical code path, not a parallel copy. **Two traps caught in review, both flagged by comments already in the file:** hoisting the clamp into `window_clamp(win)` broke `normal_x = x` (the locals are the *unclamped* request once the clamp mutates `win->`), and the one `window_create(fb_get_width()/2-150, ...)` call site would have had the scale applied twice and landed left of centre — it is now design-grid `(362, 284)`. **Honest gap:** the `WSTATE_MAXIMIZED` branch of the re-flow is reasoned, not exercised — it is reachable only by a title-bar click, and monitor-only clicking does not work (PS/2, and `mouse_button` emits no packet; `usb-tablet` is not a way out either, since NyxOS has no USB HID driver). What makes that acceptable is that its failure mode is covered: scaling a maximised window instead yields the right width and an over-tall height, and `window_clamp` caps height at exactly `fh - TASKBAR_H - TITLE_H` — the maximised height — and the clamp *is* exercised. **1024x768 is untouched** (scale = 1.0; the default desktop screendumps identically), and 640x480 shows all three boot windows whole, on-screen, clear of the taskbar. 0 faults, 0 panics across six mode switches. **No new syscalls** (still 54 — `setres` is a shell builtin, not a syscall); compositor + one command. |
| v5.9.5 | **No window can be born off-screen.** Same family as v5.9.4's icon fix, one layer up. **Every** `window_create()` call site passes hardcoded geometry — `(100,100,550,380)` File Manager, `(150,120,600,400)` Text Editor, `(80,80,640,400)` Terminal, `(200,120,450,200)` the About demo, ten of them — all sized for 1024x768. `window_create()` clamped only the MINIMUM size (`MIN_WIN_W/H`) and never checked the framebuffer, so at the 640x480 mode Settings offers the About demo spans x=200..650 and hangs off the right edge. That is not merely ugly: **the title bar is the drag handle**, so a window whose right edge (with its close button) is past the screen cannot be closed *or* dragged back — it is simply stuck. Now `window_create()` clamps into the framebuffer: size capped against the usable area (screen minus taskbar minus title bar), then the origin moved back inside, then floored at zero. **Order matters and is commented** — repositioning before capping would just shove an oversized window off the opposite edge. Fixing it at the constructor rather than at ten call sites means no future caller can reintroduce it. **A subtle second bug fixed with it:** `normal_x/y/w/h` — the geometry un-maximising restores to — was copied from the caller's RAW request, so a clamped window would jump straight back off-screen the first time it was maximised and restored; it now records the clamped values. **Verified before and after at 640x480** by temporarily forcing the boot mode: the old build shows the About window's close button sliced by the right edge, the new one shows every window whole and reachable. That temporary `kernel.c` edit was reverted and confirmed byte-identical to HEAD (it showed as modified only through a stale index mtime, and `git diff HEAD` listed no content change). **At 1024x768 nothing clamps** — the largest window is 750x542 inside 1024x710 usable — and a screenshot confirms the default desktop is unchanged. **Honest limitation:** at 640x480 the Terminal (640x400 requested) now legitimately fills almost the whole screen, because its hardcoded size simply *is* screen-sized there. That is correct clamping, not a new defect, but making the app windows' default sizes screen-relative is a real follow-up. **Syscall count is 54** (SYS_GETTIMEOFDAY=52, SYS_NANOSLEEP=53 arrived in `2257403`, between v5.9.1 and v5.9.2). The "still 52" carried in the **v5.9.2–v5.9.4** rows was stale and has been corrected in place; v5.9.1's was accurate for its commit, which is why the count was checked per version rather than swept — and why the 30 older `still 52` claims in the v5.8.x rows, correct when written, were left alone. Compositor-only otherwise; 0 faults, 0 panics. |
| v5.9.4 | **Desktop icons wrap instead of running off the screen.** The icon layout was one fixed row — `desktop_icon_x[i] = 20 + i * 76`, `y = 20`, with no bound and no dependence on the framebuffer size. Nine icons span x=20..692, which is fine at 1024x768 and **broken at the 640x480 mode Settings itself offers**: the ninth icon ("Mines") is drawn from x=628, so only 12 px of it are on screen — and because `desktop_icon_hit()` reads the same coordinates, the part you *can* see is not where the hit test is either. Nothing re-ran on a mode change, so once you shrank the resolution there was no way to get the icon back. Now the layout is a **grid that wraps on the framebuffer width** (`cols = (fw - margin) / ICON_CELL_W`, clamped to at least one column), and the Settings resolution handler calls `init_desktop_icons()` after `fb_init()` so a mode change re-flows it. **A full re-flow, not per-icon clamping:** icons are drag-reorderable, so clamping each stray icon back inside the new bounds would silently stack several on the same spot; re-flowing is both what desktops conventionally do on a mode change and the only option that cannot produce overlaps. **At 1024x768 the computation yields 13 columns, so all nine still sit in one row and the default desktop is pixel-identical** — only the narrow modes change. **Proven before AND after at the failing resolution**, not just reasoned about: the boot mode was temporarily forced to 640x480 and the old layout photographed with the last icon sliced by the right edge, then the same shot with the fix shows it wrapped onto a second row, whole, with its label. That temporary change to `kernel.c` was reverted and verified to leave no diff. 0 faults, 0 panics. **No new syscalls** (still 54); compositor-only. |
| v5.9.3 | **The window frame joins the focus story.** v5.9.2 gave the focused window an accent title bar; its FRAME still did not care. `draw_window_frame()` painted the same fixed 3D bevel (light `180,180,180` top+left, dark `80,80,80` bottom+right) on every window, focused or not, so once a window's 22px title strip was covered by another window there was no focus cue left at all. Now the frame reads focus too: a focused window takes `THEME_ACCENT` on its top+left edge and `THEME_ACCENT_DIM` on bottom+right, so its border and title bar merge into one continuous coloured edge; an unfocused window keeps the neutral bevel, which is what preserves the depth cue between stacked windows. The two bevel greys became `THEME_FRAME_HI` / `THEME_FRAME_LO` rather than staying literals, and the title text plus the three button glyphs now use `THEME_TITLE_TEXT` instead of three separate hardcoded whites. **The traffic-light buttons are deliberately untouched** (close red / minimise grey / maximise green): that is control affordance, not branding, and this is the third increment in which recolouring them was considered and rejected for the same reason. **Both states were verified, not just the good one** — a 2x crop of the focused Terminal shows the purple frame continuous with its title bar, and a 4x crop of the unfocused About window shows the neutral grey bevel intact, which is what proves the conditional actually branches rather than everything simply having turned purple. `scratchpad/shot.sh` + `crop.py`; 0 faults, 0 panics. **No new syscalls** (still 54); compositor-only, so the v5.9.0-LTS kernel guarantees are untouched. |
| v5.9.2 | **The palette actually reaches the apps — and the focused window is finally the brand colour.** v5.9.1 introduced `theme.h` but only the compositor read it: the ten `*_win.c` apps still held **206 `fb_rgb()` literals and zero roles**, so "change the accent in one place and the desktop follows" was true of the chrome and false of everything inside a window. This closes that gap. **Rolled out, deliberately conservative:** 206 → 169 literals, **61 role uses across 8 apps**. Only unambiguously SHARED chrome was converted — window bodies (`45,45,50`), borders (`100,100,100`), row dividers (`55,55,60`), panel header strips (`40,45,55`, 12 copies) and raised button faces (`60,70,80`, 10 copies), the last two newly named `THEME_PANEL_HEADER` / `THEME_BUTTON` because ten apps had each re-invented their own idea of "a button". **App CONTENT colours were left alone on purpose** — a Paint swatch, a Minesweeper cell, the terminal's ANSI palette and the Wallpaper picker's own colour chips are data, not theme; converting them would have inflated the number while making the code wrong. That is why `terminal_win.c` and `wallpaper_win.c` legitimately convert **zero**. **The branding carrier:** the apps' two selection blues (`60,90,130` ×6 and `60,80,120` ×3 — the same concept spelled two ways) both became `THEME_SELECTION`, i.e. the accent, so selecting a file or a row now shows the brand purple. Body text on `220,220,220` was unified onto `THEME_TEXT`, removing a near-duplicate shade. **Window chrome:** `title_active` was an off-brand blue (`60,130,200`); it is now `THEME_TITLE_ACTIVE` = the accent, with `THEME_TITLE_INACTIVE` as its pair. Focus was previously *technically* distinguishable (blue vs grey) but subtle and unbranded — a zoomed screenshot confirms the new pair reads unambiguously: bright purple with white text for the focused window, dark grey for the rest. The accent is safe on a title bar precisely because it is painted on the window frame, a surface the compositor controls — the opposite of the v5.9.1 workspace-marker mistake, and `theme.h` now states that rule next to both roles. **Verified visually** (`scratchpad/shot.sh`, bash + `-monitor pipe`): desktop screenshot plus an 8x crop of the title bars; 0 faults, 0 panics. **No new syscalls** (54 at this point — `gettimeofday`/`nanosleep` landed in `2257403` between v5.9.1 and here); compositor + apps only, so the v5.9.0-LTS kernel guarantees are untouched. |

## Architecture
### Boot flow
1. `boot.asm` (multiboot header, GDT, paging off) → `kernel_main`
2. `init_gdt()` → `init_idt()` → `init_isr()` → `init_irq()`
3. `init_memory()` (bitmap allocator) → `init_paging()` (identity-map 1:1 up to 4MB) → `init_heap()` (16MB heap)
4. `vbe_init()` → `vbe_set_mode(1024, 768, 32)` → `fb_init()` → `init_apic()` → `init_timer(1000)` → `init_keyboard()`
5. `init_process()` → `ensure_idle_process()` → `init_syscalls()` → `init_vfs()` → `init_load_modules()`
6. `init_ext2()` → `init_net()` → `tcp_init()` → `init_background_tasks()`
7. `mouse_init()` → `speaker_init()` → `sb16_init()` → register IRQ handlers → `sti`
8. `initramfs_load()` → `initramfs_boot()` (create initramfs files in VFS)
9. Auto-detect EXT2 on ATA disk → mount at `/mnt`
10. `bootsplash_update(23, …)` → `bootsplash_clear()` → `auth_setup()` → `login_screen()`
11. `compositor_init()` + `compositor_run()` (GUI desktop) OR `launch_shell()` (fallback)

### Critical constraints
- Paging identity-maps 64 MB (16 page tables). Any static data, BSS, or heap beyond 64 MB causes triple-fault.
- Heap is 16 MB (bumped from 1 MB for DOOM + framebuffer). Physical allocator bitmap supports up to 512 MB RAM.
- `_kernel_end` symbol in `linker.ld` marks kernel BSS boundary for memory manager.
- Serial (`init_serial()`) is a stub — only used via direct `outb(0x3F8, ...)`. VGA text mode (0xB8000) is primary console.
- Interrupts are ENABLED (`sti`). Timer (IRQ0), keyboard (IRQ1), mouse (IRQ12), and SB16 (IRQ5) are interrupt-driven with proper PIC unmasking and EOI.
- Cooperative multitasking via `switch_context`/`create_task_stack` (assembly) + background task callbacks + IRQ scheduler tick.
- Per-process paging: `alloc_page_directory` clones kernel identity-mapped page tables with supervisor-only PTEs; user pages mapped via `map_page_dir` with PTE `U/S=1` and PDE `U/S=1` for ring-3 access.
- ELF loader maps code at ELH entry point (e.g. 0x10000), user stack at 0xD0000000 (one page, 4096 bytes).
- Syscalls via `syscall`/`sysret`: rax=number, rdi/rsi/rdx/rcx/r8/r9=args; handlers in `syscall_handler_c` dispatch table (exit=0, write=1, print=2, open=3, read=4, close=5, getpid=6, sbrk=7, fsize=8, exec=9).
- TSS I/O map base set to full TSS size → all I/O ports denied from ring 3.
- build.ps1: WSL cross-compilation; QEMU 11.x with `-audiodev dsound,id=audio0 -device sb16,audiodev=audio0`

## Kernel structure
```
kernel/
  boot.asm        — Multiboot header, GDT, start
  kernel.c        — Main kernel, shell (40+ command handlers), desktop launch
  kernel.h        — All typedefs, extern declarations, constants
  linker.ld       — Linker script with _kernel_end
  memory.c        — Bitmap physical page allocator
  heap.c          — 16 MB kernel heap (kmalloc/kfree)
  paging.c        — Identity-mapped page tables (4 MB)
  process.c       — Process table, background tasks, schedule
  switch.asm      — Context switch (switch_context, create_task_stack)
  vfs.c           — Ramdisk VFS + mount table (EXT2 passthrough)
  ext2.c/h        — EXT2 filesystem driver (read-only, mountable via VFS)
  ata.c/h         — ATA/IDE PIO disk driver
  timer.c         — PIT timer (1000 Hz, interrupt-driven)
  keyboard.c      — PS/2 keyboard (US/ES layouts, AltGr)
  screen.c        — VGA text mode (80x25) + putchar hook for terminal capture
  serial.c        — Serial stub
  syscall.c       — Syscall handler table
  elf.c/h         — ELF64 loader (validate, parse PT_LOAD, map pages, create user process)
  initramfs.c/h   — Initramfs cpio parser (new-style '070701'), creates files in VFS
  initramfs_data.h — Generated C byte array with embedded cpio archive
  net.c           — Network stack init
  ethernet.c      — Ethernet frame handler
  arp.c           — ARP cache + requests
  ip.c            — IPv4 send/receive with checksum
  icmp.c          — ICMP echo (ping)
  udp.c           — UDP sockets
  rtl8139.c       — RTL8139 NIC driver (PCI, I/O, MMIO, TX/RX rings)
  dhcp.c          — DHCP client (DISCOVER → OFFER → REQUEST → ACK)
  tcp.c/tcp.h     — Full TCP connection state machine (8 connections)
  vbe.c           — Bochs VBE framebuffer (up to 1024x768x32)
  fb.c            — Framebuffer abstraction (put_pixel, fill_rect, blit)
  mouse.c         — PS/2 mouse driver (IRQ12, 3-byte packets)
  gui.c           — GUI paint demo (mouse drawing)
  font.c/font.h   — VGA 8x16 bitmap font (256 glyphs)
  compositor.c/h  — Window compositor (32 windows, z-order, drag, resize, close, workspaces)
  terminal_win.c/h — Terminal emulator window (scrollback, Tab completion, command execution)
  fileman_win.c/h — File Manager window (VFS browsing, directory navigation, file preview)
  speaker.c/h     — PC speaker driver (PIT channel 2, beep, melody)
  sb16.c/h        — Sound Blaster 16 DSP driver (DMA, IRQ, mixer, PCM playback)
  editor_win.c/h  — Text Editor window (file open/save, cursor nav, scroll, click)
  imageview_win.c/h — Image Viewer window (test pattern, zoom/pan)
  soundtest_win.c/h — Sound Test window (PC Speaker + SB16 buttons)
  gdt.c/idt.c/isr.c/irq.c — x86 descriptor tables
```

## Shell commands (40+)
| Command       | Description |
|---------------|-------------|
| help          | Show all commands |
| clear         | Clear screen |
| nyxfetch      | System info with ASCII logo |
| echo          | Print text or `echo text > file` |
| ls/cd/pwd     | Filesystem navigation (also on EXT2 via /mnt) |
| cat/touch/mkdir/rm/cp/mv | File operations |
| which/head/tail/grep/sort/wc/find/tree | File utilities |
| write/diff    | Write file / compare two files |
| env/export    | Environment variables |
| history       | Command history |
| ps/kill/mem   | Process management |
| ifconfig/dhcp/ping/setip | Network commands |
| tcptest       | TCP HTTP GET test |
| mount         | Mount EXT2: mount [drive] [part_lba] |
| date/uname/version | System info |
| exec          | Execute ELF binary: exec <file> |
| layout        | Switch keyboard layout (us/es) |
| hexdump       | Dump memory |
| crash         | Trigger kernel panic |
| reboot        | Reboot via 0x64/0xFE |
| doom          | Run DOOM game (requires doom1.wad, legacy) |
| mode          | Set VBE video mode |
| gui           | GUI paint demo with mouse |
| fonttest      | Test bitmap font rendering |
| desktop       | Window compositor desktop |
| beep/play     | PC speaker tones and melodies |
| sb16play      | SB16 DMA audio playback: sb16play [freq] [ms] |

## Shell features
- Tab completion for command names
- Environment variable expansion: `echo $VARNAME`
- Command history (last 10, duplicates filtered)
- `echo > file` redirection support
- Pipe `|` support (`cmd1 | cmd2` with temp file)

## Network stack
- RTL8139: PCI I/O BAR, MMIO, TX/RX rings, link detection, CONFIG1 fix
- ARP: Cache with request/reply, static entries, periodic cleanup
- IP: Send/receive with header checksum, local delivery
- UDP: Raw datagram send, port-based listener registration
- ICMP: Echo request/reply for ping
- **DHCP**: Full client (DISCOVER → OFFER → REQUEST → ACK), auto-config
- **TCP**: Full connection state machine (SYN, ESTABLISHED, FIN, RST handling)
- Single static IP: 10.0.2.15/24 or DHCP-assigned

## GUI subsystem
- Bochs VBE framebuffer (1024x768x32, LFB at 0xE0000000)
- PS/2 mouse (IRQ12, absolute positioning)
- VGA 8x16 bitmap font from Linux kernel (256 glyphs)
- Window compositor: z-ordering, title bars (min/max/close), drag-to-move, resize, 4 workspaces
- Taskbar with running app buttons, Start menu (12 items), clock
- Desktop icons (Files, Terminal, Editor, Viewer, DOOM, Settings, Paint, Sounds)
- Terminal emulator window (scrollback, Tab completion, command execution)
- File Manager window (VFS browsing, directory navigation, file preview)
- Text Editor window (file open/save, cursor navigation, scroll, click-to-position)
- Image Viewer window (color-bar test pattern with zoom/pan, grid overlay)
- Sound Test window (PC Speaker beep/melody/alarm + SB16 sine/square/sweep) 

## v5.1.0 — Stability and bugfix release
- **TSS struct alignment fix**: `kernel/kernel.h` — three reserved `uint32_t` fields (SS0/SS1/SS2 at offsets 28-39) were incorrectly merged into a single `uint64_t`, shifting all IST pointers 4 bytes early. Any exception using an IST (double fault, NMI) read a garbage stack pointer → triple fault.
- **GDT limit correction**: `kernel/gdt.c` — limit was `(6*8+16)-1=63` (too large), corrected to `(5*8+16)-1=55`.
- **APIC/PIC IRQ masking fix**: `kernel/irq.c` — when APIC is active, the legacy PIC stays fully masked. Prevents duplicate interrupt delivery.
- **switch_to_user_process inline asm fix**: `kernel/switch.asm` — AT&T operand order was reversed (source/destination swapped in `mov`), causing exec'd user processes to crash.
- **QEMU display fix**: `run.ps1` — changed from `-display gtk` to `-display sdl` (gtk is broken on Windows).
- **VGA 8x16 font data corruption fix**: `kernel/font.c` — font_data array had marker bytes (the glyph's own index) inserted at position `(index % 16)`, shifting all pixel data. Replaced with correct IBM VGA 8x16 ROM font. GUI text now renders legibly.
- **Boot loop resolved**: kernel boots to desktop in GUI mode and runs stably for extended periods. No double faults, page faults, or crashes observed after running 60+ seconds.
- Build: zero errors, zero warnings. Kernel confirmed stable in both serial and GUI (SDL) mode.

## v5.3.0 — Login system
- **Boot animation**: Full-screen NyxOS-themed splash (~5s) with dark gradient, ASCII bird logo, twinkling stars (60 points), animated spinner (8 rotating dots), progress bar (23 steps with percentage and status text). Fades to black before desktop.
- **Auth backend** (`auth.c/h`): Credential storage in `/etc/passwd` on EXT2 (`username:salt:hex_hash`). First boot creates default user `nyx`/`nyx`. Fallback to hardcoded default when no EXT2 disk. Hash uses djb2 with per-user salt.
- **Login screen** (`login.c/h`): Framebuffer-based login panel (centered dark panel with title, username/password fields). Keyboard input via direct `kbd_buffer` polling with `cli`/`sti`. Password masked with `*`. Enter switches fields or submits. No cursor or mouse to avoid page faults.
- **Integration**: `bootsplash_clear()` → `auth_setup()` → `login_screen()` → compositor in kernel_main. Login failure → reboot via `outb(0x64, 0xFE)`. Success → launch NyxOS Desktop.
- **Keyboard buffer exposed**: `kbd_buffer`, `kbd_head`, `kbd_tail` made non-static in `keyboard.c` for direct polling from login.c.

## Next features to add
- **Userspace polish** (ring-3 works as of v5.5.0; exec reaps on exit + file
  read/write from ring 3, v5.6.0; per-fd offsets / streaming, v5.7.0):
  - Each read/write is still capped at one ≤4 KB `kmalloc` bounce per syscall
    (loop in userspace for more). `vfs_pwrite` reallocs on every grow — fine for
    small files, revisit for large/append-heavy workloads.
  - `copy_from_user`/`copy_to_user` assume user physical pages fall in the 64 MB
    identity map; fine for small programs, revisit if RAM use grows.
  - Jobs: `exec <file>` = foreground (blocks via `kwait`), `spawn <file>` =
    background (v5.7.8); `kill` on a running scheduled proc is safe (v5.7.10); `jobs`
    + `wait [pid]`/wait-all (v5.7.11). The shell job-control set is complete.
- **Preemptive scheduler** — DONE. Kernel threads (v5.7.6, PIT via I/O APIC pin 2
  → vector 32, round-robin behind `sched_enable()`, `mtdemo` self-test). Ring-3
  processes (v5.7.7): the scheduler switches user CR3 + TSS RSP0 for `sched_managed`
  procs and reaps them on exit. `wait()`/job control (v5.7.8): `exec` = foreground
  (`spawn`+`kwait`, `PROC_BLOCKED` + `wake_waiters` on child exit), `spawn` =
  background. Legacy setjmp/longjmp blocking-exec path removed (v5.7.9) — one ring-3
  launch path (scheduler `iretq`) remains. Blocked states + timer wait queue
  (v5.7.10): `sleep()` blocks (`PROC_BLOCKED`+`wake_tick`, scheduler wakes sleepers)
  instead of busy-waiting; `kill` on a running scheduled proc marks it a zombie
  safely. `jobs`/`wait`/wait-all (v5.7.11) round out shell job control. Weighted
  round-robin priority (v5.7.12): `sched_weight`/`sched_quantum`, compositor gets
  weight 4 so the GUI beats background jobs. Heap + VFS re-entrancy guarded
  (`preempt_disable`): kmalloc/kfree (v5.7.6-era) and EXT2 ops + the VFS node pool
  (v5.7.13). Idle desktop yields the CPU (v5.7.15: compositor `sleep(5)`s when nothing
  is happening — the "block instead of poll" win, via a short timeout rather than a
  keyboard wait queue); `nice`/`renice` set job weights from the shell. Remaining
  follow-ups:
  1. **True IO wakeups (optional).** The idle-yield uses a 5 ms timeout; a real
     keyboard/mouse-IRQ wakeup would drop input latency further and let it sleep
     longer, but needs IRQ→scheduler coupling — only worth it if latency matters.
     mtdemo/spin still `hlt`-park after finishing rather than exiting.
  2. **Per-process FS state.** Fds are per-process (v5.7.14: `ufd` table in
     `process_t`, isolated + closed on reap). CWD is now **per-shell** (v5.7.21):
     each Terminal window owns its `current_dir` node and swaps it in around command
     execution, so `cd` in one shell no longer moves another. Still deferred:
     per-*process* CWD for ring-3 code — user procs use absolute paths and there's no
     `chdir` syscall, so it's low value. Also: the FS lock is coarse (whole EXT2 op
     under `preempt_disable`) — fine now, but a buffer-per-op would let FS I/O interleave.
- File Manager: copy/paste + drag-and-drop **done** (v5.7.16 — drop into folders,
  move/Ctrl-copy, paste-`_copy`). Possible follow-ups: multi-select, cut-and-drop
  visual feedback on the target folder, a two-pane view.
- Network: DHCP/DNS/TCP/HTTP all work (v5.7.3–v5.7.4); loopback + ICMP echo replies
  + a real `ping` with RTT/loss stats **done** (v5.7.17); TCP retransmission (RTO +
  exponential backoff, incoming-ACK processing) + robust time-based multi-segment
  receive **done** (v5.7.18); TCP passive open (`listen`/`accept`) + fully in-guest
  loopback TCP with a `tcploop` self-test **done** (v5.7.19); NIC-side `listen` —
  NyxOS serves HTTP to real external clients via `hostfwd`, incl. the server
  half-close path — **done** (v5.7.20). Possible follow-ups: TCP send-side
  segmentation for large bodies, a persistent multi-connection listen loop, and DNS
  reliability under high latency. Note: QEMU slirp's gateway doesn't answer ICMP
  echo and the host ISP may intercept some HTTP — verify against loopback (`tcploop`,
  `ping 127.0.0.1`), an inbound `tcpserve` + host `curl` via `hostfwd`, or a pcap
  dump (`qemu -object filter-dump,id=f0,netdev=n0,file=x.pcap`).
- **Nyx C language runtime DONE** (v5.8.0): Nyx C transpiles to C via `nyxc`, links against `nyxrt` (string/io/syscall runtime), and runs as `hello_nyx.elf` on NyxOS. Next: real `nyxc` compiler tool, more `.nyx` programs, self-hosted nyxc.
- SMP: multi-core bringup (INIT-SIPI-SIPI + trampoline → long mode → `ap_main`)
  works and is exposed via `cpus` **done** (v5.7.22); APs currently park in `cli;hlt`.
  Next (large): per-CPU scheduling — per-core run queues, a real lock (not just
  `preempt_disable`), reschedule IPIs, and TLB shootdown — to actually run threads
  on the APs.
- Page fault advanced features: demand paging + copy-on-write **done** (v5.7.23 —
  `vm_handle_fault` in the #PF path, PTE marker bits, `cowtest`). Still open: swapped
  pages (page-out to disk), and wiring demand/COW into the user-process path (lazy
  `sbrk` heap, COW `fork`) — today it's kernel-space, driven by the `cowtest` self-test.
- EXT2: mount-backed dirs (fd-based `readdir`/`mkdir`/`unlink` on `/mnt`), and
  flush-on-close instead of flush-on-every-write for large files. (Fd-based
  file read/write on `/mnt` now persists — v5.7.1.)

## Security notes (v5.4.0)
- Ring-3 syscall args are validated: `user_ptr_ok`/`user_str_ok` in `syscall.c`
  reject any pointer outside the canonical user half `[0x1000, 0x800000000000)`,
  so a user process can't name a kernel address as a syscall buffer.
- Userspace fds are opaque small integers (`UFD_BASE`+slot) mapped to internal
  VFS handles in `syscall.c`; kernel VFS pointers are never exposed to or
  accepted from ring 3.
- Still open: passwd salt is derived deterministically from the username
  (`gen_salt_hex` = HMAC(secret, username)) rather than random-per-user; the
  on-disk XOR of `/etc/passwd` is obfuscation, not encryption (the stored
  values are already PBKDF2 hashes). Fine for a hobby OS; note it's not a real
  secret-at-rest scheme.
